# Multi-agent parity: deviations from the build contract

## 2026-10-26 WP1 (shared contract)

- `sizing.learn` (A.4): its description never carried "only work you ask to be thorough gets subagents"; that phrase was in `sizing.auto`, which now reads as A.4 says. `sizing.learn` was left unchanged.
- `SubagentRecord.workflow` (4.6): the run variant's `progress` is typed `Record<string, unknown>`, because `WorkflowProgress.Progress` (core orchestration, WP29) does not exist yet. `SubagentLifecycle.read` checks it is an object. WP29 narrows the type and the check.
- Defaults for narrowing (4.2 "min(repository, user ?? default)"): added `ConfigV1.SubagentDefaults` (running 8, per_request 32, usd 25) and `ConfigV1.WorkflowDefaults` (max_agents 100, concurrency 16, steps 40 per agent, per_agent_steps 60, minutes 120, runs 1) in `packages/core/src/v1/config/config.ts`. WP5's `TaskLimits.DEFAULTS` and WP29/WP30's workflow constants should read these, so the narrowing and the runtime never disagree. A workflow key with no default (usd, tokens) keeps the repository's value when the user has none, which only narrows "no ceiling".
- `sizing.subagents` description: the contract's text says a repository "may only turn forks off and narrow models"; the 4.2 table also lets it shorten the user's `ask_timeout`, so the description says so.
- Narrowed `sizing.subagents.models` with no model in common with the user's list is kept as `[]` (no extra model allowed), the narrowest value, rather than dropped.
- `SubagentRecord.interruption.cause`: a cause a newer version adds reads as `"unknown"` instead of dropping the interruption, so an interrupted helper is never shown as plain cancelled.
- The ignored-spend notice (budget.run) now names every dropped key ("sets budget.run and budget.subagents.usd") and says "which does not apply from a repository" instead of "which no longer applies", since budget.subagents never applied from a repository. "--max-cost" is mentioned only when budget.run is among them.
- `projectExecutableConfig`, `repositorySubagents` and `ignoredSpendKeys` in `packages/engine/src/config/config.ts` are exported for `test/config/subagent-config.test.ts`.

## 2026-10-27 WP2 (one note pipeline)

- `NoteWindow.delay` (4.7) takes two optional inputs, `quiet` and `hold`, so `TaskNotify` can pass the `RuntimeFlags.noteQuietMs` / `noteHoldMs` overrides; without them it uses `QUIET` and `HOLD` as the contract says. The flags read `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_NOTE_QUIET_MS` / `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_NOTE_HOLD_MS` (positive integers; unset means the defaults).
- `TaskNotify` deps (4.7) also include `EventV2Bridge` (the `SessionStatus` service has no subscription method; the busy subscription reads `SessionStatus.Event.Status` from the bus) and `RuntimeFlags` (the overrides).
- `TaskNotify.watch` settles the child itself, exactly as `task.ts finish` did (record, budget scopes, guard release, memory hold, all under `SubagentLifecycle.controlLock`), then calls `onSettled(outcome, hold)` inside the same lock so the tool can lay the outcome over its part. The shared step is `TaskNotify.finishRun(sessions, guard, taskID, attempt, input)`, which `task.ts finish` also calls. WP3, which moves `finish` into `SubagentLifecycle`, should move `finishRun` with it.
- `TaskNotify.reports` (what a job's runs wrote) and `TaskNotify.CANCELLED_REPORT` (the old `BACKGROUND_CANCELLED`) moved with the note code; `task.ts` imports them for dependency hand-offs and the foreground cancelled result.
- "Delivering" ends once the note message has been handed to the parent (`ops.prompt` is forked into the instance scope), not when the parent's whole turn ends. Otherwise a note landing during a turn its own note started would wait for that turn to end instead of joining it, which today's 500 ms window does not do. Notes landing while a delivery waits out a revert or checks its notes are held and get a hold of their own afterwards (C.1).
- The parent's agent is no longer looked up to pass to `Truncate.output` for the 3 KB note cap: `Truncate.output` ignores that argument.
- `groups()` skips a user message whose every part carries `taskQuiet: true`: it is placed in no request, so it neither reopens a finished request nor counts as a message of one. The key is exported as `SessionTaskSize.QUIET`.
- `task.txt` rule 4 (WP9's file) said "Completion notifications are coalesced over 500 milliseconds", which WP2 makes false; it now reads "Completion notifications that land close together arrive in one message" (the A.4 wording), and `BACKGROUND_DESCRIPTION` drops its own 500 ms sentence so the rule is stated once. WP9 should keep rule 4's sentence rather than add it again.
- Permit leak found while running `test/tool/task.test.ts`: in effect 4.0.0-beta.83, `Semaphore.withPermits` takes the permit in an interruptible region, so a fiber interrupted at a scheduler yield just after taking it never releases it (seen as `metadataLock` held forever after a test's teardown, hanging every later test). Added `SubagentLifecycle.locked(lock)(effect)` (`uninterruptibleMask` around `withPermits`, the effect itself left as interruptible as the caller); `SubagentLifecycle.write` and every `TaskNotify` lock except the timer's (which must stay interruptible because `schedule` interrupts it while holding the lock) use it. Other `controlLock.withPermits` users in `task.ts` and `manager.ts` are unchanged.

## 2026-10-27 WP3 (lifecycle helpers in one place)

- `SubagentLifecycle.open` is named in WP3 but not defined anywhere in the contract. It is `open(record)`: the record's status is `queued` or `running` (the rule D.1's active board and D.3's legacy scan need). `manager.ts` keeps its two inline copies of that check; WP11/WP12, which own those paths, can switch to it.
- `INTERRUPTED_REASON`'s text is not given by the contract. It reads "Interrupted when Vector stopped (an update, restart or crash); partial work was kept." The D.3 orphan-part error is exported too, as `INTERRUPTED_ERROR = "Interrupted when Vector stopped"`.
- `claimNotification(sessions, childID, attempt, messageID)`: `attempt` is `number | undefined`, since legacy records carry none (the same rule as `write`). Because it claims only while `messageID` is unset (as WP3 says), `recover` no longer re-delivers a note whose claimed message does not exist. Before WP3 it did, which let a second recovery deliver a note again while the first claim's message was still being written; the price is that a note claimed by a prompt that then failed before writing its message is not delivered again.
- `SubagentLifecycle.finish(sessions, guard, taskID, attempt, input)` is WP2's `TaskNotify.finishRun`, moved as WP2's note asked; callers still hold `controlLock`. `task.ts` keeps a small per-call `finish` closure, because laying the outcome over that call's own part needs the call's state.
- `SubagentLifecycle.claims` is now one process-wide set instead of one per TaskTool instance. Claims are matched by parent session ID, which is unique, so the conflicts a launch sees are unchanged.
- Added `SubagentLifecycle.Claim` (the claim type) and `batchStatus(tasks)` (the batch status rule `runBatch` used, now shared with `patchEntry`).
- `patchEntry` and `settleOrphanPart` return `Effect<boolean>` (whether the part changed) and log their own failures. They write directly, without `patchPart`'s wait for a pending part, so they are for parts no live call writes. Besides the contract's running-to-error case, `settleOrphanPart` on an already settled part (a background launch) moves only that child's batch entry, or the single part's own status, from running to `cancelled` with `interrupted: true`, so its card stops showing it as running.

## 2026-10-27 Phase 1 review (WP1–WP3 fixes)

- Rejected: "settled() holds controlLock across a 10-minute patchPart poll". `onSettled` calls `persistPart`, which forks the part write in both paths (a batch through `deferred`, a single part through `forkIn(scope)`), so the lock covers only `finish`, the in-memory update and the fork. The only change is `WatchInput.onSettled`'s doc: it must not wait.
- TaskNotify's watch, timer, delivery and prompt fibers fork into the node's own layer scope. Only the windows map and its lock are instance state; the status subscription forks into the instance's cache-entry scope. A note held across a disposal is delivered from the old window, and a note enqueued after it opens a new window, so one parent can briefly have two.
- `claimNotification(sessions, childID, attempt, messageID, {stale?, note?})`: `stale` is the compare-and-set takeover; `note` is stored when the record has none, so a note enqueued without `result()` (tests, later note kinds) is still claimed. Every claim stores `notification.claimedAt`. `CLAIM_GRACE` is 60 s, and a claim without `claimedAt` (from an older engine) counts as old.
- `releaseNotifications(sessions, database, parentID, messageID, childIDs)` undoes a claim only while the parent message does not exist, so a prompt that wrote its message and then failed in its loop keeps the claim. It runs on any failure, interrupts included, for the same reason. `recover` still skips records with this process's `RUNTIME_ID`, so a note whose delivery failed is recovered after the next restart, as before WP2.
- `controlLock` is taken with `SubagentLifecycle.locked` everywhere (task.ts claim check, launch, extendRun, finish; manager.ts message, stop (both sections) and wait). The call-local part lock and the batch-local lock in task.ts are unchanged; a leak there would hang only that call.
- I19 for task routes follows 4.9: `isolation.directory` is stripped unless the principal is the owner or in control (`allowsSession`), for list, inspect, wait and stop. I19's wording ("guests never see") is stricter than 4.9 for a guest in control of its own session. WP28 or the owner should pick one rule; the handler helper is the one place to change.
- `SubagentRecord.isolation` is `Omit<Task.Isolation, "directory">` and `read()` drops a stored directory. `SubagentLifecycle.withoutDirectory` is exported for the routes. Spec 4.6 now says the part-metadata mirror carries no directory either.
- `patchPart` takes `object | ((stored) => object)` and holds `partLock` for each write. `batchEntry(metadata, index, patch)` is exported, and `patchEntry` uses it. After a batch call returns, its publish merges only its own entry.
- The ignored-spend notice stores one entry per file and key (`${repository}:${relative}#${key}`). A bare `${repository}:${relative}` entry from 1.99.111 counts as `budget.run` already noticed. The content-hash entries for folders without Git are unchanged.
- `sizing.auto` wording (finding on WP1): kept as WP1's file list requires. Spec 7.2 gains item 9, which blocks any publish from this branch before WP4–WP9 and WP30 enforce what WP1 documents.
- Removed the unused `WatchInput.callID`/`batchIndex`, `Note.hold`/`run` and `ResultInput.hold`/`format`. Spec 4.7 marks `hold` (WP16), `format` (WP19) and `run` (WP29) as landing in those WPs, and drops `callID`/`batchIndex` from `watch`, since no WP uses them.
- `docs/vector/design/subagents-v2.md` describes the per-parent window instead of the 500 ms window.

## 2026-10-27 WP4 (SpendGuard delegated ledger)

- `StopDetail.scope += "delegated"` (4.8): `StopDetail` had no `scope` field. Added `scope?: ScopeSnapshot["kind"]`; only `"delegated"` changes the wording. `afterStep(run, step, sessionID, scope?)` and `checkLimits` take it, and `apply()` passes `ledger.kind`.
- The contract gives exact text only for the delegated cap stop and the 80% note. The predicted, steps and unpriced stops got new delegated texts that name "the {cap} ceiling for this request's subagents" (or "this request's subagents used N of their M steps") and end "what it did is kept, and the main agent carries on." The loop text is unchanged: delegated ledgers run with loop rules off, so it never fires there.
- Re-arming (B.6 "stopped is cleared when spend is under the new ceiling") is precise per reason. A cap, steps or unpriced stop clears when the new budget no longer reaches it. A predicted stop clears only when the dollar ceiling rises, so the same budget, re-armed at every launch, does not reopen the ledger after each predicted stop. A loop stop is kept. Spend already past a lowered ceiling (or a seed past it after a restart) sets the stop at once, so B.2 check 3 (`delegated(ledger)?.stopped` after re-arm) refuses the launch without waiting for a step. `warned` is kept only while `usd` and `warnAt` are unchanged, so a new ceiling warns again at its own 80%.
- Lookup "by id across any lineage" uses a `delegations: id -> key` map in the service state, ordered by most recent link. `prune()` retains the last `LAST_RUNS` ids, plus an older one while a kept binding still counts on it, so a long-lived child never has its spend reset.
- A child linked with `delegated` while its root has no active run is bound with the delegated ledger as its run lineage, even when the root has a finished run (the old non-delegated rule binds to that finished run). So a resume after the root's run stopped at its cap is not stopped by it. Nested children take their parent's binding run, as before.
- Engine change outside WP4's file list: `ScopeSnapshot.kind` now includes `"delegated"`, which no longer fits `SubagentRecord.budgetScopes` (`Task.BudgetScope`, fixed kinds, I29). Added `SubagentLifecycle.scopeFields(scopes)`. It returns `budgetScopes` without the delegated scope, plus `delegatedScope` when one exists (the key is omitted otherwise, so an earlier value is not erased). `SubagentLifecycle.finish` and `TaskManager` stop write both. `TaskManager.list` shows only the filtered `budgetScopes`. This also implements B.6 "Records" for every place that persists scopes today. No engine test file was edited.

## 2026-10-27 WP5 (TaskLimits service, not yet wired)

- `seed(conversationID, requestID)` returns the spend seed `{spentUsd, steps, unpricedSteps}` instead of `void` (4.7), because B.6 has the task tool pass that seed to `guard.link({delegated: {seed}})`; it also loads the request's launch count. `admit`, `capacity` and `status` seed their key themselves before checking, so no caller has to call `seed` first. The database keeps no step counts, so `steps` seeds as 0: after a restart the request's subagent step count starts again, while spend and unpriced steps carry over.
- B.2 check 3 needs the ledger "re-armed with the current budget" before the child exists, and `link` needs a child. Added `SpendGuard.rearm(rootID, delegated)` in core (outside WP5's file list): it re-arms the delegated ledger as `link` does, or creates it at its seed, binds no session, and returns the snapshot. `admit` calls it with the conversation ID as root and the seeded spend, so the ledger exists from the first admission at the seeded amounts. The later `link` from WP6 only re-arms it, and its seed is ignored then.
- `admit`'s input gains an optional `parentID`, the launching session. A nested launch first borrows from slots its parent reserved: these are B.5's researchers using the lead's prepaid slots, which the 4.7 input could not tell apart from the pool. A `resume` whose `parentID` holds a slot (a subagent resuming its own child) is handled like a nested launch: it never waits, which avoids the B.3 deadlock.
- `status(conversationID, requestID?, limits?)` gains an optional `limits`. TaskLimits is a global node with no Config, so it shows the limits last passed to `admit`, `capacity` or `status` for that conversation. Before any of those it shows the defaults, or the guest limits for a guest-marked conversation; the FreeModelGuests dependency is there for that. WP7 should pass `resolve(config, guest)`.
- `resolve` takes `Pick<ConfigV1.Info, "budget">`, because the engine's `Config.Info` type is not exported. The engine config can be passed as it is.
- `admit` does not perform B.2 check 4 (workflow runs). Its input cannot tell a workflow-run launch from another root launch, and TaskLimits does not know which runs are running. `TaskLimits.workflowRuns(runID)` builds the exact `workflow_runs` refusal for WP31's own check.
- Workflow reserve (B.3): a workflow agent never takes a slot that would leave fewer than `min(WORKFLOW_RESERVE, running - 1)` free. Such an agent waits, which is the condition the contract names ("while any workflow launch is waiting"). Capping the reserve at `running - 1` keeps workflows runnable at `running` 1 or 2, where the literal rule would never grant a waiting agent its slot.
- The queue is strict. A head waiter that needs several slots (a research lead waiting for 1 + reserve) collects every freed slot until it fits, and nothing behind it overtakes it. Nested launches are refused while any root waiter is queued, which is B.3's rule that a free slot is promised to the queue.
- Releases follow the scope's exit. On any exit, admit's finalizer releases unbound places and reserved slots. On a failure exit it also releases the place and prepaid slots of bound children whose run never started (B.3 "a failure before start … releases it"). A nested admission's prepaid slot is also released at a successful close if its child never started: a subagent's launches always run in the foreground, so such a child never will (for example, after a failed dependency), and no slot leaks. A root launch with a reserve keeps its prepaid slots after a successful close, for a background child that starts later.
- `run` with origin `nested`, for a child that has no prepaid slot (WP7's `task_manage message` extension of a subagent's child), waits in the queue like a root launch. Nested launches never wait, because they are refused at admission, but a child launched earlier that runs again has no admission to refuse.
- `bind` finds the admission by identity (a WeakMap), so `Admission` stays exactly as in 4.7. Callers must pass the object that `admit` returned.
- Memory: idle conversations past 256, and requests past 64 per conversation, are dropped oldest first. A dropped one is counted again from the database when it is next used.
- `TaskLimits.node` is registered in `effect/app-runtime.ts` and `httpapi/server.ts` beside TaskNotify. Nothing calls the service yet.

## 2026-10-27 WP6 (admission and slots in the task tool)

- A user's slash command (`SessionPrompt.handleSubtask`) may wait for a slot on its card (B.4), but its call looks like the main agent's own foreground launch. Added the ctx.extra flag `SUBTASK = "vector.task.subtask"` (exported from `tool/task.ts`); `handleSubtask` sets it beside `delegation`. Only a call without it is moved to the background when its admission would wait.
- Origin `workflow` is not computed: `WorkflowMarkers` lands in WP30, which should set it in `task.ts prepare` from `ctx.extra[WorkflowMarkers.AGENT]`.
- requestID fallback (B.2, "latest real user message of the root"): the root's newest user message that `SessionTaskSize.real` accepts, or one with no parts yet (a message still being written, and the test fixtures), else the root's ID. Notes, judge findings and markers never open a request.
- `prompt.ts` delegation context (the request opener until WP8): `sized.carried.opened` (a compaction replaced the opener), else `sized.users[0]`, else the latest real user message in the window, else `lastUser`. It is computed once per step and passed to `SessionTools.resolve` and `handleSubtask`.
- Exempt launches (the judge) get no `conversationID`, `rootSessionID` or `requestID` in their record and are not linked to the delegated ledger. Otherwise the restart seed would count the judge as a launch of the request, and a ledger stopped at its ceiling would stop the judge at its first step. I4 speaks of "delegated" children; the judge is Vector's, not delegated.
- A resumed child's record takes the current request's `requestID` (the request whose ledger it now spends on, 4.6). TaskLimits.seed counts children by their stored requestID, so after a restart a resumed child is counted as a launch of the newer request, although a live resume is not counted (I2). It only makes a restarted count higher, never lower. WP14 (resume) or a later seed change can store the launching request separately if this matters.
- `TaskLimits.Refused` is mapped to `new Error(message)` in `task.ts`, as every other prepare refusal is an `Error`. The reason tag is not kept.
- The record starts `queued` with `slotReason` when the admission says the launch waits and it has no prerequisites. The run's `waiting` callback sets the same, and the run clears both when it starts (`transition({status: "running", slotReason: undefined})`). The part mirrors `slotReason` as `queuedReason` (4.6) and drops it once the run settles. A resumed child's record gets `slotReason` cleared or set explicitly, so a stale one does not survive.
- B.4 queued text: `{running}` is the conversation's running limit ("the most this conversation runs at once"); the admission's held count can be lower while reserved slots fill the rest. The summary is "Queued: waiting for a free subagent slot". It is shown only while the child is still queued when the result is built, so a slot that freed in between gives the normal started text.
- B.4 promotion calls `background.promote(info.id)` right after `background.start`, so the existing `onPromote` (detached part, record `background: true`, `TaskNotify.watch`) runs and the foreground race returns `backgroundResult()`.
- B.8: a single launch, a resume included, adds the busy line to its result (the old code skipped `task_id` resumes). Batch entries carry none; the batch adds one line after its entries from the largest `active` seen at admission.
- B.6 "At 80%" (`TaskNotify`, `notify.ts`, outside WP6's file list): `result()` adds "Subagents in this request have spent {spent} of their {cap} ceiling." to the note once the child's request ledger has spent its `warnAt` share (0.8 by default). It is added once per ledger and ceiling, so a raised ceiling warns again at its own level. `render` gained an optional `spend` line. Foreground results do not carry it (they are not notes).
- `TaskLimits.node` is now a dependency of `ToolRegistry.node` (the task tool needs it). `FreeModelGuests` was already one.
- Existing tests changed per 5.B.3/5.B.4/5.B.8: "launches a seventeenth background subagent..." became "a ninth background subagent is queued and says how many are running or waiting" (cap 8), and the foreground busy-line test expects the new B.8 wording. The test layer adds `TaskLimits.node`, `TaskManager.node` and `FreeModelGuests.node`.
- "A new layer, simulating a restart" builds `TaskLimits.node.implementation` again over the test's database and SpendGuard, and gives it to a new task tool.

## 2026-10-27 WP7 (limits on the board, research reservation, fair batch reports)

- `research.ts` needed no change. The lead's reserve comes from `ResearchRun.brief` (`research-run.ts`), which every lead launch passes through: the research tool and a direct `task` call with `subagent_type: "research"`. `Brief.ok` gains `reserve` (the depth's `researchers` cap: 2 Focused, 4 Deep by default), and `task.ts` passes it to `admit` only when the lead's researchers stay within depth 2 (a lead launched by the root). The reserve is 0 when the launching request is capped ("no subagents") or Vector is offline, since such a run starts no researcher.
- `TaskLimits.place` clamps a reserve to `running - 1`. A Deep lead's 1 + 4 slots in a conversation of fewer than 5 (guests get 4; a user may set 2) could never be granted and the lead would wait forever. Researchers past the clamp are ordinary nested launches.
- Added `TaskLimits.release(admission, childID)` (addition to the 4.7 interface) for WP5's known gap: a root launch's prepaid slots (only research leads have them) stayed held until restart when its job was stopped, or failed on a prerequisite, before its run took them. `task.ts` calls it when the job's first run exits; it frees only what that admission bound to a child that never started.
- `TaskLimits.SLOT_REASON` (moved from `task.ts`, which re-exports it), `TaskLimits.conversationOf(sessions, root, guest)` and `TaskLimits.currentRequest(sessions, rootID)` (moved from `task.ts`) are shared so `TaskManager.list` computes the same pool and request as a launch.
- B.10: the root's board uses its latest real user message as the request. A subagent's own board uses its record's `requestID`, the request its launches count in (B.2). `list` passes `TaskLimits.resolve(current config, guest)` to `status`, so the pool's limits follow the config. An internal `collect` (the old `list`) serves `stop`, so a stop does not read limits.
- I19: the task list route drops `limits` for any principal that is not the owner ("guests never see the owner's limit values"). Checked in `test/server/auth-guest.test.ts`.
- `TaskManager.message`: an extension run waiting for a slot marks the record `queued` with `slotReason`, and its start puts it back to `running` without the reason (B.3 "lifecycle shown"). Origin comes from the record: a workflow agent is `workflow`, a child below the root's child is `nested`, otherwise `root`. A record with no `conversationID` (legacy, or the exempt judge) takes no slot, as before.
- C.5: the envelope reserve is 256 bytes and 8 lines, not 6 lines. A rendered entry is 6 lines of tags, plus a summary line for background entries, plus the blank line between entries. `Truncate.NOTICE` (exported from `truncate.ts`, outside the file list; the notice text is unchanged) bounds what a cut adds. A cut report asks for that room in both bytes and lines, so one cut by bytes keeps all its lines and the reverse; `TaskReport.cuts` repeats the sharing until no other report is short. `task-report.ts` exports `cuts` and `size` beside `shares`. A cut entry saves the child's raw report; the output shows the neutralized text. Error entries share the budget too. A batch result always sets `metadata.truncated` (false when nothing was cut), so the tool wrapper never cuts the joined block.
- `prompt.ts`: a capped research lead loses both `task` and `task_manage` explicitly. Before, a capped lead whose offered tier was Thorough (a warm cache) kept both, because `withheld(thorough)` is empty.
- Tests outside WP7's list: `test/task/limits.test.ts` (release and the reserve clamp), `test/lib/research.ts` (TaskLimits.node in the research runtime group so a test can read the slots), `test/server/auth-guest.test.ts` (a guest's board has no limits).
- Generators: the SDK build and `packages/client` `bun run generate` produced no diff. WP1 had already added `Task.ListResult.limits` and `ExperimentalCapabilities.subagentLimits`.

## 2026-10-27 WP8 (sizing: offer rules and the request model)

- `prompt.ts` (outside WP8's file list) gains `withheldOnWork(tier, agents)`: the new `TaskSize.withheld` table plus today's "no task or task_manage below Thorough unless the user named a subagent". Both tool-filtering call sites (build and research lead) use it, so the tool set every request is sent with is unchanged until WP9 wires `offer` and flips `workDelegation` (the "behaviour behind a capability flag stays hidden" Done rule). WP9 should delete `withheldOnWork` and call `TaskSize.withheld(build.offered, agents, offer)` as A.1 says.
- The size-note texts (A.5) do change in WP8, since `note` is WP8's. Between WP8 and WP9 the Standard note says "delegate only an independent, substantial part or a broad search" while Standard still has no task tool. This build state is not publishable (release gate 7.2 item 9).
- `prompt.ts` delegation context now uses `sized.ledgerRequestID` (WP6's entry says "WP8 switches to ledgerRequestID"; WP9's file list repeats it, so that WP9 item is already done). For every request that is not a Chat message upgraded by a note, the value equals the old `carried.opened ?? users[0].id`.
- "Notes match at `id <= before`": `before` is not defined anywhere. Read as: every completion note in the request's messages up to the step being sized, the opener's own parts included, so a note `SubagentLifecycle.recover` prepends to the human opener counts. `size()` only ever sees messages up to the step, so no extra input was added.
- `notified` and the Chat rule use one predicate for a note: a `taskNotification` part whose state is not `cancelled`, without `taskNotification.quiet` and without the `taskQuiet` marker. The launching request is the one holding the last task call (`state.metadata.sessionId`, or `tasks[i].sessionId` of a batch) that launched or resumed the child before the note. A child the request launched itself counts at the request's own tier. A note whose launch is out of view (a compaction summarised it) counts as Standard.
- `ledgerRequestID` of a Chat group a note upgraded: the launching request's own `ledgerRequestID` (the highest-tier one, the latest of equals). The child's `SubagentRecord.requestID` cannot be read from the history, but it is the same value, because the launch's delegation context is that request's `ledgerRequestID`. With the launch out of view it is the previous non-Chat request's `ledgerRequestID`, else the opener.
- Chat with a note: the result's reasons become `["notified"]`, as `"escalated"` does; an escalated Chat request is checked first. The note entries of such a group take the group's tier and reasons, so the "thanks" carries the size the turn runs at. This is stable: a Chat group never runs on the build lane before the upgrade.
- Escalation is suppressed only by the opener's own classification. A compaction-carried request starts from the recorded size, which has never recorded a cap, so it is never suppressed (as before).
- Snapshot part metadata is `{ids, titles, at}`; 4.6 lists `{ids, at}`. `titles` (parallel to `ids`) was added so `Request.snapshot` can be the `{id, title}[]` that A.2 types. Exported for WP9: `SessionTaskSize.SNAPSHOT`, `snapshotOf(message)` (undefined when the message has no snapshot part, `[]` for a recorded empty one, so WP9 can tell "not taken" from "none running") and `snapshotPart(children, at)` (text `TaskSize.running(children, "work")`, or `""` when none; WP9 decides whether to save an empty one, for example as `ignored`).
- "A compaction-carried request reads the snapshot from `carried.opened`": the opener is not in the projected history, so `Carried` gains optional `snapshot`. `carry()` records the request's snapshot and `carriedOf` reads it back.
- `notes` entries are `{user: WithParts, result, judge}`. "Judge state" is compared as on or off. For a compaction-carried request, the first entry rides on the first user message still in view (the earliest joined message, else the compaction marker) and starts from the recorded size.
- `TaskSize.running` titles: whitespace collapsed to one line, `"` turned into `'` so a title cannot close its quotes, cut to 60 code points, then `neutralizeTags`. An empty title reads "untitled". More than 6 children end with " and N more".
- `packages/core/src/session/runner/llm.ts` is unchanged. The V2 runner has no subagent tools, so the new table changes nothing it sends (Light's extra `workflow` entry names a tool V2 does not have).
- Changed expectation in `packages/core/test/task-size.test.ts` (spec 5.A.1): "Work withholds only subagents, and Light also the to-do list" became the `withheld` block the WP8 entry lists.

## 2026-10-27 WP9 (prompt wiring, policy and tool text)

- Resumed from an attempt cut off before it committed (backup `.vector-build/wp9-partial-20261027-1737.patch`). Its edits were reviewed against 5.A and kept. This entry covers the result.
- `kimi.txt` is unchanged. A.4 asks it to add that the subagent's final report reaches only you, but policy line 1 ("works on its own and reports only to you; the user does not see its report") is in every policy variant that offers the task tool. The owner's rule is to state each rule once. `default.txt` and `trinity.txt` have no parallel-subagent example, so A.4 does not apply to them. Only `anthropic.txt` changed.
- To follow the same rule, `task.txt` drops what the A.3 policy now says:

- Byte sizes:
  - `task.txt`: 3469 → 3099.
  - `BACKGROUND_DESCRIPTION`: 580 → 352.
  - task description before the agent list: 4051 → 3453.
  - `escalate.txt`: 823 → 875 (spec text).
  - escalate Thorough result: 199 → 168.

- A.7: `TaskSize.Effort` (what `LLMRequestPrep` takes) is `off | default | high`. So a child's recorded effort is passed only when it is `default` or `high` (`childEffort` in prompt.ts), and `low` and `medium` pass nothing. Today nothing records an effort; WP18 widens this when per-call effort lands.
- A.2 "records under the conversation": the snapshot and the quick-lane line read the session's own children (`sessions.children`), the ones its task_manage can reach. They do not read the whole TaskLimits pool. A record counts when its `parentMessageID` sorts before the request's opener. It is outstanding when it is running or queued and live by TaskManager.observe's rule (this runtime, a job, or a busy status), or finished with an unclaimed notification.
- The snapshot part is saved only when the session launched at least one child before the request. A request with none saves nothing and sends no line. The part is `synthetic` and `ignored`, so `sizeNotes` renders it beside the first note and nothing sends it twice.
- `namedOnly`'s `resume` map is built only for a Light request whose note arrived (`notified`) and is not capped. It maps every child of the session to its recorded agent. A brief whose task_id the map does not know passes, and the task tool's own "not a subagent of this session" check refuses it.
- Out of WP9's file list, these texts said that only Thorough offers subagents, which 5.A.1 makes false. Each now reads "Standard and thorough may hand work to subagents; light and chat do not":
  - PromptInput `size` (prompt.ts);
  - `command.*.size` (core `v1/config/command.ts`; `config.json` regenerated);
  - the `vector run --size` help (`cli/cmd/run.ts`).

  The help snapshot (`test/cli/help/__snapshots__/help-snapshots.test.ts.snap`) was edited to match. The snapshot test was not run, because it spawns about 60 CLI processes. Instead, one `vector run --help` with COLUMNS=120 in an isolated home printed exactly the new snapshot lines.
- `test/session/task-size.test.ts`:
  - The fake provider now sets `concurrency: 64`. A local server otherwise gets one request line, so a held subagent request would queue the main agent's next request.
  - The note window is 50 ms quiet and 300 ms hold through RuntimeFlags.
  - "on a caching provider a Work follow-up … cannot launch a subagent" became a Light follow-up (5.A.1: Work may launch now).
  - The "no subagents" test also checks the capped refusal text.
- The SDK build and `packages/client` `bun run generate` both ran and produced no diff. `workDelegation` was in the contract since WP1, and the PromptInput `size` description is not in the OpenAPI document.
- Release notes (WP40a): after this upgrade, a conversation's next request writes the provider's prompt cache again. The subagent policy, the task tool's description and the tool set on Work requests all changed, and a request's size note now rides on its opening message instead of its last user message.
- Docs handoff (WP40a):
  - "When Vector delegates": On Work and Thorough requests the agent may hand parts of the job to subagents. It does so for independent, substantial parts that can run at the same time, broad searches whose raw output would flood its context, and new work you send while it is busy. Small, single-file or tightly coupled work, and anything that needs back-and-forth with you, it does itself. It puts every brief it knows in one background call, says in a line what it launched, and keeps working; each report arrives on its own. Light requests start no new subagents, Chat replies use no tools, and "no subagents" in a message stops new launches for that request while the agent can still check, steer or stop the helpers already running. "Be thorough" adds high effort for the agent and the subagents it starts.
  - "While subagents run": You can keep writing while helpers work. "Thanks" or "how's it going?" gets a short reply that names the helpers still working, without a tool call. A report that lands after it is handled by the full agent, with the tools of the request that launched the helper. A new request sent while helpers run lists them for the agent, which checks them before starting anything that would duplicate their work.

## 2026-10-27 Phase 2 review (WP4–WP9 fixes)

Every finding was checked against the code. Two pairs were duplicates (fork/isolation text; loop rules with no root run), and two more (the board's request after a join or a note; task_manage's description) were the same defect seen twice, so each pair got one fix.

- Research reservation (WP7, B.5). Confirmed: `place` clamps a lead's reserve to `running - 1`, but `ResearchRun` still allowed the depth's full researcher cap, so at running 4 a Deep lead's fourth researcher (or a Focused lead's second at running 2) was refused for want of a slot. `ResearchRun.researchers(depth, sessionID)` is now `min(depth cap, TaskLimits.resolve(config, guest of the root).running - 1)`. The lead's `reserve` and the researcher-count check both use it, so every researcher the run may have starts on a slot the lead holds. One part of the finding did not hold: the "has its N researchers" refusal also comes from `prepare`, so a batch of 4 at running 4 is still refused whole. It now names the real count ("has its 3 researchers"), and the lead's retry with 3 succeeds. Test: `research-tool.test.ts`, running 4 with a Deep plan.
- Unreleased brief fields (WP9, two findings). Confirmed: task.txt rules 3 and 7, SUBAGENT_INTRO, policy lines 6, 8 and 9, and the escalate texts advertised fork, model, effort, output_schema, isolation, merge/discard and the passing of effort down to subagents, none of which exists yet. The decoder dropped those keys silently. The text is cut until the WPs that add each feature: rule 7, the isolation clause in rule 3, "unless you fork it", the fork sentence of line 6, the list in line 8 (now "owned_paths, depends_on, success_criteria and limits"), "at your effort … model or effort on a brief" in line 9, and "and the subagents you launch" in escalate.txt and the Thorough result. Each of those WPs adds its sentence back:
  - WP18: per-brief model and effort, and Thorough's effort for subagents (escalate.txt, escalate.ts, line 9, and the app comment on `sizeDefault`);
  - WP19: output_schema;
  - WP20 and WP21: fork (the intro and line 6);
  - WP28: isolation, merge and discard (rule 3).

  The keys are also refused at decode instead of dropped: `UNRELEASED` in `task.ts` adds `Schema.optional(Schema.Never)` for the five fields to `InputParameters`, the decode schema only. The advertised JSON schema is unchanged and the parameter snapshot still matches. `formatValidationError` names the field ("isolation is not available in this version of the task tool; leave it out"). Each later WP replaces its `UNRELEASED` entry with the real field. This is narrower than the finding's `onExcessProperty: "error"`, which would also fail calls that carry any harmless extra key.
- Provider prompts (WP9, A.4). Confirmed: since Work requests carry the task tool, anthropic.txt (lines 50 and 51 and two examples), default.txt and trinity.txt steered every search that spans several areas to an explore subagent, and anthropic.txt repeated the specialists line. Both are policy lines 2 to 4. The lines are deleted, and the two examples now search with Grep and Glob. `SystemPrompt.provider`'s explore replacement is kept as a guard; no built-in prompt names explore now. Changed expectations (5.A.3 lines 2 and 3 and the owner's state-each-rule-once rule): `system.test.ts` "no provider prompt steers a search to explore…" (was "a provider prompt suggests explore only where the agent may launch it"); `prompt.test.ts` "loop leaves broad searches to the policy…" (was "loop suggests explore…"); and "loop names no explore agent when config turns explore off", which drops its check for the replacement text.
- The board's request (WP7 and WP8, B.10, two findings). Confirmed: `limitsOf` read the root's latest real user message, so after a message that joined the running request, or a "thanks" that a note made work, the board showed 0 launched and $0 spent while launches counted under the opener or the launching request. `TaskLimits` gains `track(rootID, requestID)` and `tracked(rootID)` (additions to the 4.7 interface; bounded to 256 roots), plus the helper `TaskLimits.requestFor(limits, sessions, rootID)`, which returns the tracked request, else `currentRequest`. `SessionPrompt` records its `delegationContext.requestID` at every step of a root, non-internal session, so the board reads exactly what launches use. SessionPrompt's node gains the `TaskLimits.node` dependency, which ToolRegistry already had. Before a root's first step in a process, and after a restart until its next step, the fallback is the latest message, as before. Tests: `task-manage.test.ts` "list keeps the running request's count after a message joins it"; `task-size.test.ts` (the I23 join test now also checks the tracked request is the opener).
- Parallel roots share the request (WP5 and WP6, B.1). Confirmed: each Swarm or Parallel Workspace root used its own opener, so N workers had N ledgers and N × per_request launches. `task.ts delegationOf`: for a root whose conversation is not its own, the request is `requestFor(conversation root)`, the conversation's current request. `prompt.ts` is not changed: its context only reaches `delegationOf`, which applies the rule in one place. `limitsOf` uses the same rule. Tests: `task.test.ts` "two parallel-agent roots under one parent share the running cap" also checks that all three children carry the parent's request and that it counts 3 launches; `task-manage.test.ts` "a parallel-agent root's board shows its conversation's request".
- Late slot wait of a foreground launch (WP5 and WP6, B.4). Confirmed: `enter` and `drain` ignore `demand`, so a launch admitted with a free slot could lose it during its ask, and the main agent then blocked. Fixed as the finding proposed: runTask's `waiting` callback promotes the job (`movable`: single foreground launch from the main agent, not a slash command, background subagents on). `backgroundResult` shows the queued text whenever the record is queued with `SLOT_REASON`, no longer only when the admission said it waits. The test is "a single foreground root launch whose free slot goes to another child during its ask moves to the background" (running 1). The finding's dependent child cannot become ready during the ask without its prerequisite holding the only slot at admission, which would make the launch wait from the start. The test therefore uses a background launch admitted during the ask, which reaches the same `enter` race. It fails without the fix.
- Loop rules with no root run (WP4, I4, two findings). Confirmed. When a child's launch run is the delegated ledger, its own child ledger is created with loop rules on. `apply()` passes a step's tool calls only to the stepping session's own child ledger, never to an ancestor's, so a loop stops that child alone. The shared ledger, and with it the siblings and later launches, carries on. Test: spend-guard "12. a child linked with no root run is paused by its own loop, and its sibling carries on".
- Predicted stop on re-arm (WP4, B.2 check 3). Confirmed. This follows 4.8 and supersedes the WP4 entry's per-reason rule for `predicted`: re-arming clears a predicted stop whenever spend is under the ceiling, since the estimate was the stopped child's own next step. A launch is therefore no longer refused with the false "reached their spend ceiling" text. A loop stop is still kept (delegated ledgers never take one). Changed expectation: spend-guard test 10's predicted section (spec 4.8).
- Raised running limit (WP5). Confirmed. `admit`, `capacity` and `status` apply the passed limits with `drain` (`limited`), so a raised `budget.subagents.running` starts the waiters it has room for, ahead of the new admission. Test: limits.test.ts "a raised running limit starts the waiters it now has room for, by status or by admit". Existing test changed: `task-manage.test.ts` "a message queued for a child whose run ended takes a subagent slot" set running 1 only through `status` and then read the board, which applies the config's 8. Under this fix that starts the waiter, as it should, so the test now sets `budget.subagents.running: 1` in its config. Its assertions are unchanged.
- Failed seed query (WP5). Confirmed. `query` returns undefined on failure. `withSeed` then keeps nothing and reports `seeded: false`. `admit` and `capacity` refuse with the new reason `unavailable`: "Vector could not read how many subagents this request has started. Try again, or do the work yourself. No child was started." (an addition to `Refused.reason`). `status` and `seed` answer with a transient empty count, and the next call reads the database again. Failing closed was chosen because `place` would otherwise store the pending entry at 0, and the delegated ledger would be created at no spend, so a later retry could not repair either. Test: limits.test.ts "a request whose count cannot be read…" (renames the session table for the duration).
- Batch report files (WP7, I13). Confirmed. A cut batch entry now saves the neutralized text, the same text it shows (the `saved` field is gone). This supersedes the WP7 entry's "a cut entry saves the child's raw report". Test: the 40/40/1 KiB batch test's alpha report ends with control tags past the cut, and its saved file holds them neutralized.
- "Don't be thorough" (WP8). Confirmed. `TaskSize.classify` gives NOT_THOROUGH its own reason, `TaskSize.CAPPED_THOROUGH = "capped:thorough"`. `SessionTaskSize.capped` (no new subagents: `withheld`, `namedOnly`, the note's capped clause, research's reserve) counts only "capped" and "capped:joined". The new `SessionTaskSize.held` (capped, or capped:thorough) blocks escalate: escalation suppression in `size()`, the escalate tool (`TaskSizeContext.held`), deleting escalate on a non-caching provider, and the note's escalate clause. `workflow.ts automatic()` treats "capped:thorough" as the user's word, as it treated "capped". Escalate's held-only refusal: "The user asked not to go thorough for this request. Carry on with the tools you have." Changed expectations (5.A.1: Standard may launch, and only "no subagents" caps it):
  - core task-size "the user's own negations…" (not-thorough moves to a new test that checks its own reason);
  - task-size-history "the user's no subagents or don't be thorough holds against escalate" (`held` for both; `capped` only for "no subagents");
  - task-size.test.ts "the user's no subagents or don't be thorough holds…" ("don't be thorough" keeps `task` and its note has no "no new subagents").
- Repeated rules and task_manage's description (WP9, three findings). Confirmed. The following are cut:

The judge-scope description in `general.tsx` reads "Large tasks are the ones Vector runs as Thorough…". Changed expectation: `memory-workflow-model.test.ts` (spec 5.A.1).
