## 2026-10-27 WP10 (durable job rows and liveness)

- `Store.promoted(id, holder)` takes the same `{owner, attempt}` guard as `settled`. 4.8 writes it as `promoted(id)`. The extra argument lets "a stale owner or attempt is a no-op" hold for every write.
- Store writes keep their order per job. `started`, `promoted` and `cancel`'s write are forked (detached), so a slow store never delays `start`, `promote` or `cancel`. `settle` awaits its write inside the run's exit, after waking the job's waiters, so closing a registry ends only once its jobs' rows say how they stopped. Each write first waits for the job's previous write. The 2 s timeout covers that wait plus the write.
- `candidates` works out the cause instead of storing it. A running row with a dead owner is `crash`. An interrupted row is `disposed` when its owner's process is still alive (this process, or a live pid in the same boot), and `shutdown` otherwise. `disposing()` stays a boolean, as 4.8 has it. A CLI disposes its instance before it exits, and the next engine then sees a dead process, so its rows read as `shutdown`.
- `prune(before)` keeps an interrupted row until it has been reported, so a repository left unopened for more than 7 days still gets its note. It also never deletes running rows. The caller passes `now - 7 days`.
- The machine id is created under a temporary name and linked into place. A damaged file is replaced by rename. When the state folder cannot be written, the process uses an id of its own, so other engines see it as `unknown` and never act on its work. `BackgroundJobStore.useMachineFile(file)` lets tests keep the file out of the user's state folder.
- Linux boot: `owner_boot` is an integer column, so the boot_id UUID is stored as its first 48 bits.
- `current()` is this process's `{pid, boot, machine}`. The contract names `current` without defining it.
- Instance disposal: the `disposing(directory)` marker is set inside `InstanceRegistry.disposeInstance`, the function `InstanceStore.disposeContext` (and `reload`) call as `runDisposers`. `instance-store.ts` itself is unchanged. The marker is counted per directory, because a reload and a dispose can overlap, and it is cleared once the disposers finish.
- Runtime shutdown is covered two ways:
  - `disposeRuntimes()` (the CLI's exit path) calls `InstanceRegistry.shutdown()` first, so every directory counts as disposing from then on.
  - A runtime torn down some other way, such as the desktop sidecar closing its server listener (which builds its own memo map), has no such call. For that case the engine's BackgroundJob layer adds a finalizer that runs before its instance cache closes. It marks every directory it holds as disposing until the cache has closed. The extra engine test "a running durable job's row is interrupted when the service itself shuts down" covers this.
  - For WP11: `InstanceRegistry.disposing(directory)` is true during both kinds of shutdown, but in the second only while the BackgroundJob layer itself is being torn down. Other layers torn down in parallel may run before or after that.
- Behaviour kept until WP11 (needed for the engine typecheck). A job stopped while disposing now settles as `interrupted`. The engine's job-status consumers read that as `cancelled`, as they did before this WP:
  - `TaskNotify.watch` (`finish` takes `FinalStatus`);
  - task.ts `joined` (`outcome`);
  - task.ts `dependencyFailure`;
  - task.ts foreground result;
  - `TaskManager.observe` (`stopped`).
  WP11 replaces these with its own handling (watch ignores it; joined and the foreground result skip `finish`). The existing notify test "disposing the instance neither drops a held note…" still passes, so its `cancelled` expectation is unchanged.
- Dependencies: the engine's `BackgroundJob.node` now depends on `BackgroundJobStore.node`, a global node with `Database.node` as its dependency. Every engine test that builds `BackgroundJob.node` therefore also builds an in-memory database, which most of them already did through `Session.node`.
- Not run: tool/task.test.ts and the other engine suites that build BackgroundJob (not in WP10's list). task/notify.test.ts was run once, alone (16 pass), because WP10 changes the status `TaskNotify.watch` reads.
- Generators: the core migration (`20261028021005_background_job`, with migration.gen.ts, schema.gen.ts and schema.json) was regenerated, and `script/migration.ts --check` passes. No route, HTTP schema or config changed, so the SDK, `packages/client` and `script/schema.ts` generators were not run.

## 2026-10-27 WP11 (interrupted handling and restart recovery)

- Recovered reports go through TaskNotify's window like every other note. `TaskNotify.Note` gains `stale?: string`, which 4.7 does not list. The delivery claims each result note with `claimNotification({stale})`, so it can take over a claim left by an engine that stopped before writing its message. Recovery checks each note as `recover` does (a dead owner, the grace period, the message never written, the launch still present) but does not claim it itself. Whichever of the delivery and a human prompt's `recover` claims first delivers the note, so it is delivered once.
- `TaskRecovery` depends on `SessionPrompt`, for the `TaskPromptOps` that `TaskNotify.enqueue` needs. Its node is registered in `app-runtime.ts` and in the server's `app` group. `InstanceHooks` is a global node. `register` holds a hook while the caller's scope is open; `init` runs every hook concurrently and logs failures. The boot hook forks the first pass into the recovery layer's scope and waits at most 10 s, so a slow pass is never cut short after it has claimed rows. The 5-minute loop is one fiber per instance, started by the boot hook.
- `reconcile()` (the public call) runs the full pass, including the legacy scan. The 5-minute loop runs a lighter pass: rows, plus report notes nothing has claimed. Stale claims and legacy records are checked only at boot.
- Note text: the helpers are listed one per line between the contract's two sentences, instead of inline as "{list}.". A depth-2 helper is indented under its launcher, and a helper whose launcher is not listed says "launched by <id>". Titles go through `TaskSize.titled`, which is now exported from `packages/core/src/task-size.ts` (outside the file list). The fixed text is 214 bytes, plus about 30 bytes and the title per helper. The note says "resume with task_manage resume", which does not exist until WP14.
- A legacy record's cause is `unknown`, because an older Vector cannot say why it stopped.
- `TaskManager.observe`'s abandoned mark is not counted as a report. Recovery also reports a record that observe marked (status `cancelled`, `interruption` without `reportedAt`). For a row this is natural; the legacy scan also includes such records. The test "an older Vector's helpers are reported once, including one the task board already marked" covers it.
- Liveness for records goes in `SubagentLifecycle.localLiveness` and `liveness` (subagent-lifecycle.ts, outside the file list). Local rules: this process's own runtime ID or open lease is alive; a closed lease or a legacy ID is dead. Another process's lease is judged from its job row, through `forParent` and `BackgroundJobStore.alive`; a lease with no row is dead.
  - `observe` marks a record abandoned only when its owner is `dead`. A live or `unknown` foreign engine's helpers are no longer marked (I27).
  - `recover` now skips `ownedHere` records: this process's runtime ID or any of its leases. Before, it skipped only `RUNTIME_ID`.
  - prompt.ts `outstanding` uses `localLiveness(record) === "alive"` instead of `runtimeID === RUNTIME_ID`.
- `observe` keeps its existing abandoned reason text, which the existing test checks, and adds `interruption {at, cause: "unknown"}`. It also fills `Task.Info.interrupted` (`at`, `cause`, `reported`, `resumedAt`, `dismissedAt`). No WP was assigned that field, and it is optional in the schema. The workflow-run cascade marks the run session's children whose role is `agent`, that are open, have no job here and have a dead owner.
- `TaskManager.stop` also skips its record writes while disposing. D.3 lists it, but WP11's file list names only `observe` in manager.ts.
- task.ts changes:
  - The per-call `finish` skips a `cancelled` finish while the directory is disposing.
  - The release path also skips `finish` when the job settled `interrupted`. This covers the WP10 note that the disposing marker can be released before a waiter runs.
  - A foreground call whose job settled `interrupted` returns a `cancelled`-state report with `INTERRUPTED_REASON` and does not call `finish`.
  - A batch entry interrupted while disposing writes neither its record nor its entry, so `settleOrphanPart` later marks the entry `interrupted`.
  - `background.start` passes `durable` with the instance directory. The record carries the lease as `runtimeID` and `owner {pid, boot}`.
- Recovery settles a record with `usage` and its spend scopes when this process still has them (an instance it disposed), then calls `guard.release`, as `finish` would. `interruption.at` is the row's `completedAt` for an interrupted row, and the time the pass ran for a crash. It is never later than an earlier observe mark.
- Reports are recovered only for helpers whose parent is a root that is not archived. A nested helper's report reaches its parent through `recover` when that parent resumes.
- Test edits outside WP11's list:
  - test/task/notify.test.ts: "disposing the instance neither drops a held note nor settles an interrupted child" now expects the interrupted child to be left `running`, with no note and no `onSettled` (D.3 "TaskNotify.watch ignores it", I26). The held note's delivery is unchanged.
  - test/tool/task.test.ts: its layer adds `BackgroundJobStore.node` so the new test can read rows.
- Known gap: a CLI process that exits within the note window (1.5 s) after its boot pass drops the queued note. The rows are already claimed, so the records show the interruption but the agent gets no note. A long `vector run` is not affected.

## 2026-10-27 WP12 (stop including helpers)

- `TaskManager.interrupt` follows D.1's order: `fence`, then `ops.cancel(root)`, then the sweep. It fences even with `helpers: false`, because D.1 lists the fence as step 1 unconditionally. A main-only stop through route 1 therefore also keeps a helper's later report from starting a turn until the user writes. `POST /session/:id/abort` is unchanged and does not fence.
- `aborted` is not defined by the contract. It is true when the session's status was not idle when the stop arrived.
- Excluded and skipped. Every desktop-bridged row (`DesktopTasks.owns`) is left running. That includes a Vector-runtime Parallel Workspace, which the desktop reports with `source: "subagent"`; the non-goals and I.2 ("workspace tasks excluded") leave every Parallel Workspace out. `excluded` counts the bridged active rows the desktop still serves. `skipped` counts bridged active rows with `connection: "disconnected"`, which Vector cannot reach. No stop command is sent to the desktop.
- Sweeps. There are at most 4: the first, then up to 3 repeats. Each sweep runs the shared stop path (`halt`) with `all` over the active board, restricted to native subagents, and the loop repeats only while a sweep stopped a task that no earlier sweep returned. A quiet board costs one sweep; a board with helpers costs two.
- `shells` is always 0. `ShellJobs` lands in WP33, which adds step 5.
- `stop` and `interrupt` share one internal `halt`. `stop`'s new `origin` defaults to "agent" when it is omitted, so the task_manage tool (not in WP12's list) needs no change. The HTTP `tasks/stop` route passes "user". `stop: {by, at}` is written in the settle step, and only when the record ended `cancelled` for that attempt. A child that completed before the stop reached it is not marked, because `needsHuman` would otherwise gate its continuation.
- Active board details:
  - Mirroring is computed from every desktop row, before the active filter. A native child that any desktop row mirrors stays hidden behind that row, so the sweep never stops a workspace's own session.
  - Abandoned records are pushed so that `observe` settles them. Rows that `observe` turns into `stopped` are then dropped from the active result.
  - Truncation: the walk stops once it has visited 2000 sessions with more left to walk, or when more than 200 active rows are found.
- Because `stop()` now reads the active board, `all` and the dependents/descendants cascade return only active tasks, plus any finished task named explicitly. Before, finished board rows came back unchanged. The cascade follows `parentSessionID` and `dependsOn` among active rows, plus the IDs named. Depth 2 keeps every descendant one hop from a selected task, so no finished intermediate is needed.
- Tests:
  - session-stop.test.ts covers the route with a mocked `TaskManager`: payload validation, the result, guest control through a test principal, and the capability flag. `interrupt` itself is tested in tool/task-manage.test.ts.
  - The auth-guest test gives the guest a claimed session (`VECTOR_SERVER_GUEST_SESSIONS`) as "its own". A session the guest creates is not in its claims, so control access refuses it, as for `tasks/stop` today.
- test/tool/task-manage.test.ts's layer adds `TaskNotify.node`, `DesktopTasks.node` and `Storage.node`, which the new tests read. The exerciser's capabilities scenario now also checks `sessionStop`.
- test/tool/desktop-tasks.test.ts (not in the list) was run once on its own (11 pass), because the active board changes what `stop` reads.
- Generators: the SDK build regenerated `packages/sdk` (route 1 and the `active` query). `packages/client` `bun run generate` produced no diff, because the experimental group is outside its contract.

## 2026-10-27 WP13 (move to background for singles and batches)

- Route 2 takes `payload: [HttpApiSchema.NoContent, TaskBackgroundInput]` (as `local.check` does) instead of the plain `{taskIDs?}` struct in 4.9. The generated SDK sends no body when no task is named, and a plain struct payload answered that request with 400. No body moves every promotable task; `{taskIDs: []}` and malformed bodies are still 400.
- `TaskManager.promote` returns `{tasks: []}` (not an error) for a session with a parent or while background subagents are off, which is what the old route did (false). A missing session is `not_found` on route 2; the old route maps any `TaskManager.Error` to false, so its contract (a boolean, false for a session it cannot reach) is unchanged. The result lists the tasks this call moved, observed after the move; a job without a subagent record is moved but not listed.
- `TaskManager` gains `RuntimeFlags` (node dep) for the flag check and for `controls.background`. `controls.background` is written only when true (omitted otherwise), so existing `Task.Info` payloads are unchanged. It is true for an active child whose job is running in the foreground, which is not stopping, and whose parent is a root session.
- `experimentalHandlers` no longer reads `BackgroundJob` (the old route's logic moved into `promote`).
- task.ts: `promotable` is no longer passed (the job default is true); every launch gets `onPromote`, made uninterruptible so a promoted entry is always watched even if the HTTP caller goes away mid-promote. A promoted entry renders as `<task_entry index="i" id="ses_…" state="running"/>` with `BACKGROUND_STARTED` once above the entries (its size is reserved in the 5.C.5 fair share). Exception: an entry promoted while still queued for a slot keeps its full text (the queued note), which says it waits for a slot. A batch launched in the background renders as before (each entry repeats `BACKGROUND_STARTED`); D.2 changes only promoted entries. That repetition is a possible prompt-size follow-up.
- Tests outside WP13's list:
  - test/server/session-stop.test.ts: its `TaskManager` stand-in gains `promote: () => Effect.succeed({tasks: []})`, because the old background route now calls it. Its assertions are unchanged.
  - test/tool/task-manage.test.ts was run once on its own (23 pass) because manager.ts changed.
- test/server/task-batch-promotion.test.ts: the old test "background endpoint refuses foreground-only batch jobs but still promotes a single task" is replaced (spec 5.D.2: batch entries are promotable). The file now runs the real `TaskManager` implementation (`node.implementation`) over a core job registry with real sessions; services promote never reads are stubbed.
- test/tool/task.test.ts "preflights all permissions…" no longer asserts that `jobs.promote` refuses a batch entry (5.D.2); it still checks the entries run in the foreground.
- Generators: the SDK build regenerated `packages/sdk` (route 2). `packages/client` `bun run generate` produced no diff (the experimental group is outside its contract).

## 2026-10-27 WP14 (resume)

- Briefs: the contract gives RESUME_BRIEF and RESUME_STOPPED_BRIEF only. RESUME_BRIEF says Vector stopped (an update, restart or crash), which is false for a failed child or one its parent stopped. Added SubagentLifecycle.RESUME_ENDED_BRIEF for those ("Your run ended before you finished, and you are asked to continue. ..."). Choice: stop.by "user" gives RESUME_STOPPED_BRIEF, else an interruption gives RESUME_BRIEF, else RESUME_ENDED_BRIEF. A completed child (user only, with text) gets no fixed brief, only the text envelope. The brief and envelope go in one synthetic text part, like TaskManager.message.
- All or nothing: every ID passes checks 1 to 11 and its admission (check 12) before any child starts; one refusal fails the whole call and starts nothing. So the engine never returns a partial ResumeResult; the desktop handling of a partial result (5.I.3) stays harmless.
- Check 3, unknown owner: refused for the agent; allowed for the user, because the route has no confirm field and 5.D.3 says the UI asks the user to confirm. A record this process ran (ownedHere) skips the liveness check: check 2 (its job is not running here) covers it.
- Check order and codes: check 1 refuses a caller that has a parent with `forbidden` and a grandchild with `invalid`. Running job, a live foreign owner and an open record give `running`; completed from the agent gives `finished` ("call task with its task_id and a new brief"); completed from the user without text, research agents, never started, dismissed and a reverted launch message give `invalid`; needsHuman for the agent gives `forbidden` with HUMAN_FIRST; owned-path overlap gives `conflict`; any TaskLimits refusal gives `limit`. TaskManager.Error and TaskApiError gained all three new codes (limit, conflict, running) now, as 4.9 lists.
- Check 11 uses the existing sibling claims (running task jobs of the root plus SubagentLifecycle.claims), because TaskClaims lands with the file-safety WPs. Checks and starts run under controlLock, so a task_id resume of the same child cannot race it.
- The resumed child's requestID is the root's current request (TaskLimits.requestFor), as task.ts resumes do, so its spend goes to the current request's delegated ledger. A guest (principal, or a guest-marked root) resolves the guest limits and budget.
- Guest training models: besides refuseTrainingFreeModels on the prompt ops (as taskMessage), the route refuses a guest at once (400 forbidden, FREE_MODEL_TRAINING_OWNER_ONLY) when a named child's recorded model is a free model that may train. The handler reads Provider through Effect.serviceOption at layer build: the server provides it to every handler, and the two handler tests that build experimentalHandlers alone (session-stop, task-batch-promotion) are unchanged. The auth-guest test is a handler-level test with mocked services, because a may-train free model needs a live OpenRouter catalog that a full Server.listen test cannot have.
- task.ts: besides the needsHuman gate in prepare (after lineage, before the ask), a task_id resume now also writes interruption.resumedAt and clears stop, waiting and reports, as TaskManager.resume does, so the board no longer shows the helper as interrupted.
- controls.resume (observe): true when background subagents are on, the task is a direct child of a root, finished (stopped, failed or completed), its job is not running, it is not stopping, it has messages (it started), it is not a research agent and not dismissed. Liveness, the launch message and needsHuman are left to the route, to keep the board cheap.
- SessionRunState.settleIdle writes stop {by: "user", at} after settling the record, with the attempt guard.
- The resumed note's parent agent is root.agent ?? "build", as TaskRecovery uses; the launching tool part is not patched on resume.
- New lifecycle exports: HUMAN_FIRST, agentMayResume(sessions, rootID, record) and written (was private). The manager lists the research agent names as literals rather than importing agent.ts, to avoid an import cycle.
- task_manage: the description gains one clause, "resume continues stopped, failed or interrupted children in the background;" (description 540 -> 616 bytes); the advertised schema gains "resume" and the task_ids/text notes (843 -> 884 bytes). The resume action takes task_ids 1..8 (4.4); the route takes 1..32.

## 2026-10-28 WP15 (waiting on you and ask_timeout)

- `Question.expire(requestID)` takes no `message`: a timed-out question's text is fixed (`Question.TIMED_OUT`, the D.4 sentence) and travels as `RejectedError({ timedOut: true }).message`. `Permission.expire(requestID, message)` is as 4.7 says; TaskAttention passes `Question.TIMED_OUT` as the feedback, so both kinds tell the helper the same thing.
- No "answered" attention note is sent. Section 2 says the main agent gets one quiet note per helper at most every 10 minutes, and D.4 names only the waiting and expired notes, so the 4.6 table's `answered` state is unused. This also keeps the root's history shorter.
- The 10-minute rule spans separate waits. The record's `notedAt` goes away with `waiting` when a wait ends, so TaskAttention also keeps a process-wide last-noted time per helper (pruned past 256 entries). The check and the write are one step under `metadataLock`, through the new `SubagentLifecycle.modify(sessions, id, change)` (subagent-lifecycle.ts, outside the file list).
- A helper with several pending requests: each has its own timer. `waiting` shows the earliest one still pending. When it is answered or declined, `waiting` moves to the helper's next pending request (keeping `notedAt`), or clears.
- `TaskSignals.publish` fires only when a helper starts waiting (it had no tracked request). Another request from a helper already waiting does not end a `wait`.
- `TaskSignals.publish` resolves only the waits registered before it: a fiber it resumes may start a new wait at once, and the same signal must not end that one (found by the attention test).
- `TaskManager.wait`: ended by attention, it returns `timedOut: false` and `attention: [ids]`. It races with `Effect.raceFirst`, so a desktop wait's error still fails the call as before.
- `TaskNotify.Note` gains `attention?: "permission" | "question"`, written as `taskAttention.kind` (4.6). notify.ts is outside the file list.
- Notes go to the root ancestor with no `launchID` and with the helper's attempt, so a note about an earlier attempt is dropped. An archived root gets no note.
- `observe`: status is `waiting_on_user` when one of the child's requests is pending in this instance, or when no local job runs it, it is not abandoned, and its open record says it waits (a child another live engine runs). `Task.Info.waiting` is filled from the record only in that status.
- Callers of a timed-out question: the question tool returns title "No answer" with `answers: []`; plan_exit returns "No answer" (untested: building it needs Provider, and it is not in the test list); a browser handoff returns `handoff: "cancelled"`; research_plan returns the dismissal metadata (`approved: false`, `answer: "cancel"`), because a timeout never approves a plan (R18). Each returns `Question.TIMED_OUT` as its output.
- Tests: attention.test.ts drives time with a TestClock given only to the watch (`InstanceHooks.init` run under it), so its timers and the notes it queues wait on that clock while the rest of the test runs on the real one. "The child continues" is checked at the request level: the declined request fails with `CorrectedError`, which `failToolCall` never treats as blocking; the helper's other request stays pending; and its record and job keep running. No model loop runs. In the two-instance test, each instance's helper gets one waiting note and one expired note at its own root, and each request is declined by the instance that holds it. Besides the directory filter, only the owning instance passes the pending-request and job checks.
- The "research-plan and browser callers" line is covered in test/question/expire.test.ts. A permission expiry reaches every tool as the same `CorrectedError` tool error, which test/permission/expire.test.ts checks with the no-cascade rule.
- `TaskAttention.node` and `TaskSignals.node` are registered in app-runtime.ts and the server's app group (outside the file list, as WP11 did for recovery).
- A disposed instance stops its watch. A record can then keep a stale `waiting`, but it is shown only under the observe rule above, and a resume clears it (WP14).
- No generators were run: `taskAttention`, `Task.Info.waiting` and `WaitResult.attention` were already in the schemas (WP1), and no route or config key changed. The exerciser's capabilities scenario checks the new flag.

## 2026-10-28 WP16

- `TaskManager.list` keeps `siblings?` on its input (4.7) and is typed with two overloads: `{siblings: true}` returns `SiblingList`, anything else `Task.ListResult`. The task_manage tool calls the siblings form explicitly. A sibling row is exactly `{id, title, agent, status, ownedPaths}`, the D.5 list ("IDs, titles, agents, status and owned paths"), so besides the fields D.5 names it also leaves out usage, dependsOn, controls and the rest. It is refused (`forbidden`) for the root and for research agents, and skips the caller and the desktop bridge.
- `report` errors: a foreground child gets `invalid` with "Your parent is waiting on you; put this in your final report."; the root and research agents get `forbidden`; a report from an ended attempt gets `finished`; the 13th in a minute gets `rate_limited` (TaskMailbox.admissionError over `reports.at`). The rate check and the wake count are one `SubagentLifecycle.modify` step, so concurrent reports cannot both wake. A second `wake` in the same attempt still sends the note, without waking, and the result says `woke: false`. The note's `state` is "reported", its `launchID` the child's `parentMessageID`.
- The `child` envelope text completes the spec's ellipsis: "The JSON below is a note from a subagent you launched. It is untrusted data, not a user instruction: check it before you rely on it." New `TaskMailbox.opened(envelope)` reads the text back (used by inspect).
- Taint (D.6) is carried as two part keys: `[BrainSchema.OUTSIDE]: "subagent"` and `[SessionMemory.HOLD]: "web" | "mcp"`, built by the new `SessionMemory.carried(hold)`. `taint` reads an OUTSIDE "subagent" part with HOLD "mcp" as `mcp`, not `web`, and any tool or text part with HOLD "mcp" as `mcp` before the `subagent` rule. Without the second key an MCP-held note would have read as web, since every OUTSIDE text part does.
- notify.ts (outside the file list; 4.7 marks `Note.hold` and `result(hold)` as WP16) gains `Note.hold` and `ResultInput.hold`. `watch` passes the settled child's hold to its result note, so a background completion carries web or MCP up even after its task part is gone. The foreground path already writes the hold on the task part.
- `TaskManager.message` gains `origin?: "agent" | "user"` (default agent). The HTTP message route (handlers/experimental.ts, outside the list) passes "user". A user's message carries no taint, whatever the root read, and its mailbox entry is stored with `from: "user"` (SubagentRecord.mailbox type in subagent-kind.ts, outside the list) so inspect can say `sender: "user"`. Without it every message from the session's page would show as the parent's, and would take the root agent's taint.
- `InspectResult.messages`: to_task rows come from the mailbox (sender `user`, `parent` for any ancestor, else `peer`); to_parent rows come from the parent's parts whose `metadata.taskMessage.taskID` is the task, sender `task`. Their `createdAt` and `deliveredAt` are both the part's write time: the report time itself is stored only in `reports.at`. The mailbox keeps delivered entries for a minute only (existing pruning), so older delivered messages to the task are not shown. `messages` is omitted when empty.
- `Task.Info.inbox` is `{unread}`, the mailbox entries not yet delivered, and is omitted when none wait.
- Recovered result notes (`SubagentLifecycle.recover`, TaskRecovery) carry no hold: the stored notification has none. Left as is.
- Changed expectations outside WP16's list: test/tool/task-manage-schema.test.ts (4.4: the advertised action list gains "report", plus `siblings` and `wake`, and report inputs are validated); test/session/research-taint.test.ts (5.D.6: a task call whose subagent ran an MCP tool now holds its parent as `mcp`, no longer `subagent`).
- task_manage description rewritten to state each action once: 616 to 556 bytes. Its JSON schema grows 884 to 1076 bytes (report action, siblings, wake). No generators were run: no route, HTTP schema or config key changed (Task.Message, ReportResult, inbox and InspectResult.messages were already in the schemas from WP1).

## 2026-10-28 WP22 (per-file lock and read-before-write) — entry written 2026-10-30 in the Phase 5 review

- No entry was written when WP22 was committed (`e92862a2`), and no run of its new test files was recorded. This entry is written from that commit and the runs below. It records no contract correction: the Phase 5 findings named none in WP22's files.
- What changed for users. edit, write and apply_patch refuse to change a file that the conversation has not read. A partial read or an @-attachment counts as a read. They also refuse a file that changed since it was read, naming the subagent that changed it or saying "outside your edits (an editor, a formatter or a shell command)", and a file another conversation deleted after the read. Re-creating a file the conversation deleted itself is allowed. apply_patch Add File onto an existing path is refused ("<file> exists; use *** Update File"), as is an edit with an empty `oldString` onto an existing file. Move targets are checked and are in the ask patterns, so a move onto a memory file asks `memory_source`. On Windows, a write refused because another program holds the file is retried at 50 to 800 ms and then fails with nothing changed.
- Release notes (WP40a): Vector's edit, write and apply_patch tools no longer change a file the conversation has not read, or one that changed since it was read (by another subagent or outside Vector). They say why, and the agent reads the file again before retrying. apply_patch's "Add File" no longer overwrites an existing file; "Update File" is required. On Windows, a file another program holds is retried for about a second and a half before the write fails with nothing changed.
- Evidence (2026-10-30, Phase 5 review round 1). The tree was HEAD `c4c2f761` plus that round's uncommitted fixes, none of which change WP22's files. Files ran one at a time through the lock wrapper, with a TMPDIR outside `/tmp` (see that round's entry below).
  - file-lock 8/8, with the darwin probe case skipped on Linux; file-state 11/11; edit 35/35; apply_patch 32/32.
  - prompt-guard 39/39; spec-guard 6/6; task-size 51/51.
  - prompt.test.ts: WP22's two changed cases ("an edit and its check in one response…", both orders) passed 2/2, selected by name. The file's other cases include shells that start Bun and were not run here.
  - write 16/17 under `umask 022`, as WP23 ran it. The failing case, "throws error when OS denies write access", sets a file to mode 0444 and expects the write to fail. This shell runs as uid 0, which mode 0444 does not stop (a plain shell write to such a file succeeds here), so the run is no evidence either way for that case. WP23 recorded 17/17.
  - memory-sources 8/10. The two failures are the same on the unmodified HEAD source and are standing; see the Phase 5 entry.
- These runs are on the Phase 5 tree, not on the WP22 commit. The earlier per-file results for edit (35), write (17) and apply_patch (31 plus one real-session memory-source case) are WP23's, on the WP23 source.

## 2026-10-28 WP23 (claims and the write chokepoint)

- This continuation uses the committed build contract. The handoff's newer Mac-only `.vector-build` contract and deviation notes were not available in the cloud workspace; they have not been represented as recovered.
- `TaskClaims` keeps a bounded, process-local trusted assignment cache (10,000 child sessions) after a claim settles. A settled child can still delegate a nested follow-up within its original assignment, as the existing task-tool regression requires, without trusting mutable session metadata. Settling removes active exclusion immediately; disposing the directory forgets the cached assignment too. Only bound claims affect a running writer's assignment, so a provisional resume cannot broaden its permission before admission completes. A missing trusted assignment on a child fails closed and requires an explicit parent resume; cache eviction or disposal can never turn a scoped child into an unrestricted writer.
- Claims live through the background job's full lifetime, including queued `task_manage message` deliveries and task-tool extensions. They are released by a job waiter rather than by the first run's finalizer, because a run can finish while the job still has queued work. Extensions retain the earlier assignment and implicit changed-file claims.
- Admission refuses explicit owned paths that overlap another running unscoped child's already changed files, as well as its explicit paths. Otherwise both children could be admitted and then block each other at write time.
- `Tool.Context.assertWritable` is optional for existing tool/plugin callers and standalone fixtures. `SessionTools.resolve` always supplies it in production; edit, write and apply_patch repeat it inside their locks. Tests exercise the actual runtime permission wrapper and an actual equal-content write race, so stale-file checks cannot mask a missing ownership hook.
- The protected-directory registration API is present for WP27. Isolation directory/branch registration and git mutation enforcement are still owned by WP24–WP28; this work does not claim those isolation surfaces are wired.
- The WP22 memory-source integration fixture created a child directly, bypassing task admission. It now binds an explicit unrestricted trusted claim before running the real session. Production still refuses a child whose trusted assignment is missing; the fixture does not widen that guard.
- Validation: `bun typecheck` passed in `packages/engine`. Individual-file checks passed for claims (9, plus one platform skip), owned paths (6), actual write ownership (1), session-tool permission plumbing (2), task (134), edit (35), write (17), and apply_patch (31 standalone cases plus the real-session memory-source case). The actual two-child TaskManager resume case also passed. After the final dependency-edge cleanup, the late-extension fallback and concurrent dependency-cycle cases were rerun and passed. The existing write permission fixture expects mode 0644, so that file was run with `umask 022` instead of the cloud shell's inherited 077; no production permission behavior changed.
- Independent review closed the early-agent lookup, implicit-claim admission overlap, pre-start cancellation cleanup, stale assignment fail-open, extension ownership transfer, late-extension fallback, and dependency-edge release findings. WP24–WP28 and the complete Phase 5 review remain pending; this commit is WP23 only.

## 2026-10-28 WP24 (shell writes and git rules)

- The existing edit permission patterns retain their spelling and membership. Ownership uses separate resolved `written`, `mutates` and `git` scan fields, and omits unresolved file targets. The shell checks ownership before any permission prompt and again after permissions return. The final check and reservation take the same short file locks as edit/write/patch, then reserve resolved targets for an unscoped child before spawning. Possible writes, failed commands and partial writes keep that conservative ownership until the child settles; no target is claimed before permission is granted, and unresolved targets and read-only/index-only git operations are not claimed. Directory mutations also protect descendants.
- Git metadata identity and checkout placement are kept separate. Read-only `rev-parse --show-toplevel` checks confirmed that `--git-dir` can select another worktree's HEAD/index while files still resolve from the command cwd; `--work-tree` overrides the file placement. `GitMutation.Result` therefore carries the metadata tree, actual working tree/directory, and explicit git-directory/index selectors. Guards check both known roots and protected metadata selectors. An external temporary index or git directory cannot hide a detected change to files in a known working tree.
- `TaskClaims` accepts an authoritative registered git-directory mapping for linked worktrees, and the live shell scanner consumes it. WP27 must supply that value only from its validated isolation registry. Registration also protects that metadata directory from unrelated writers.
- Pathspec magic and pathspec files are conservatively whole-tree changes instead of invented literal filenames. Broad or unresolved ref targets are refused because they could alter the reserved `vector/subagent/*` namespace; callers can use explicit unrelated ref names. Report-only `git apply` flags do not suppress writes when `--apply` re-enables them. Mutating stash operations keep a separate marker so isolated helpers cannot use `stash pop` or `stash apply` merely because their table effects are tree/head.
- A directly isolated helper retains commit/merge/rebase authority in its own worktree. Whole-tree operations still wait for any of its own shared descendants that are editing there, preserving I17. Shared descendants do not inherit the isolated parent's permission to change HEAD/index.
- Shell detection remains best effort. Interpreters, scripts, `bash -c` strings, the V2 bash tool and filesystem MCP servers remain outside this scan; this change does not make shell execution a filesystem sandbox.
- Validation: engine `bun typecheck` passed. Individual-file runs covered 118 classifier cases, all 32 shell-ownership cases (with a targeted rerun after correcting one test's expected protected-selector message), 62 existing shell cases, four shell-write permission cases, nine claims cases plus the platform-specific case-folding skip, and six existing owned-path cases. Tests include actual shell reservation races, retention after shell writes/removals, rechecking after a permission prompt, linked metadata/current checkout placement, readonly protected Git inspection, explicit metadata/index selectors into another child's scope, pathspec and ref ambiguity, aliases, negations, and unchanged edit asks.
- Independent static review closed broad/dynamic refs, report-mode overrides, pathspec magic, attached branch flags, flag negation, stash handling, metadata/working-tree routing, readonly inspection, and implicit shell ownership findings. WP25–WP28 and the complete Phase 5 review remain pending.

## 2026-10-28 WP25 (isolation building blocks)

- Copy, hash, file-list and checkpoint-backup primitives now live in core; the desktop imports them and retains the checkpoint re-export. Copy admission counts all copied entries/bytes, including files above the existing five-MiB diff/hash cutoff, before writing a stable source tree. Copy checks limits again while copying if the source changes. Escaping and broken source links are omitted; safe internal links are rebased into the destination instead of pointing back at the source. Destination parent links and existing hard links are refused. These checks are filesystem boundaries, not a sandbox against unrelated concurrent processes changing paths. Exclusions now apply relative to the selected project root, so an ancestor named `build` or `.cache` no longer silently prevents a project copy. Repeated checkpoint backups replace only a final backup symlink without following it; parent links and hard links still fail closed.
- Trust inheritance compares captured bytes, modes and execution-directory identities with workspace-internal paths rebased to relative identities. External dependencies remain tied to their absolute identity. It grants the child's original path-bound fingerprint only while the parent's matching approval still exists under the same approval lock. Config retains its original capture inputs (including TUI settings and `.vector` inputs) rather than deriving them from the smaller loaded-file list; pure mode and an untrusted parent cannot inherit approval. Forget removes only the workspace approval, never a cache path assembled from persisted data. The existing unavailable-snapshot guards now also refuse an empty error message; truthiness could otherwise admit an unavailable capture.
- `Worktree.createForTask` is an internal raw checkout primitive at the reserved project/child location and branch. Every creation Git call disables fsmonitor, forces LFS skip-smudge over caller env, and enables long paths on Windows. It does not load an instance, register a sandbox, emit readiness or run startup scripts. Failed population deliberately leaves the created checkout/branch available for WP27's registry-driven recovery; this primitive does not delete a directory or branch using caller-supplied metadata. Registry creation, task isolation lifecycle and runtime wiring remain WP27/WP28 work.
- Normal worktree listing hides a task checkout only when its direct task-root placement, registry directory/branch and actual Git branch all agree. A name or reserved-looking branch alone does not hide a worktree; malformed, missing or stale registry records remain visible.
- Validation: individual-file checks passed for new core copy (27), core trust inheritance (10), engine Config inheritance (6), and raw task worktrees (5); existing core workspace trust (23), desktop merge (12) and follow-up (45), engine worktree lifecycle (14) and removal (1, plus one Windows-only skip) also passed. Core, engine and desktop package typechecks passed. The raw-worktree fixture was corrected to create its registry parent before writing and to remove its empty test directory recursively before testing a link; its full file then passed. Windows-specific long-path behavior is asserted conditionally and awaits Windows CI.
- Independent static review closed the repeated-symlink checkpoint-backup regression and cleared the trust/copy/raw-worktree boundaries. WP26–WP28 and the complete Phase 5 review remain pending; these internal building blocks do not yet expose task isolation to callers. No public HTTP schema changed, so no client generation was required.

## 2026-10-28 WP26 (routing helpers across instances)

- `TaskPlace` keeps trusted execution/home contexts and parent lineage in process memory, with no silent eviction and no recovery from session metadata. Creating a descendant records inheritance before its first Created event and keeps its row at home. Reusing an existing session recovers its ancestor links from persisted `SessionTable.parent_id` rows before selecting execution context; an existing explicit isolation assignment is preserved. Registering isolated work also records unplaced ancestors at home so a root callback cannot accidentally run in a child's workspace. Task-directory classification reads the reserved registry location and matching directory/branch fields; names and live placement alone grant no isolation authority.
- `SessionPrompt` routes prompt/cancel and the direct loop, shell, command and free-model-resume entry points. `SessionRunState` routes its own public operations too, covering direct HTTP message deletion and revert busy checks. Cancellation stops runners at work, then rejects home-hosted permission and question requests. Permission rejection retains its existing same-session cascade and does not reject another session's asks.
- The additional `TaskJobs` helper keeps ID-based manager job operations in the launcher's registry and enumerates only trusted work contexts sharing the current home. Stop-all's fallback and session deletion can therefore reach nested jobs without scanning unrelated projects or other homes. Task-tool execution stays in its launching session's context. Notification windows, result delivery and the full job watcher run in the parent's context; attention watches remain home-hosted and route their job check to the launcher. These changes extend the WP's file list to `session/run-state.ts`, `task/jobs.ts`, `task/notify.ts` and `task/attention.ts` to close callers found by the required audit.
- Session events use an explicit family inventory (SessionV1, status, permission, question and Todo); file/watch/LSP/Vcs events retain work location. An explicit event location remains authoritative. Public metadata replacement preserves the latest raw `metadata.subagent` value under the same lock as lifecycle updates, including malformed legacy values; retaining an earlier HTTP read would lose a concurrent lifecycle update. Deletion runs lightweight cleanup hooks while each row still exists, then drops placement after its deletion event, and does no Git work itself.
- Required call-site audit: `statuses.get` in manager observe/message/interrupt, notify schedule and task-tool extension, plus `status.get` in prompt/outstanding, are centrally home-routed by `SessionStatus.get`; status writes in prompt, processor and run-state use its home-routed `set`. Status lists in session/instance HTTP handlers and global lifecycle remain home-local; a caller executing at work resolves its unique trusted home. The similarly named Maps in GitHub review and snapshot code are unrelated.
- Required call-site audit: manager observe explicitly lists permissions/questions at the child's home; prompt cancellation explicitly lists and replies there. Attention's list/expiry calls already execute in its home watcher, which rejects events with another directory. Permission/question HTTP handlers and CLI SDK callers retain their existing home endpoint context. Run-state uses in prompt (cancel/loop/free-model resume/shell), revert busy checks and HTTP message deletion are covered by the public `SessionRunState` routing wrapper. TaskManager get/start/extend/cancel/wait/promote use the routed job facade; its lease is explicitly read in the target task's launcher context. Notify's sibling list/full completion watcher, attention's job check and prompt outstanding-job checks are likewise launcher-routed.
- WP27 must close the active-descendant settle boundary before Phase 5 approval: an isolated parent's shared descendants must not keep writing while its tree is committed, disposed or removed. Dropping the parent's work mapping while one of those jobs remains in its old launcher registry would also break later control. A real active-grandchild regression is required with that lifecycle implementation.
- Retiring work now retargets inactive shared descendants at home and keeps their lineage. Explicit isolation assignments are distinguished from inherited ones, so a later validated parent resume refreshes inherited descendants without overwriting another explicit assignment. The real Session fixture covers retire, parent rebind and grandchild lookup.
- WP27/WP28 execution admission must also handle a direct prompt to a shared descendant after its isolated parent retires work: restore validated ancestor work or refuse before spend, rather than silently execute at home. I19 review must cover structured child message paths (`Assistant.path.cwd/root`) and their events, not only `Task.Info.isolation.directory`, before guest isolation can be exposed.
- Validation: the five new files cover 22 passing cases. Existing individual files cover run-state (5), task tool (134 across an initial 132 passes and two targeted passes), manager (44), notification (16), attention (5), Session (17), permission (87), question (14) and HTTP sessions (26). The original task-tool full-file run had a notification-claim race and a two-second losing-owner timeout. Canonical `0d2e3dc1` reproduced the notification race; the fixture now waits for the independent notification claim before comparing records. The losing-owner case passed on canonical and targeted current-source runs; its deadline and production code were left unchanged. This is not a claim that the complete 134-case file ran green after those targeted checks.
- The run-state fixture's two stale one-update assertions also failed identically on canonical `0d2e3dc1`. They now assert the two existing writes explicitly: cancellation outcome, followed by `stop.by: "user"`. No production timing or lifecycle write behavior changed for those fixture corrections.
- Final engine package typecheck passed after the fixture changes. Independent static review closed existing-session recovery, full notification routing and descendant retirement/rebinding. WP27/WP28 and the complete Phase 5 review remain pending; no public schema changed or client generation was required.

## 2026-10-29 WP27 (isolation lifecycle)

- The internal isolation service owns a private registry and derives reserved paths from project/session IDs. Destructive operations reread that registry and match Git's actual reserved checkout and branch. A bound checkout additionally verifies its Git directory, common directory, working root and index destination; replacing `.git`, redirecting `core.worktree`, or changing branches cannot redirect Vector's internal writes. Interrupted creation can bind previously unsaved Git metadata only through the parent's common directory, its direct `worktrees` child, and Git's exact checkout backpointer. Session metadata grants no filesystem authority.
- Start remains `preparing` through raw checkout/copy creation, trust inheritance and agent comparison. A scoped acquisition owns the loaded instance until durable `running` state and placement transfer succeed. Errors and interruptions release that acquisition, including failure of the final record write. Worktree creation remains an internal primitive without start scripts or sandbox registration. Every isolation Git call disables fsmonitor/hooks, skips LFS smudging and enables long paths. Internal commits use `core.hooksPath=os.devNull` and signing disabled; the redundant `--no-verify` flag was removed at the coordinating agent's request. Repository pre-push checks remain mandatory.
- Temporary indexes preserve the parent's index while capturing staged, unstaged and untracked files and stacking saved dependencies. Admission accounts for complete copy size, including files omitted by the desktop's default five-MiB hash limit; optional hashing limits leave existing desktop defaults intact. Ignored links are validated inside the launching checkout, excluded from settlement and unlinked without following replaced ancestors.
- Saving and retirement stop and join every shared descendant before committing, disposing or removing work. This required a core background-job correction: terminal status is not a completion barrier while a cancellation's finalizers are still running. Per-run drain accounting now holds duplicate cancellation, waiting and same-ID reuse until those finalizers finish; closing registries refuse new execution. Bounded durable-store writes stay asynchronous during normal work and are joined at registry disposal, preserving their existing nonblocking behavior. A stale attempt cannot settle a newer resume.
- A logical review base is separate from the provenance seed. Partial merges advance only selected paths, and resumed work can subsequently edit or delete those paths. Copy mode keeps immutable private baseline versions. An explicit owner's `includeStacked` includes actual dependency data in Git and copy modes; preserving that logical base across resumes does not bypass the owner-only option or the refusal of unmerged dependencies for agents.
- Merges compute off-tree, retain the real index, validate targets and permissions again inside shared file locks, and write a private manifest and pending record before the first parent write. Git's older merge fallback uses a disposable index and checks conflict output as well as exit status. Interrupted merges restore their checkpoint; a completed merge/discard retains its recovery artifact before cleanup. Git artifacts are binary patches; copy artifacts are a versioned JSON-lines bundle containing exact file bytes as base64, so binary copy results remain recoverable. Terminal inspection reads the saved artifact with the same patch-size bound.
- Failed or subsequently modified results without a genuine saved snapshot retain their checkout rather than being discarded with an empty placeholder. Recovery may retry saving once its underlying failure is resolved. Detected shell directory mutations also protect ancestors of registry data, immutable `.git` pointers and the parent checkout. Registry data remains distinct from the editable worktree in ownership checks. These are checks of detected writes, not a shell or operating-system sandbox.
- Capture limits make retention a deliberate exception to automatic unchanged/merged/discarded cleanup. A new or changed nonignored oversized output leaves a failed, recoverable record and its checkout intact; an omitted copy hash never means a deletion. Initially oversized copy files are compared with their physical baseline, including deletion or reduction below the cap. Unchanged pre-existing large files and intentionally excluded initial inputs retain their existing behavior. Two red controls on the committed pre-fix implementation (`757f8d0a`) reproduced both a growing copy file deleting its parent's baseline and an oversized-only Git result being removed as unchanged. Separate native Git controls confirmed that both index flags could hide even under-cap changed bytes from ordinary diff/add/status.
- Git staging clears assume-unchanged and skip-worktree flags in the task/private index, while preserving absent sparse entries and the parent's real index. Cleanup inspects a disposable index with those flags cleared. Oversized Git baseline equality uses streaming raw-byte fingerprints tied to the saved blob identity, so flags cannot hide changed bytes and unchanged CRLF/normalized checkouts remain valid. Merge, discard and every actual removal verify that saved results still capture current output; failed outputs can be reduced/restored and reconciled before retrying.
- Copy link changes are outside the supported logical file set and retain the checkout rather than disappearing as unchanged. A no-follow inventory compares original provenance links, normalizes internal targets across copied trees and keeps generated-directory exclusions. It preserves unchanged inherited links through partial merge/resume. Filename dictionaries and rehydrated baseline hashes have no prototype, so files or links named `__proto__`, `constructor` or `toString` cannot disappear or become false baseline entries.
- The WP additionally changes core background jobs, task claims, the internal writable-input shape, shell/ownership plumbing and an expected-branch worktree-removal primitive to close the audited boundaries. Registered task workspaces disable LSP and MCP unless user isolation settings enable them, and always disable snapshot tracking. The new logical-base helper isolates the private-index and immutable-copy boundaries for focused coverage.
- Validation covers 386 distinct passing cases across 15 individual files: core jobs (22), core copy (29), engine isolation (72), logical bases (6), claims (9), shell ownership (34), task management (44), run-state (5), task tool (134), LSP/MCP/snapshot defaults (4/4/3), raw task worktrees (5), existing worktree lifecycle (14) and removal (1). Two platform-specific cases were skipped on Linux. The full 134-case task-tool file passed without a transient failure. Unchanged passing files were not repeated for the capture correction.
- The complete isolation file passed all 69 cases before the final dictionary correction (167.69 seconds). The final source then passed 12 affected cases, including three additional filename cases, covering 72 distinct cases overall; this is not a claim that the complete 72-case file ran green. The final core copy file passed all 29 cases. Final engine and core package typechecks passed after every source correction. The earlier trust-fixture failure was resolved by approving the workspace before configuration loads, and the subsequent full 69-case run includes that test. No production trust guard or test deadline was widened.
- Independent static review closed registry/metadata redirection, unsafe link ancestors, failed snapshot retention, preparation/recovery and acquired-instance lifetime, recursive protection, core finalizer draining, duplicate admission and the combined partial-merge/resume/stacked-baseline cases. A further independent review verified the final source hashes and closed cap omission, hidden index flags, raw baseline normalization, absent sparse entries, physical copy-baseline changes, copy links and prototype-named files. The module remains unwired until WP28; the complete Phase 5 review remains pending.
- WP28 must wire execution admission and lightweight session-deletion enqueueing, including deletion without an ambient instance. Its I19 gate must persist an owner-only conversation-family fence before private briefing/events and cover historic/live events, relay guest markers, descendants and ancestors whose reports contain private data. Live ancestry alone is insufficient after an intermediate session is deleted. No public HTTP schema changed in WP27; public routes and client generation remain WP28 work.

## 2026-10-30 WP28 (isolation wiring and merge surfaces) — status: implemented and committed; review rounds 1–6 fixed; the first four full CI runs red: every failure of the first two diagnosed and fixed in the CI5 entry below; the third (run 41147540669, on CI5's `f6f04e34`) and the fourth (run 41156583300, on round 2's `ee698eac`) red only on three Windows engine shards (1, 2 and 5 of 8, then 2, 3 and 5), with the same three failures, each diagnosed and fixed in the CI6 entry below (the original cause of six of shard 5's 91 cases stays an inference); Phase 5 rounds 1 and 2 committed (round 2: both reviews' findings on WP22–WP28, round 1 and CI5, one finding deferred, since fixed and committed in the P5F10 entry below); the fifth full run (41163614397, on CI6's `06762768`) shows those fixes on Windows: green on every job that ran its tests, engine windows 1 to 7 of 8 included (2, 3 and 5 among them), with engine windows 8/8 stopped before its tests by the runner's ripgrep install (Chocolatey's feed answered 504), so its files did not run in that run (8/8 was green in run 41156583300 on `ee698eac`); the sixth full run (41171765221, on P5F10's `76a6c0f5`) green on all 17 jobs, engine windows 8/8 included; main integrated in the MAIN entry below and committed (merge commit `3e5d1bff` and four commits after it; MAIN commit gate entry below), the push and the full run on that integration pending

Contract corrections (intent-preserving):
- Route 4's patch, directory, branch and details go only to the authenticated owner, not "owner or control". A guest in control of a session, and owner credentials carrying the forwarded-guest marker, are guests. Every other viewer with ordinary view scope (a session claim, or the guest workspace directory that holds the session) receives a server-built counts projection: whitelisted identity, status and counts, the constant title "Isolated task", zero timing, no files, `mergeable: "unknown"`, no patch, branch or paths. It reads the registry only, never Git. The projection is recognised from the final bytes on that one route, by exact shape, rather than by response identity, because compression replaces the response object; the final gate now decodes gzip/deflate bodies and refuses any encoding it cannot inspect. No header or query flag widens it. `diff=true` from a non-owner still yields counts.
- `task_manage inspect diff:true` gives the patch to an agent wrapped as subagent data (TaskMailbox envelope). The family is owner-only by then, so the patch never reaches a guest.
- The `isolation` parameter is `Schema.Literal("worktree")` with the §4.4 text. Omitting it on a resume keeps the saved assignment; naming `worktree` for a child that was shared is the resume mismatch. The batch duplicate-brief key includes `isolation`.
- Durable audience state adds `session_audience`, `session_lineage` (with a `fork` flag, review round 4) and `session_private_directory` (migration `20261029032955_session_audience`) beyond §4.10, so the owner-only family fence, guest-input provenance and private roots survive deletion and restarts. Launching isolation fences the whole conversation family permanently (ancestors, descendants, forks, future children). Guests cannot isolate, and guest input after the fence is refused. Raw HTTP/SSE content, public sharing and warp are owner-only for that family.
- Owner credentials carrying the forwarded-guest marker are refused every owner-only route except a warp's `POST /sync/steal` (review round 5). Engines before WP28 steal with the marker to say a guest worked in the session; the handler takes that request as a guest's (a private family is refused, guest input is recorded before the move), so the marker only narrows what the same credentials may do without it. A guest's own credentials stay refused there.
- `isOwnerOnlyRoute` adds only the merge/discard regex; the workflow rules stay with WP32. Merge and discard are also refused before any remote forward for a non-owner, including a guest in control and a forwarded owner. The desktop bridge's owner check now also refuses a forwarded owner; `owner(message)` carries each route's refusal text.
- Core `BackgroundJob` gains `onComplete` (not in §4.8) as the save barrier. A cancel that stops a running job waits for it (it only records `settling` and forks the save); a cancel that reaches a job already ended on its own (`completed` or `error`) waits only for its runs, never for that job's save (review round 6). `TaskManager` gains `counts`; `changes` takes `control` instead of `owner`; `discard` takes `reason`, kept bounded to 1000 characters only on the private registry record (`discardReason`).
- `Tool.Context` gains `assertReadable`, `assertGitReadable` and `assertOutput` (beyond §4.11). These are checks of detected access and text, not an operating-system sandbox; string detection misses encodings and arbitrary interpreter output. Wired: Tool.define (before truncation), plugin tools (before truncation), SessionTools (before the `tool.execute.after` hook, on streamed metadata, and on error text, which is replaced by a constant refusal), MCP tools and the three MCP resource tools (before truncation), shell (working directory and detected reads before the ask; every recognised `git` invocation with its own assignments; each output chunk with a 4 KiB carry-over before buffering, the truncation file or metadata), grep and glob roots, LSP files, Read, and prompt attachments (text and binary).
- Same-repository guest behaviour: a task's linked Git metadata lives in the ordinary repository's `.git/worktrees`. An ancestor check no longer counts a private root reached only through a nested `.git` segment, so a guest may list and search the shared checkout (tools skip `.git`); selecting `.git` or anything inside it still overlaps. Guest Git in a repository whose common directory holds private metadata stays refused for every command, read-only ones included, because the object store and refs are shared. That is a conservative rule, not a repository-wide privacy mark.
- The Git routing guard refuses `GIT_CONFIG_*`, trace and namespace settings that the command itself adds. Configuration the engine was started with (for example a proxy's `GIT_CONFIG_*`) is inherited identically by the probe and the command, so it no longer refuses every guest read.
- Execution admission: `TaskIsolation.admit` walks ancestors only for owner-only families (an unfenced family cannot hold isolation records), refuses a retired isolated task or its shared descendants, and refuses an orphan only in a private family. Legacy HTTP prompt, prompt_async, command and shell pre-admit through the new `SessionPrompt.admit` and answer 400 (their declared error) before anything is stored; free-model resume answers `InvalidRequestError` with the message. The in-run check stays as a backstop. The V2 `session.prompt` and `session.resumeFreeModels` routes, whose runner cannot see task placement, refuse any helper with a parent inside an owner-only family with `ConflictError`; the launching root still runs there. V2 compaction and revert are not gated (no tools run at the work placement).
- Recovery reconciles a job-driven isolation record (preparing, running, settling, failed) only when nothing can still run it. The task job row for the record's attempt or a later one decides when it exists: its owner must be gone (`BackgroundJobStore.alive(...) === "dead"`); a row for an earlier attempt never decides for a newer one. The row is written after the child's subagent record, within a bound, and a failed write is only logged, so without a matching row the child's own record decides through `SubagentLifecycle.liveness`, as it does for the helper itself: its attempt must have reached the record's, and its owner must be gone. A record whose child session was deleted is recovered too (nothing can still run a deleted conversation). A live or unknown owner leaves the record alone, which covers a slow start in this process and another engine on the same data folder. A `failed` record already settled for its attempt is retried at boot only, never on the 5-minute passes. Interrupted merges and discards are finished at boot only. Each record's failure, and a registry that cannot be listed at all, is logged without stopping the ordinary interrupted-helper pass; `settle({recover})` also skips work this process still runs. Once recovered, a record left `running` or `settling` by a lost row can be resumed, discarded or swept like any other.
- The task tool now reads `TaskIsolation` through `ToolRegistry`'s declared dependency. A run joined to one already going reuses that run's attempt and skips reserve/start/resume while its checkout is live. A message whose job's runs ended while it prepared (for example while it waited on its approval) is refused with the same "saving its changes" error as a resume during a save, instead of starting a new job that would wait for the save while holding the control lock. `TaskManager.resume` keeps judging against `root.id`, which `resumable` already proves is the trusted parent row.
- CLI `vector export` keeps `privacy`, `guestInput` and `ancillaryUsage` on `--sanitize` local archives, and refuses portable conversion (`--public`, or `--sanitize` of a native session) of an owner-only family. The owner's local raw export is unchanged; guest-only families may still use the portable format.
- Revert and unrevert of a session with an isolation record return 409 `ConflictError` (resource `isolated-task`).
- `TaskChangesQuery.diff` is `QueryBoolean`, advertised to the SDK as boolean or `"true"|"false"` like the other query booleans.
- Known limits: copies on public replicas made before a fence cannot be revoked. Per-call guard cost: owner families pay one guest-input query per check (one recursive family query; the second family walk runs only when a legacy guest list exists, and that file is reread only when its stat changes); guest answers are remembered. The family query walks `session_lineage` and `session` parent edges by index in each direction (about 0.1 ms for a 21-member family among 10,000 sessions, against about 80 ms for the earlier union-of-all-edges walk); it is not cached because guest input can arrive mid-call.
- Private directory keys fold case on win32 and on darwin (NFC, then lowercase), at fence time and at lookup, including keys stored before this change. The engine's file-lock keys, which `PrivateAccess` passes in, are folded on a case-insensitive Mac volume, so without this every guest read and Git check on such a Mac compared a lowercased path with a cased root and never matched. Folding on a case-sensitive Mac volume only refuses more.
- Guest event delivery: an event with no session reference still fails closed, except connected/heartbeat and a fixed set of workspace-level events (file edits and watcher, VCS branch, LSP, project and directories, MCP tools, installation, instance disposal, worktree and workspace status, plugins, model catalog, IDE). Those pass the same private-text and directory checks, and every session or directory nested in them may only narrow them. Toasts, PTY, memory, research and other unscoped events stay closed to guests.
- Guest read guards run before the tool's own stat and asks: grep and glob check their search root before their ask and stat, LSP before the outside-folder ask, and Read before its stat (unchanged).
