Review round 1 (2026-10-30, findings verified against the code; one file at a time through the lock wrapper, fresh XDG roots):
- Isolation is decided from the durable launch assignment (`SubagentLifecycle.read(child).isolation`) as well as the registry. A child launched isolated but still waiting for its slot or its dependencies has no registry record yet; a shared task depending on it is now refused, and resuming it (task tool or `TaskManager.resume`) keeps isolation, reserving and starting its checkout in the slot. `TaskIsolation.check` accepts a resumed child with no reservation. Regression (task tool): "a queued isolated child keeps its assignment…" fails on the old decision (it ran into the 30 s test timeout).
- The foreground result reads the registry with `find`: a child stopped before its slot reports cancelled instead of a raw ENOENT naming the registry path. Regression "a foreground isolated child stopped before its slot reports cancelled…" fails on the old render.
- Recovery as described above (row attempt match, row-less records judged by the child's record, failed records retried at boot only). Regression in isolation-recovery: a stale dead attempt-1 row with a live attempt-2 owner is left alone, then recovered once the child's owner is gone; it fails on the old decision. The boot-only retry of settled `failed` records has no automated test: the periodic pass is not reachable from a test without waiting 5 minutes.
- Case folding and the indexed family query as described above. Core audience regressions: darwin folding (fails on the old source) and 200 family checks among 20,000 sessions under 2 s (old source: 35 s). Engine private-access: a darwin `FileLock.key` is recognised as private.
- Guest list filtering runs the cheap scope check before the privacy query (engine and server `allowsSessionLocation`).
- Workspace-level events reach guests again (above). Regression in session-audience: watcher, branch and LSP events pass; a private path, a nested private session or a toast does not.
- Grep, glob and LSP guards moved before their stat and asks (above). New SessionTools-driven regressions in private-tools: a guest's shell output naming a private folder across two chunks is refused before the truncation file (fails without the 4 KiB carry-over: the file is written), an owner's same command saves it; guest read/grep/glob through a link into a private folder or its parent are refused before any ask (fails without Read's guard), owners are asked; a guest's MCP resource naming a private folder is refused before truncation (fails without the pre-truncation check).
- Runs after these changes: core audience 17/17, public-session-share 27/27, free-model-guests 3/3; engine private-tools 5/5, private-access 8/8, session-audience 9/9, isolation-recovery 3/3, task tool 145/145 (full file, 131.8 s; an earlier full run had 1 failure in "stopping a background subagent stops what depends on it without starting a parent turn" whose error output was not captured, so its cause is not known; the case passed alone and in the second full run, and in review round 6 it passed 10 times in 10 separate processes and in the full file), isolation 72/72 (full file, 145.2 s), task-manage 44/44 (the Bun-spawning case excluded by name), recovery 3/3 (the 4 Bun-spawning cases excluded), task-isolation 3/3, isolation-admission 6/6, grep 19/19, glob 2/2, read 45/45, lsp 4/4, httpapi-session 26/26, auth-guest 27/27, audience-lineage 4/4, session-tools-writable 2/2, shell-ownership 35/35. Typechecks green: core, engine, server. No public HttpApi or schema change, so no regeneration.
- Not changed in this round, noted for the next one: `reserve` stacks every `depends_on` entry, and `start` fails on a shared one (no registry record); the spec stacks only isolated dependencies with state `changed`. Fixed in review round 2 below.

Review round 2 (2026-10-30, seven findings verified against the code; each regression run against the old behaviour first; one file at a time through the lock wrapper, fresh XDG roots):
- Stacking follows F.4 start step 2. `reserve` (task tool and `TaskManager.resume` alike) stacks only dependencies whose isolation record is `changed`; a shared, unchanged, discarded or merged one is not stacked, so it never blocks `start` or `merge`. `start` filters the reserved list again, because a dependency merged or discarded between reservation and start would otherwise leave the reservation failing on every resume ("no saved changes to stack"). Merge still waits for a stacked dependency that is unmerged (the owner may `includeStacked`). Regressions: task tool "an isolated child stacks only changed isolated dependencies…" (a child depending on a completed shared task and on an `unchanged` isolated one starts and merges with `stacked: []`; a `changed` one is stacked and merges first; on the original code the child never started), isolation "a dependency discarded after the reservation is not stacked…" (fails without the start filter).
- `TaskManager.resume`'s save barrier is the task tool's: both call the shared `TaskIsolation.complete` (normal end saved before the job's waiters return; Stop or interrupt marks `settling` and forks the save). Regression (task tool file): "TaskManager.resume runs an isolated child in its worktree, saves before completion, and a Stop never waits on Git" (attempt 2 runs in the same checkout and is `changed` when `jobs.wait` returns; cancelling attempt 3 under a held commit returns within 5 s, the record is `settling`, the control lock is free, and the forked save reaches `changed`).
- Runtime fault found while verifying the above (Effect 4.0.0-beta.83 under Bun): a Stop that runs synchronously inside one of the job's own steps (its run completed a Deferred whose waiter stops it, as these tests do) interrupted the job's fiber re-entrantly. Its finalizers and the completion callback started while that step was still on the stack, and the step's result (`true` from `Deferred.succeed`) was then delivered to the callback's first pending step, shifting every later result by one. `TaskIsolation.complete` saw `true` for its record and never marked `settling`. The earlier one-step registry read only hid it inside the read's span frames. `BackgroundJob.cancel` now yields before closing the job's scope, so the interruption starts from a fresh tick. Core regression "a Stop issued inside the job's own step leaves its completion callback's results its own" (old source: `[true, "first"]` instead of `["first", "second"]`).
- Review, merge and discard of a task with no isolation record answer `not_found` "This task has no isolated changes." (as counts already did), never a raw ENOENT naming the private registry path under `invalid`. Regression (task-manage): a shared child through `changes`, `counts`, `merge` and `discard` (old source: `invalid`).
- Replication refuses per conversation, not per response. `/sync/history` with the replication marker omits every private or guest-input conversation and returns the rest; a session warp names the conversation it moves in `x-vector-session-transfer` (an internal header like the replication marker, which only narrows) and only that conversation's restriction refuses it. One restricted conversation therefore no longer keeps a remote workspace in `error` or blocks warping its public sessions. Regressions: session-audience "replication omits private and guest-input history per conversation…" (old source: 403 for the whole history), control-plane workspace (a warp sends the transfer header and maps the source's 403 to `WorkspacePrivateTransferError`).
- Asking whether a task is isolated no longer depends on the data folder's link guard: `find` returns "not isolated" when the task's registry directory does not exist, and reads an existing entry under the full guard. Ordinary result notes, revert, resume and dependency checks keep working with a linked data or isolation folder; only isolation itself refuses it. Regression (isolation-admission): a linked `task-isolation` folder, with `find`, `records` and HTTP revert of an ordinary session (old source: "Isolation data cannot be a symbolic or hard link."), and an existing entry under the link is still refused.
- The owner's control-plane relay (replication marker, owner credentials, no forwarded guest) carries unscoped events such as terminals and toasts after the same private-text, session and directory checks; a guest's stream still drops them. Regression (session-audience): the relay stream carries `pty.created` and `tui.toast.show`, the guest stream does not (old source: dropped for the relay).
- Deleted conversations' changes no longer count toward `max_kept`, and `sweep` scans the registry once per project per process but sweeps conversations deleted later on each following pass (recovery runs every 5 minutes), so their checkouts do not wait for a restart. Regression (isolation): at the cap of 20, deleting a session with a changed task re-admits isolation, and the next pass discards and removes it, keeping its patch (old source: still refused with `kept`).
- Known limits (unchanged by this round): a restricted conversation's history is re-read and dropped on each replication history request, because the requester never holds its cursor; live events of private or guest-input conversations on a remote workspace do not reach the owner's local app through the relay (the owner reads them through routed requests); a replica made before a restriction keeps what it has.
- Resolved in review round 3, not standing (the case was rewritten to the WP27 same-ID rule; the file is 13/13 since): in this round engine `test/background/job.test.ts` "ignores stale settlements after restarting a stopped job" had timed out at its same-ID restart. It did so with the base commit's `packages/core/src/background-job.ts` too: WP27 made a same-ID start wait for the previous job's completion, and the old test restarted while a stopped run still waited on its release. Its other 12 cases passed at the time.
- Formatting: prettier applied to the WP28 files that were prettier-clean at the base, and to the new files; whitespace only.
- Runs after these changes: core background-job 26/26; engine task tool 147/147 (full file, 231.3 s), isolation 74/74 (full file, 216.3 s), task-manage 45/45 (the Bun-spawning case excluded by name), isolation-admission 7/7, session-audience 10/10, task-isolation 3/3, isolation-recovery 3/3, control-plane workspace 38/38, notify 16/16, session-stop 4/4, task-batch-promotion 5/5, auth-guest 27/27, background job 12/13 at the time (the failure above, resolved in review round 3, 13/13); after formatting, core audience 17/17 and private-tools 5/5. Typechecks green: core, engine, server. No public HttpApi or schema change, so no regeneration.

Review round 3 (2026-10-30, nine findings verified against the code; each regression run against the old behaviour first where it guards a fix; one file at a time through the lock wrapper, fresh XDG roots):
- Merge and discard accept no body (`[HttpApiSchema.NoContent, …]`, as `taskBackground` does): the SDK sends `merge({ sessionID, taskID })` and `discard({ sessionID, taskID })` with no body, which the plain struct payload refused with a decode 400 ("Expected object, got undefined") before the task manager. No body merges every saved file and discards without a reason. Regressions (task-isolation): a bodiless merge while saving answers code `running`, and a bodiless merge and discard through another session answer code `forbidden` (old declaration: the decode error, no code). The exerciser's three `.missing` scenarios now send merge and discard without a body and assert code `not_found`, not only the status.
- Engine `test/background/job.test.ts`: the case that restarted a stopped job under its ID is rewritten to the WP27 same-ID rule (a contract correction of the test, not of the code): the restart is forked, has not begun while the stopped run still winds down (its run not started, the job still `cancelled`), and once released the stop settles `cancelled`, the restart is `running` and nothing late overwrites it. The file is green again (13/13); the round-2 failure is resolved.
- Spec "Current cross-engine transfer limit (WP28)" now describes per-conversation omission, the `x-vector-session-transfer` warp refusal and its local zero-network refusal, instead of whole-history refusal.
- Replication history also leaves out an ordinary conversation whose events would fail the final response check (private text such as a task worktree path in tool output, or a private session or directory it names), using that same check per conversation (`SessionAudienceHttp.response` on the conversation's rows) before the final gate, so one such conversation no longer turns the whole history into a 403. A warp naming a conversation that is left out is refused. Regression (session-audience): a public conversation whose update shows a fenced worktree path is left out, the rest arrives with 200 and without the path, a warp of it is refused and of a public one is not, and the owner's ordinary read is unchanged (old source: 403 for the whole history).
- `TaskIsolation.check` no longer counts the task being resumed toward `max_kept`: resuming moves its record from `changed` to `running`. Regression (isolation): at the cap, `check` with the kept task's own `resumeID` is admitted while a new launch is still refused (old source: `kept`).
- The legacy guest list (`free-model-guest-sessions.txt`) no longer turns a non-ENOENT read error into a defect. A list that exists but cannot be read is logged and fails closed only for privacy: `hasGuestInput` answers true for an unfenced family (guards apply, isolation is refused), `fenceIsolation` refuses a family not yet fenced with a typed `Denied`, and `register` still works and imports the list on a later call. A family already fenced keeps answering false and may add roots: its fence read the list and guest input is refused after it, so treating its owner as a guest would only refuse the owner's own isolated work. While the list cannot be read, `FreeModelGuests.marked` (which asks `hasGuestInput`) treats an unfenced owner family as marked, the conservative side. Regression (core audience): the list's path made a folder (EISDIR, as a held file on Windows gives a non-ENOENT error): registering works, an unfenced family has guest input and cannot be fenced, a fenced one stays the owner's and adds a root, and with the list gone again an empty list marks nothing (old source: `register` dies).
- Private roots are cached per audience instance and reread only when `session_private_directory` changes: each check reads the row count, the last rowid and the last row's directory (rows are only ever inserted, and another engine on the same data folder may insert them), never every row. `containsPrivateText` runs one character-trie scan over the text for every spelling of every root, starting only where a spelling's first character occurs, instead of an `includes` per spelling per root; `isPrivateDirectory` looks up the target and each folder above it in a set instead of comparing with every root. `overlapsPrivateDirectory` still compares with every root (cached). Regression (core audience): 5,000 roots, 200 text checks over 8 KB and 200 directory checks under 2 s, a root deep in the list found in plain and JSON spellings and as a folder, and a root inserted directly afterwards (as another engine would) seen by the next check. The previous per-root scan took 3.7 s for the same checks in a standalone measurement, table reads excluded.
- Shell: the guest read guard before the asks also covers the outside folders the scan resolved (`scan.dirs`), recursively, so a private folder reached through `~`, a wildcard, a variable or `..` is refused before the owner's external-directory prompt. Regression (private-tools): `cat <parent>/private-shell-gl*/secret.ts` from a guest family is refused with no ask; the owner's same command is asked `external_directory` and runs (old source: the guest was asked and the file's content returned).
- Route 4 with real guest credentials (test coverage): a new task-isolation case builds the production stack with an owner password, a guest password, a guest session claim and a guest workspace directory. A guest whose session claim covers one fenced family, and a guest whose workspace directory alone covers another, each get exactly the counts projection (no path, title, file or patch) for `diff=true`, the owner gets the full patch on the same stack, merge and discard are 403 for the guest, and the same guest asking through an unclaimed member of the invited family gets 403.
- Runs after these changes: core audience 19/19, free-model-guests 3/3, public-session-share 27/27; engine background job 13/13, task-isolation 4/4, session-audience 11/11, private-tools 6/6, isolation 74/74 (full file, 202.2 s), task tool 147/147 (full file, 200.3 s), auth-guest 27/27, control-plane workspace 38/38, shell-ownership 35/35, private-access 8/8, audience-lineage 4/4. HttpApi exerciser: effect and auth modes for `experimental.task` 15/15 each, coverage mode pass=325 missing=0 skip=0. Generators: `./packages/sdk/js/script/build.ts` and `bun run generate` in packages/client ran; the payload change is not visible in OpenAPI, so the generated SDK and client are unchanged by this round. Typechecks green: core, engine, server, sdk/js. Prettier applied to this round's files that were prettier-clean at the base and to new files (the exerciser index was not clean at the base and is left as is; its new lines are formatted).

Review round 4 (2026-10-30, seven findings verified against the code; each regression run against the old behaviour first; one file at a time through the lock wrapper, fresh XDG roots):
- Task text (the Phase 2 hand-off, A.3 line 8, A.4 rules 3 and 7). Confirmed: the isolation, merge and discard text that the Phase 2 entry held back for WP28 had not been restored, so rule 3 still offered only non-overlapping owned_paths for parallel edits. Rule 3 now reads "Entries for parallel implementation need non-overlapping owned_paths or isolation: "worktree", and concrete success_criteria. An isolated child's changes stay in its worktree until you merge or discard them with task_manage." This carries A.4 rule 7's isolation clause in rule 3, where the Phase 2 entry assigned it. Rule 7 itself is not added for isolation alone: WP18 to WP21 state model, effort, output_schema and fork in their parameter descriptions instead. Policy line 8 reads "The task tool's description has the rules for owned_paths, isolation, depends_on, success_criteria and limits." (model, effort and output_schema stay out of it, as WP18 and WP19 left them). The system.ts comment no longer says isolated worktrees are not taken yet. Changed expectation: system.test.ts (the policy names isolation in line 8; output_schema stays out). Byte sizes: `task.txt` 2489 → 2614; `<subagent_policy>` general 2836 and specialists-only 2211 (each 11 bytes more), manage-only 182 (unchanged).
- Reservation left by an earlier attempt (task tool). Confirmed: a run that found its isolation record `preparing` called `start`, which keeps the reservation's attempt, so a resume after a Stop between reserve and start (or after a failed start prelude) ran the child in a checkout recorded for attempt 1, and `TaskIsolation.complete` for attempt 2 skipped the save: the record stayed `running` with no job, and every later resume, merge and discard was refused until a restart. Any existing record that is not this run's live checkout is now taken over with `TaskIsolation.resume` for the run's attempt, as `TaskManager.resume` does. Regression (task tool): "a resume takes over a reservation an earlier attempt left preparing, and saves it under its own attempt" (old source: the note has no Vector counts line and the record stays `running` at attempt 1).
- Server filesystem guard on macOS. Confirmed: `FileAccess` compared Git's common directory with the requested path case-sensitively, so on a case-insensitive Mac volume a guest's `/api/fs/read/.GIT/objects/…` or `.GIT/refs/…` (which no earlier guard matches) passed. Both sides are now folded with `SessionAudience.foldPath` (the rule the audience already used for private roots, now exported), and `FileAccess.make` takes an optional `platform`. Regression (engine private-access): with `platform: "darwin"` a guest's `.GIT/objects/<tip>` and `.GIT/refs/heads/<branch>` are refused and the owner's are not, while on Linux `.GIT` stays an ordinary absent folder (old source: the darwin `.GIT` read passed).
- Git locale. Confirmed: both guest discovery probes recognised a folder outside any repository by Git's English "not a git repository", so under a translated locale every guest read there was refused. Both probes now run with `LC_ALL=C`, as TaskIsolation's own Git runs do. Regression (engine private-access, on Linux with `localedef` and the de_DE locale source, skipped elsewhere): a German locale built into a private `LOCPATH` and a one-message Git catalog (`GIT_TEXTDOMAINDIR`) make Git answer in German; a guest's read and `git status` outside any repository pass, and the private repository stays refused (old source: "This operation could expose private task data."). The server probe drops every `GIT_*` variable, so the test catalog cannot reach it: its half of the change is the same one line, and the test's server call passes with or without it.
- Guest input across forks. Confirmed: the family walk followed fork lineage both ways, so a guest's input in a fork marked the conversation it was forked from and that conversation's other forks, through `FreeModelGuests.marked` (guest limits, memory and skill learning, distill, research, the training gate), the tool guards, replication and the export marker. `session_lineage` gains `fork` (folded into the uncommitted audience migration; the generator rewrote `schema.json` and `schema.gen.ts`, and `script/migration.ts --check` is clean). `register` writes its `forkOf` edge with `fork = 1`; a parent edge between the same two sessions wins. `hasGuestInput` follows parent edges both ways and fork edges only from a fork to its source. The owner-only fence still covers every edge both ways, and `canIsolate` still agrees with it: guest input in any fork of the family refuses isolation, because the fence would make that fork owner-only too. Regressions: core audience "guest input in a fork stays out of its source and the source's other forks, and a source's reaches them" and free-model-guests "a guest's input in a fork marks the fork, never the conversation it was forked from" (old walk: the source was marked).
- Local export lock. Confirmed: `SessionArchive.export` ran entirely in a BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction, the JSON encode and Schema decode of the whole history included, so a terminal export held back another engine's writes on the same data folder until SQLite's 5 s busy timeout. It now reads the session row, the audience flags and the history in one deferred read transaction (one WAL snapshot, no write lock; a fence only adds rows, so content written after it is not in the snapshot either) and encodes and decodes after it ends. Public capture is unchanged: `withPublic` still holds its IMMEDIATE transaction around the whole preview. Regression (core public-session-share): "a local export reads beside another engine's open write transaction" (a second connection holds BEGIN IMMEDIATE and the export finishes within 3 s; with the read made IMMEDIATE again it fails with LockTimeoutError "database is locked").
- The round-2 lines that still presented the engine `job.test.ts` failure as standing are annotated as resolved in round 3.
- Observed, not changed (outside this round's findings; resolved in review round 5, the file is 15/15): engine `test/server/free-model-guest.test.ts` "a session warped here from an engine where a guest worked in it stays off may-train models" fails, 14/15: its `/sync/steal` with owner credentials and `x-vector-guest-request: 1` now answers 403, because WP28 treats owner credentials carrying the forwarded-guest marker as a guest. It fails the same with this round's audience change reverted, and the test file is unchanged since the base. The sending side already refuses to warp a guest-input conversation (`requireTransfer`, the bounded transfer policy), so this test's expectation and the steal relay need reconciling in the next review round.
- Runs after these changes: core audience 20/20, free-model-guests 4/4, public-session-share 28/28, database-migration "applies tracked migrations to an empty database" 1/1 (the file's `--check` case spawns Bun and was run directly instead: clean); engine task tool 148/148 (full file, 194.6 s), private-access 10/10, system 20/20, audience-lineage 4/4, private-tools 6/6, session-audience 11/11, isolation-admission 7/7, auth-guest 27/27, free-model-guest 14/15 (above; 15/15 after review round 5). Typechecks green: core, server, engine. No public HttpApi change, so no regeneration.

Review round 5 (2026-10-30, six findings verified against the code; each regression run against the old behaviour first unless stated; one file at a time through the lock wrapper, fresh XDG roots):
- Forwarded steal (the blocker test regression and the cross-version finding share one cause). Confirmed: `free-model-guest.test.ts` "a session warped here from an engine where a guest worked in it stays off may-train models" failed 14/15 with 403 at its `/sync/steal`, because the authorization layers refused owner credentials carrying the forwarded-guest marker on every owner-only route, `/sync/*` included. An engine before WP28 replays a guest-worked conversation with owner credentials and no marker (accepted and stored with no guest provenance) and then steals it with the marker, so that 403 left an unmarked replica here and a half-done warp. `ServerAuth.refusedRequest` replaces `ownerOnlyRequest` in the engine and server authorization layers (credential and no-password paths alike) and lets a forwarded owner reach only `POST /sync/steal`; the contract correction above records it, and the spec's transfer-limit paragraph says so. The handler takes the request as a guest's: `assertSession` refuses a private family, `admit` records guest input, and `FreeModelGuests.mark` (whose legacy list older engines on the same data folder read) runs before the move; it now marks whenever the requester is not the plain owner instead of testing the raw header. Removed as unreachable: the WP28 sender's `guests.marked(...)` marker after `requireTransfer` (which already refuses any guest-input conversation; `marked` asks `hasGuestInput` first), with `Workspace`'s FreeModelGuests dependency and the comments that said a guest mark travels with a warp. Regressions: the free-model-guest case, unchanged, is green; server authorization "only a warp's steal carrying the forwarded-guest marker reaches its handler" (with the steal exception removed: 403 where 200 is expected), which also keeps a guest's steal and a forwarded `/sync/replay` at 403; engine session-audience "a forwarded steal is a guest's: a private family stays unmoved, a public session moves with its guest input" (a private family's forwarded steal answers 403 with its workspace and guest flag unchanged; a public session moves with guest input recorded and isolation refused; forwarded `/sync/replay` and `/sync/start` answer 403). The session-audience case was not run against the old source; its public steal is the same forwarded steal the old gate answered 403.
- Guest guards without Git. Confirmed: `PrivateAccess.read`/`git` and the server's `FileAccess` probed Git for every guest path even when no private root existed, and refuse any failed probe that does not say "not a git repository"; `Git.run` reports a spawn failure as exit 1. On a host where Git cannot run, or in a folder Git refuses (dubious ownership), every guest file tool, guest Git guard and `/api/fs/read|list|find` was therefore refused although nothing had been isolated, and every guest tool call cost two to four Git subprocesses. `SessionAudience.hasPrivateDirectories`, served from the stamp-checked roots cache, now skips those guards while no root exists; with no root neither the target nor Git's common directory can overlap one, so nothing is weakened. Once a root exists a probe Git cannot answer still refuses. Not done: caching probes per directory within one call. Regression (private-access): with only an empty folder on `PATH` and no root, a guest read, an ancestor read, the `git status` guard and the server file guard pass; after a fence elsewhere the read and the server guard refuse (old source: refused at the first read).
- Public capture lock. Confirmed: `captureArchive` ran the whole preview, its encoding and the private-text scan inside `withPublic`'s BEGIN IMMEDIATE, about once a second for a live share. It now takes the history and its privacy flag from `SessionArchive.export`'s one deferred read snapshot (the round-4 export), refuses `privacy: "owner-only"`, and builds, encodes and scans the public archive after the snapshot is released. A fence only adds rows and precedes private bytes, so a snapshot that does not show it holds none of them. The capture no longer writes `session_audience`/`session_lineage` rows: session creation, import and the fence record lineage, and the family query walks live parent edges anyway. `SessionAudience.withPublic` had no other caller and is removed with its core audience case ("public work is refused after the durable fence before its callback runs"); the share cases "private ancestors are never uploaded by auto update, restart retry or finalizer" and "an in-flight public send releases SQLite and can only send its captured pre-fence body" cover that refusal through the capture. Regression (core public-session-share): "a live share captures its update beside another engine's open write transaction" (a second connection holds BEGIN IMMEDIATE and the flush's update reaches the network within 3 s; old capture: LockTimeoutError "database is locked").
- Recovery fences. Confirmed: every pass (boot, then every 5 minutes) fenced every registry record, settled ones included, before deciding whether it needed work. Only a record about to be reconciled is now fenced, just before `reconcile`. No new test: the fence is idempotent and `reserve` fences before a record exists, so the change is visible only as fewer write transactions, which a test could observe only by spying on the service; the 3 isolation-recovery cases still cover the reconcile paths.
- Evidence accuracy. Confirmed: the status line and the Remaining list did not name the red free-model-guest file. It is green after the first item; the status line, the round-4 lines and Remaining are updated. Checked: no line still presents the engine `job.test.ts` failure as standing (round 4 annotated the round-2 lines).
- Runs after these changes: engine free-model-guest 15/15, session-audience 12/12, private-access 11/11, private-tools 6/6, auth-guest 27/27, isolation-recovery 3/3, control-plane workspace 38/38; server authorization 5/5, auth 12/12; core public-session-share 29/29, audience 19/19. Typechecks green: core, server, engine. Prettier applied to this round's three test files (the server authorization test was prettier-clean at the base; the other two are new). No public HttpApi or schema change, so no regeneration.

Review round 6 (2026-10-30, seven findings verified against the code; each regression run against the old behaviour first; one file at a time through the lock wrapper, fresh XDG roots):
- Evidence accuracy. Confirmed: the round-1 run line called the task-tool failure in "stopping a background subagent stops what depends on it without starting a parent turn" timing-based and unrelated to isolation, although its output was never captured. The line now states only what was observed. Rerun: that case 10/10 in 10 separate processes, and green in the full file (150/150 below). `bun test --rerun-each` cannot repeat these cases: from its second run every case fails with "ManagedRuntime disposed" (the file's runtime is disposed after the first run), so such a run is evidence neither way. Checked again: no line still presents the engine `job.test.ts` failure as standing (round 4 annotated the round-2 lines; the round-2 run line reads "12/13 at the time … resolved in review round 3, 13/13").
- Unreadable registry and the result. Confirmed: `TaskNotify.result` read the registry (`find`) before anything else, so a typed failure there (for example the data folder moved behind a link after launch) skipped the result summary, the notification write and the enqueue; the parent was never told. The foreground task tool failed the whole call with the registry error. Both now log the failure and deliver the result; a child launched isolated carries the constant line `TaskIsolation.UNREADABLE_REPORT` (no counts, no filenames) in place of Vector's counts line. The fence is still asserted for a found record or an isolated child, and a refusal of that reassertion is logged rather than dropping the note: the task tool fenced the family before the child or its record existed, and a fence is never lifted. Regression (task tool): "a background note still reaches the parent when its isolation record cannot be read" (the registry moves behind a link while the child runs; old source: the parent never hears back).
- Local-source warp. Confirmed: `sessionWarp` checked only `requireTransfer` (a private or guest-input family) before replaying this engine's events, while `/sync/history` also leaves out a conversation whose events fail the guest-facing response check. An ordinary conversation whose history showed a fenced worktree path could therefore be replayed to a remote engine that has no fence for it. `sessionWarp` now runs that same check (`SessionAudienceHttp.response(…, false, audience, true)` on the conversation's events) before anything is stopped or sent, and again on each replay batch, and refuses with `WorkspacePrivateTransferError`. The spec's transfer-limit paragraph says so. Regression (control-plane workspace): "a local conversation whose history shows a private task folder refuses warp before any request" (no request is made; an ordinary conversation still replays and steals; old source: the warp went through).
- Stop during a normal save. Confirmed: since `onComplete`, `BackgroundJob.cancel` of a job that had already ended on its own awaited `drained`, which is released only after the completion callback, so a Stop arriving during a normal end's save (the parent's Stop releasing a foreground child that had just returned, among others) waited, uninterruptibly, for Git. Jobs now keep a second barrier, `ran`, released once every run has finished and before the callback; a cancel that reaches a `completed` or `error` job joins `ran` only. A cancel that stops a running job still joins the callback, and `wait`, `done` and a same-ID start still wait for the save. `TaskManager.stop`, whose cancel no longer waits for that save, leaves a job that ended on its own to whoever waits on it (its watcher or the foreground call), so the task is reported with its real outcome instead of being settled as cancelled. Regressions: core background-job "cancelling a job that already ended returns without waiting for its completion callback" (old source: the cancel could not be timed out, and the case hit the 30 s test timeout), task tool "stopping the parent while a foreground isolated child's normal save runs does not wait on Git" (old source: the 30 s test timeout). The `TaskManager.stop` change has no test of its own: `observe` already reports such a job `completed`, so stop reaches one only in a race between its listing and its cancel.
- Changes view during a Stop. Confirmed: `TaskIsolation.changes` ran the patch, the parent tree (or, in copy mode, a hash of the whole parent project) and the merge assessment under the per-task lock whatever the record's state, and a Stop's completion barrier (`markSettling`) takes that lock. A record that is `preparing`, `running` or `settling` now gets its stored counts with `mergeable: "blocked"` and no patch, without Git or hashing: it has no snapshot of this attempt to show and cannot merge. Regression (isolation): "a running task's changes are read without Git, so a Stop's settling mark never waits behind a Changes view" (every Git call of the viewing service is held; old source: `markSettling` timed out).
- Sweep of a failed save. Confirmed: a deleted conversation's record whose save had failed for its attempt was saved again (`reconcile`, then `settle` with `recover`) on every recovery pass, every 5 minutes, without a log, and stayed queued because `discard` refuses an unsaved record; this contradicted the rule that such a save is retried at boot only. `sweep` no longer reruns a save that already failed for the record's attempt (TaskRecovery's boot pass, which runs before the sweep, retries it), and a discard refused for any reason but `running` drops the id from the queue with a warning; the next start's registry scan finds the deletion marker again. A save still running stays queued. Regression (isolation): "a deleted conversation's failed save is not run again on every sweep; the next start retries it" (neither the registry scan nor a later pass rewrites the record, and the checkout and its bytes stay; after a successful reconcile a rescan sweeps it; old source: the first pass rewrote the record. The record's inode number came back on that rewrite, so the check compares the write time as well).
- Legacy guest marks after a fence. Confirmed: the import of the legacy guest list set `guest_input` on every listed conversation, one whose family was already `owner_only` included. From then on `register` refused every later creation in that family, the owner's included, and `fenceIsolation` refused every reassertion, so `Session.createNext` and the event-level hooks died there and recovery stopped before `reconcile`. The import now skips a conversation whose family is already fenced and logs it once per instance: the fence is already the restrictive state, and the mark stays in the list, which `hasGuestInput` (and so `canIsolate`: no new isolation starts in that family) and FreeModelGuests (the training gate) still read. A listed family that is not fenced is imported as before. The event-level hooks are unchanged: with the import fixed, a fenced family cannot gain `guest_input` (a guest mark, a guest registration and a join into it are refused in the same serialized transaction), so their `Denied` now arises only where failing closed is intended, an isolation hint for an unfenced conversation with guest input or a creation that would join a guest-input conversation to a fenced family. Regression (core audience): "a legacy guest mark written after the fence leaves the fenced family working for its owner" (an owner's child, an event-style registration, a fork and a fence reassertion succeed; a guest is still refused; the family still has guest input and cannot isolate; an unfenced listed conversation is still imported durably; old source: `Denied` "This conversation is private to its owner.").

## 2026-10-30 Phase 5 review, round 1 (WP22–WP28 from d4a90679) — status: five findings verified, four source fixes and one record fix; committed

Contract corrections (intent-preserving):
- F.1's rule that a lock is never held across a permission prompt also covers TaskIsolation's per-task lock. An agent's merge plans and builds its diff under the lock, then releases it. It runs `assertExternalDirectory`, `assertWritable` and the edit ask without the lock, and takes the lock again to plan once more. The approval covers only the result it showed. If the record changed in between (another attempt, tip, merged list, state, deletion mark or any other field except the cached `mergeKey`/`mergeable` that a Changes view writes), the merge is refused with code `state` and nothing is written. Owner merges take the lock once, as before.
- F.4 merge and `reconcile`. A partial merge's next logical base (`advanceGit`, or `advanceCopy`) is now built before the checkpoint and the first parent write, not after the writes. The checkpoint, the writes and the record save that marks the merge complete run uninterruptibly. A failure anywhere in that span is rolled back, and a Stop or a dropped request waits for it. A copy base version that the record never published is removed when the merge fails.
- The checkpoint manifest now records what the merge writes at each path: a sha256, a link target, or nothing for a deletion. Recovery (`reconcile`, and the in-process rollback) restores a path only while it still holds what the merge wrote or what was there before. A path changed again since keeps that change. The task returns to `changed` with `merged` unchanged, and `error` names the kept paths for the owner (owner-only details, as before). `reconcile` takes the targets' file locks for this. A manifest without these records, written by the earlier WP27/WP28 source on this branch, is restored as before.
- F.4 `check`'s read-only agents are exact names: explore, review, judge, security, research, researcher and research-judge (`Agent.readOnly`). Before this, a case-insensitive prefix match refused user agents such as "reviewer-fix". The task tool's claims and `TaskManager`'s resume claims use the same predicate. On resume, `TaskManager` now also treats research agents as non-writers, which the task tool already did.
- Copy isolation's identity check allows a data folder whose own path passes through a link: the checkout must resolve to its reserved names under the real path of the data folder. Links at or below the data folder are still refused by `guarded`.

Findings (each checked against the code; each regression run against the old source first):
- Agent merge prompt under the per-task lock (major). Confirmed: `merge` held `lock(taskID)` across `by.ask`, so the owner's `changes` and `discard`, and `resume`, waited for the user's answer. Fixed as above. Regressions (isolation): "an agent's merge prompt holds no task lock: the owner reviews and discards meanwhile, and the late approval writes nothing" and "an approval of a result that was resumed and saved again while it waited is refused and writes nothing". On the old source both timed out at the owner's `changes` and at `resume` (10 s).
- No WP22 record (minor). Confirmed: no dated WP22 entry, no recorded run of `file-lock.test.ts` or `file-state.test.ts`, and no release-notes line. The WP22 entry above now holds them. Its runs found the standing memory-sources failures below.
- Read-only prefix match (minor). Confirmed: `/^(explore|review|judge|security|research)/i` disagreed with the task tool's exact names. Fixed as above. Regression (isolation, the existing read-only case extended): every built-in read-only name, research's three included, is refused, and "reviewer-fix", "Security-hardening" and "explorer" pass `check`. On the old source "reviewer-fix" was refused `readonly`.
- Merge written but recorded unfinished (major). Confirmed: after the writes, `advanceGit`/`advanceCopy` ran outside the rollback guard and before the record said the merge was complete. Recovery then restored the checkpoint over the parent's files without checking them. Fixed as above. Regressions (isolation): "a partial merge stopped while its next base is built has written nothing, so recovery cannot undo later edits" (the commit of the next base is held and the merge interrupted there; old source: the parent file was already written when the merge stopped, and the record still said the merge was in progress); "recovery undoes only what an interrupted merge left and keeps files changed since" (a crash between writes and record is simulated, then two of three merged paths are edited; old source: recovery removed the edited `two.txt`).
- Copy isolation through a linked data folder (major, portability). Confirmed on Linux: with the data folder reached through a linked parent, a copy-mode start failed "Copy isolation directory changed identity." (`verify` runs in every copy-mode start, through `protect`). This is the macOS `/var` and `/tmp` case and the Windows short-name or mapped-path case; those platforms were not run here. Fixed as above. Regression (isolation): "copy mode starts, saves and merges when the data folder is reached through a link" (the test points `Global.Path.data` through a linked parent of the same folder for its duration).
- Lane note on the progress record: checked again, and no line presents the engine `job.test.ts` failure as standing (the round-2 lines are annotated as resolved in round 3). The round-3 line that still called it "the standing failure recorded in round 2" now says the round-2 failure is resolved, so the word no longer appears next to it. The WP28 status line and Remaining now carry the first CI run's result.

CI evidence (read from the job logs on 2026-10-30; logs kept outside the repository):
- Run 41133403420 on `57f66561` (Phase 5 round 1) concluded `failure`. It is the primary evidence.
  - Green: unit (linux, with the generated-client check, the HttpApi exerciser gates and the hosted API tests), engine linux 1–3, e2e (linux), e2e (windows), the desktop engine bundle under Node, and engine windows 2/6 (25.4 of its 30 minutes).

  - Correction to the earlier record. Run 1's shard 5/6 timeout was not inside task.test.ts. local-fit.test.ts printed nothing from 19:58:28 to 20:09:56 UTC after its first 30 s timeout. task.test.ts started at 20:16:36 and had passed 35 cases, about 2 s each, when the step limit stopped it at 20:17:49.

Root causes and fixes. Nothing here ran on Windows, so every Windows item is fixed by inference only. Line numbers are HEAD's (run 2's).
1. Product, `Worktree` path comparison on Windows. `canonical` used Effect's `realPath`, which is Node's non-native realpath and keeps 8.3 short names such as `C:\Users\RUNNER~1`. Git for Windows lists a worktree under the real path it stored: the shard 5 log shows `worktree C:/Users/runneradmin/…` for a checkout created at `C:\Users\RUNNER~1\…`.
   - So `remove` with an expected branch failed "The registered task worktree no longer checks out its reserved branch". Settle then reported `failed`, and merge, discard, sweep and reconcile failed after it. `list()` also failed to hide registered task worktrees.
   - The same mismatch hits a checkout that is gone on every platform when the data folder is reached through a link. Git keeps listing the stored real path, while the old fallback kept the caller's spelling. TaskIsolation's resume calls `remove(record)` in exactly that state.
   - Fix: `canonical` resolves through `FSUtil.realpath`, which is native on Windows and resolves the deepest existing ancestor of a missing folder. It keeps the lowercase fold on Windows and stays Node-compatible.
   - Cases fixed:
     - 22 isolation.test.ts cases. Settle reported `failed` instead of `unchanged`; or merge, discard, resume or the reconcile failed with that error; or a sweep then missed the task.
     - Their titles are at HEAD lines 217 (both files), 325, 383, 437, 492, 593, 610, 683, 998, 1026, 1043, 1205, 1225, 1321, 1344, 1410, 1439, 1557, 1675 and 1726 (both actions).
     - server task-isolation:258.
     - isolation-admission: retired, V2 runner, orphan, and "an agent may merge or discard only its direct children".
     - task.test.ts stacking: "never reached unchanged".
     - create-for-task:152's hidden case.
   - Regressions in test/project/worktree-remove.test.ts:
     - "a task checkout that is gone is removed with its branch when the data folder is reached through a link". It runs on every platform. On the old source it failed with "no longer checks out its reserved branch".
     - "a task checkout named by its 8.3 short name is removed with its branch". It runs on Windows only and first asserts that the short and long spellings differ. CI-only.
   - main (`78526052`) has the same `canonical`. Its `remove` has no expected-branch mode, so there a mismatched spelling cleans the folder and leaves Git's entry and branch behind. Not changed on main by this lane; it reaches main with the later integration.
2. Fixture, line endings. Git for Windows defaults to `core.autocrlf=true`, so the task checkout and a merge's checkout bytes are CRLF, while these tests compare LF bytes.
   - `core.autocrlf false` is now pinned before any write, following workspace.test.ts's precedent, in:
     - isolation.test.ts's fixture: 9 cases, which failed at the asserts on lines 188 (both flags), 208, 471, 560, 987, 1112, 1199 and 1598;
     - create-for-task's seed case: 127;
     - task.test.ts's stacking case (its merge asserts at 6360–6384);
     - task-manage's isolated merge/discard case (its 1209 assertion).
   - The last two would have failed at those assertions once causes 1 and 3 were fixed.
   - The "autocrlf merges use checkout bytes…" case still sets `true` itself, so that behaviour stays covered. Product behaviour is unchanged: a merge writes checkout bytes under the user's line-ending settings.
3. Fixture, removing a link to a folder. Bun's `rm` of a link to a folder fails with EFAULT on Windows.
   - Affected:
     - isolation-admission's linked-folder finalizer. The junction it left at `task-isolation` then refused task-manage's merge/discard case in the same process ("Isolation data cannot be a symbolic or hard link").
     - isolation-recovery's registry link.
     - core copy.test.ts:248.
     - task.test.ts's unreadable-record release.
   - All now `unlink` the link, following memory-skills.test.ts's precedent; the two finalizers tolerate only ENOENT, as `rm`'s `force` did.
   - Only the cleanup changed, and each link keeps its kind, because only its removal failed. In run 2 isolation-recovery's test body passed with its untyped `fs.symlink` (a symbolic link, never a junction) and failed only at the finalizer's `rm` (line 326). Its link now names `"dir"`, so on Windows it is a real directory symlink whatever type an untyped call picks; Linux ignores the type. It stays the Windows case where the registry guard refuses a real directory symlink, while the admission case covers a junction. That the explicit type still passes there is inferred, not yet seen.
4. Fixture, escaping link. Git for Windows reads an NTFS junction as a directory, so the escaping-link case never produced a Git link for the merge to refuse ("Only a changed isolated task can be merged.").
   - It now creates a real directory symlink on every platform and first asserts that settle is `changed`.
   - Hosted Windows runners can create symbolic links: the filesystem ELOOP case already makes real ones there.
5. Fixture, short temp paths and raw spellings.
   - file-lock.test.ts built its expected keys from `realpathSync`, which keeps RUNNER~1. It now uses `realpathSync.native`, as `FileLock.key` is the real path, case-folded on Windows (I33).
   - These now compare `FileLock.key`s instead of raw spellings: create-for-task 152 (list() is case-folded) and 219 (Git prints forward slashes and long names), and task.test.ts's TaskManager.resume placement.
6. Fixture, simulated case-sensitive platform on a case-insensitive host. Core audience "Exact-Case" and private-access `.GIT` simulate `linux` on a Windows host, where the other spelling names the same folder.
   - They now take the host's answer (whether the other spelling exists).
   - Linux assertions are unchanged. On Windows the same folder under another spelling stays private.
7. Fixture, path spellings in text.
   - Replication case: it matched a raw Windows path against JSON, which escapes backslashes. It now matches the JSON-encoded spelling, which also makes its `not.toContain` meaningful on Windows.
   - Open-stream case: the drive-less `/private/placeholder` is now `path.resolve(...)`. The fence stores `D:\private\placeholder`, so the drive-less literal never matched.
   - Observed and not changed, for the safety review: the private-text matcher covers backslash, forward-slash and JSON spellings, but not drive-less or MSYS (`/c/...`) ones.
8. Fixture, shell. The git-screening case is bash syntax, but Windows' default shell is PowerShell, where `GIT_DIR=.git git log` is no assignment. Its first screened command was therefore `git worktree list`.
   - It now runs under bash on every platform (Git Bash on Windows).
   - It is skipped there only without Git Bash, gated like the file's pwsh/cmd describe. Every assertion is unchanged.
9. Timing, measured, not loosened. In task.test.ts's "task isolation saves", every wait that spans an isolated start, resume or save now uses `ISOLATED = "20 seconds"`. Each such step creates or loads a real Git worktree and instance, or commits through Git.
   - Evidence: locally 1.4–2.5 s (Phase 5 round 1). On Windows run 2 the cases failed exactly at these waits, while the cases that passed with one isolated child took 8.1–8.8 s each. Each wait still waits on the real record or Deferred.
   - Changed, by line and message:
     - 5875 and 6104 "the isolated save never started";
     - 5882 and 6115 "the isolated save never finished";
     - 5923 and 6085 "the isolated task never started" (5923 was 5 s);
     - 5939 "the joined run's save never finished";
     - 5974 and 6576 "the isolated child never ran" (2 s);
     - 5993 "the forked save never finished";
     - 6341, the `settled` helper's "task … never reached …";
     - 6417 "the first run's save never finished";
     - 6477 "the resumed isolated child never ran" (2 s);
     - 6491 "the forked save of the stopped resume never finished";
     - 6531 "the isolated save never started";
     - 6538 "the save never finished after the Stop".
     All others were 5 s.
   - Unchanged, because they prove the behaviour or follow no Git work:
     - the control-lock checks (2 s);
     - every Stop, join and interrupt timeout (5 s);
     - "the message never reached the running task";
     - "the message never reached its approval";
     - "the stopped task was not marked settling" and "the stopped resume was not marked settling" (the Stop records it before it returns);
     - "the parent never heard back";

   - Per-test limits raised to 60 s, where the work alone approaches the default 30 s on Windows:

     - isolation.test.ts's four includeStacked-resume cases: the Git ones took 27.4 and 27.6 s in run 2 before their final checkout removal.
10. local-fit stall. The fixture combined process-wide fake-clock jumps with real TCP and readiness work. It stalled about 11.5 minutes on Windows in run 1 and hit 4 test timeouts in both runs.
    - main fixed it in `dfc98974` (test only: in-memory fetch responses). This branch's file was identical to the merge base `6eaf445a`, and the provider source differs from main only in transform.ts.

    - This is a pre-integration port of one test file, not a merge of main; the later integration sees identical content.
11. Windows shard budget. Bun deals the sorted file list out one per shard in turn; checked against all six run-2 shard logs, 497 files, no mismatch.
    - With six shards, the shard holding isolation.test.ts ran over the limit and two others needed 25–26 minutes.

12. memory-sources pair, Linux and Windows.
    - Case 1: public-format imports are guest input by decision (handoff 7.3). `SessionArchive.import` registers actor `guest` for them, and `SessionMemory.writeHold` ranks guest first, so the saved entry's hold is `guest`, not `web`. The expectation now says `guest`; the entry is still asserted quarantined.
    - Case 2: the subagent part created a child directly, without the trusted claim that task admission gives. Since WP23, `SessionTools` refuses such a child's write before the `memory_source` ask. A temporary debug run on HEAD's source showed the child's write part failing with "This subagent's trusted file assignment is unavailable. Resume it through the parent before editing or delegating."
      - The fixture now binds an explicit unrestricted claim, as WP23 did for apply_patch. This is not a regression: production still refuses a child with no trusted assignment.
13. Settings import (robustness; the Phase 5 environment note). For a non-Git workspace the import walks each folder up to `/` or home, which mirrors the loader, so the walk is right. The match was wrong: it treated every extensionless file in each such folder as possible TOML settings. An unrelated one mentioning `permission:` then stopped startup with "Cannot import settings from … Repair this file".
    - Fix (core `migration.ts`): the only extensionless candidate is the neutral `config`, the one the importer's own tests and ordering name.
    - Regressions (import-settings.test.ts):
      - "an unrelated extensionless file above a non-git workspace neither stops the import nor is imported". On the old source it failed "Cannot import settings from …/v03-backup-x1".
      - "a broken neutral settings file that names permission still stops the import". This fail-closed path had no test.
      - "config-shaped files of two families above a non-git workspace stop the import; one alone is imported there". It pins today's behaviour for the owner question below, so that a change of answer has a test to flip; it passes on the old source too.
    - In "skips unreadable folders…", the locked extensionless file is renamed from `notes` to `config` with the same body, so it is still a candidate. That case still proves an unreadable settings file neither stops loading nor is imported.
    - main has identical `migration.ts`, so it needs the same fix; it reaches main only with the later integration or a main PR, not through this lane.

Contract corrections (intent-preserving):
- §7.2's "Engine shards (Linux 4, Windows 6)": Windows is now 8, with the same 30-minute step limit, so that every shard can finish now that the WP27/WP28 isolation tests run to completion on Windows. If a later run shows a Windows shard over about 25 minutes, the next step is more shards, not a longer limit.
- Settings import: `config` is the only extensionless settings file; any other extensionless file is not read. In each folder above a non-Git workspace, every `*.json`, `*.jsonc` or `*.toml` is still a candidate whatever its name, unless the name is excluded (it starts with `vector`, `tui`, `package`, `auth`, `credential`, `secret`, `license` or `desktop-theme`, followed by `.`, `-` or nothing, or it contains `.local.`) or the file is over 1 MiB. "Config-shaped" below means a candidate whose keys are all ConfigV1 keys, `theme`, `keybinds` or `tui`, that passes the strict decode and sets at least one of them other than `$schema`. Three cases still stop startup there, and all are left as they are:
  - a candidate (or `config`) that does not parse and mentions `permission` or a `/config.json` schema ("Repair this file before starting Vector");
  - one that parses but fails the strict decode while its `$schema` names a `/config.json` or it has a top-level `permission` ("Correct this config before starting Vector");
  - config-shaped candidates of two or more families in the same folder, where a family is a name other than `config`, `config.json` or `config.toml` without its extension ("Multiple settings files found in … Merge them into … before starting Vector"). For example `a.json` = `{"model":"x/y"}` beside `b.json` = `{"theme":"dark"}`, as any two programs could leave in `/tmp`.
  - Also, config-shaped candidates of one family in such a shared parent are imported into a `vector.jsonc` written in that folder, for example `/tmp/vector.jsonc`; the sources are kept.
  - The last two are pinned by the new discover test named in cause 13.
  So the coordinator's requirement that an unrelated file must not stop startup is met for extensionless files only. Owner question below.
- memory-sources: public-format imports hold memory as `guest`. What users see for a `vector pr` or public-format import:
  - the memory hold reason reads "an invited guest is in this session", although no guest joined;
  - document adds are refused ("A session an invited guest is in cannot keep documents");
  - personal memory is withheld.
  Meanwhile the `memory_source` ask for the same import still says hold `web` (taint; case 2 asserts it). The test change records the specified behaviour and does not approve that wording. Follow-up for the spec/tests review or the owner: either give imports their own reason text or align the two labels.
- The memory-sources subagent fixture binds an explicit unrestricted trusted claim (WP23's own fixture rule).
- Waits and per-test limits as listed in cause 9. The remaining fixture changes (causes 2–8) are portability-only: each still asserts the same files, folders and behaviour.

## 2026-10-31 Phase 5 review, round 2 (WP22–WP28 from d4a90679, round 1's fixes and CI5) — status: eleven findings verified; eight fixed in source with regressions, one record fix, one coverage test, one deferred (since fixed in P5F10); committed

Contract corrections (intent-preserving):
- F.4 merge step 4. An agent merge's `edit` ask names its targets relative to the instance's worktree, as edit and write do, because the chokepoint resolves them there for `prompt_source`, `memory_source`, the read check and the second `assertWritable`. A project that is not a Git repository has the file system root as its worktree; its merge asked with paths relative to the project folder before.
- F.4 start step 3. With stacked dependencies, the stacked seed is committed onto the unstacked seed ("Vector: your uncommitted changes when <title> started"), not directly onto HEAD, with the message "Vector: the changes of <ids>, stacked when <title> started". The task branch then reaches the unstacked seed, which an owner's `includeStacked` merge and a later discard read. Trees and diffs are unchanged; a task with nothing stacked gets the same seed as before.
- New, F.4 merge. Each partial-merge logical base (`IsolationBase.advanceGit`, both for a partial merge and for `includeStacked`) is pinned by a private ref `refs/vector/isolation/<taskID>/<commit>` in the parent repository before the record names it. A ref is never moved, so a merge that fails after pinning leaves the current base pinned too. `remove` deletes the task's refs before the checkout, since everything removed has its patch saved or nothing left to merge. Agents' git commands may not change these refs, like `vector/subagent/*` branches (the same refusal). They show in `git for-each-ref` and `git log --all`, as task branches do.
- F.4 settle and `reconcile`. "Errors become the state failed" now excepts a start that failed or was stopped before its checkout existed: no saved tip, no checkout and, in Git mode, no reserved branch. Examples are a stacked dependency that conflicts ("A conflict fails the child before any spend") or a Stop while seeding. Such a task ends `unchanged`, keeps start's error (owner details only), and its line reads "Vector: this task's isolated <worktree|copy> could not be prepared, so it changed no files." `reconcile` treats a failed or preparing record so when it has no Git binding; before, only a preparing record without a seed got this treatment. A record whose checkout was made, so has its binding, recovers as before. A record whose reserved branch exists is still retained with the existing error. A resume's reset to `preparing` now clears an earlier attempt's error.
- F.4 merge step 8. Merge writes go through a new name in the target's folder and a rename (`TaskIsolation.place`), links included; before, a link was written as `rm` then `symlink`. A rollback restores a link the same way. On Windows a link replaces an existing entry by unlink and then rename, after the link has been made, because Windows renames neither a link to a folder over an entry nor anything over one. Recovery compares link targets with backslashes read as slashes on Windows, where Node writes a relative target with backslashes.
- Registry (spec: `record.json`, `patches/`, `checkpoints/`, `baseline.json`). A copy's `baseline/`, `baseline.json` and every `base-*` version are removed when its copy is removed (unchanged, merged or discarded) and before a start copies again. A partial merge removes the base version it replaced once the record names the new one. The record, `patches/` and `checkpoints/` stay.
- F.4 `check` disk rule. Copy mode needs max(1 GiB, 2 × the measured copy bytes), because it writes the project twice (copy and baseline). Before, copy mode counted 0 bytes. Worktree mode is unchanged: max(1 GiB, 1.5 × HEAD's tracked bytes).
- I33. At a volume's root, the case probe tries the entries inside that root. A folder there has no name of its own on the volume, so before, the probe answered "ignores case" for the whole device without testing anything. On a Mac that is a repository at a mount point such as `/Volumes/Dev`.

Findings (each checked against the code; the regressions ran against HEAD's source first, with every swapped file restored and its sha256 checked):
1. Merge ask relative to the wrong root, copy mode (major). Confirmed for `prompt_source`, the read check and the second `assertWritable`. For `prompt_source`, `PromptSource.is("/vector.json", …)` misses the project's loaded `vector.json`. Two parts of the claim do not hold. `memory_source` still asked: `SessionMemory.source` matches `.vector/RULES.md`, `.vector/BRAIN.md` and `BRAIN.md` by name, so `/.vector/BRAIN.md` matched. And a repository's AGENTS.md is not a prompt source, by design (prompt-source.ts), so it asks neither way. The merge's own `assertWritable` already ran with absolute targets before the ask, so claims were enforced. Fixed as above. Regression: new `test/task/isolation-merge-ask.test.ts`, in Git and copy mode. It drives the real SessionTools chokepoint through a probe tool that calls `TaskIsolation.merge` as `task_manage` does, with `edit: allow`, on a child that changed the loaded `vector.json`. It expects a `prompt_source` ask whose pattern resolves to the parent's `vector.json`, rejects it, and checks the parent file is unchanged. On HEAD the copy case failed (no ask, and the merge wrote the file); the Git case, a control, passed.
2. Commits no ref reaches (major). Confirmed: the `reviewBase` commit (commit-tree onto its base) and `unstackedSeed` (a sibling of the seed, not its parent) were unreachable, and `git gc --prune=now` deleted both. Fixed as above (chained seed, pinned bases). Regressions (isolation): "a partial merge's logical base survives Git's gc…" and "a stacked task's pre-stack seed survives Git's gc through an includeStacked merge and a discard". Both run `git gc --prune=now` between the steps, and both check the refs are gone after removal. On HEAD both failed with "fatal: bad object <oid>".
3. WP28 Remaining (minor, records). Confirmed from run 41133403420's job list and its shard 4/4 log (read 2026-10-31): unit (linux) passed, its "Run HttpApi exerciser gates" step included; engine linux 1–3 passed; shard 4/4 failed only the memory-sources pair. test.yml has no macOS runner. Remaining rewritten above. The round-1 timing note is marked resolved by CI5 cause 9.
4. I33's darwin case never ran (minor, coverage). Confirmed, and the mount-point failure was confirmed by reading `statProbe`. Fixed as above. The darwin key case had been skipped off macOS, although the probe stats the real volume whatever `platform` says. It now runs on every host against the host's own answer. A new case keys a missing file directly in the temp folder's volume root, so that root is the first folder probed. On HEAD's `file-lock.ts` the root case failed on Linux (got "ignores case"). A real APFS volume is still unverified, because no runner has one.
5. Approval after a Changes view (minor, coverage). Confirmed gap. New isolation case: an agent merge prompt is pending, the owner opens Review (diff, control, review), the approval merges, and the parent file is written. It passes on HEAD, as expected. With the `mergeKey`/`mergeable` exclusion removed (a temporary mutation), it failed with code `state`.
6. A link the merge failed to make (major). Confirmed by reading. `rm` followed by a failing `symlink` left the path absent, which matched neither the merge's write nor the earlier content, so recovery kept the deletion and called it a later change. The Windows separator mismatch was confirmed by reading Node's `preprocessSymlinkDestination`; it was not run, because nothing here is Windows. Fixed as above. Regression: "a merge's link that cannot be made leaves the parent's file in place". It makes `TaskIsolation.place` fail at the link call (a target with a NUL byte, refused by the call as Windows refuses an account without the link privilege). The parent file stays, and no temporary name is left. The old write was inline, so the case cannot run against HEAD. The Windows fold has no test here.
7. A start that failed before its checkout (minor). Confirmed: on HEAD, a stacking conflict left the record `failed` with the verify error after the save barrier ("Registered task worktree or reserved branch no longer matches Git."). Recovery then failed with the same error. Discard refused it as unsaved, and the note claimed a retained checkout. Fixed as above. Regressions (isolation): "an isolated start that fails before its checkout exists ends unchanged with its error through settle" and "… through reconcile". On HEAD, settle gave `failed` and reconcile failed.
8. A copy resume reuses the earlier baseline (minor). Confirmed: copying into the earlier `baseline/` failed on its link ("The copy destination is a link"), and the files deleted since stayed in it. Fixed: start drops earlier baselines, and with finding 9 the removal does too. Regression: "a copy resume after an unchanged save copies a fresh baseline, with its links". It plants an earlier baseline that holds a link and a deleted file before the resume. On HEAD it fails earlier, at finding 9's assertion. With only start's cleanup removed (a temporary mutation), it failed at the resume with "The copy destination is a link: …/baseline/link.txt".
9. Copy registries never shrink (minor, cost). Confirmed. Fixed as above. Not changed: an age-based prune of terminal registries, and `records()` reading every registry entry on each 5-minute pass. A terminal entry is now its record, patch and checkpoints. Regression: "copy merges keep only the baseline they use, and none once the task is merged", with three partial merges. On HEAD it failed with an extra `base-*`. The disk rule has no test, because it reads the real disk.
10. Another conversation's subagent title and ID in refusal text (minor, privacy). Confirmed by reading. `FileState.verify` and `OwnedPath.assertWritable` name the writer's or claim's title and ID from process-wide maps, and `PrivateAccess.output` matches only private root paths. Deferred, not fixed here. A correct fix needs a family relation that SessionAudience does not offer yet: whether the writer's session belongs to the caller's family. It also needs a decision on the generic wording of four refusals (stale write, ownership, protected tree, whole-tree git), which five test files assert. What leaks is a title and a session ID, not a path, patch or file content, and the HTTP counts projection is unaffected. Open for the safety review. Fixed in the P5F10 entry (2026-10-31).
11. Guest-provenance cost (minor, cost). Partly confirmed. `importLegacy` ran one SELECT per listed ID, plus a family walk for each conflict, inside BEGIN IMMEDIATE on every `register` and `fenceIsolation`. `guestIn` built a Set on every call. `PrivateAccess.output` asked `hasGuestInput` before anything else. The claim that `markGuest` imports does not hold: it runs no import. Fixed. An ID read as already marked, or found in a fenced family, is not read again; both marks only ever turn on. Once every listed ID is settled, an unchanged list costs nothing. IDs a call inserted are confirmed by a later read, because a refused `register` rolls its transaction and the import back. The parsed Set is cached with the list. `output` returns early without private roots, as `read` and `git` do. Regression (core audience): "legacy guest marks a refused registration imported are imported again by the next decision". It passes on HEAD. It failed under a mutation that settles inserted IDs at once (the rollback hazard). The saving itself has no timing test.
