## 2026-10-31 CI6 (the last three Windows engine shards) — status: the three red Windows shards in runs 41147540669 and 41156583300 diagnosed from their error output, one cause per shard; every failure attributed to its shard's cause from its own error output except six of shard 5's 91 (three before the readable log window, three whose output shows no sign of the cause), which are attributed by inference; fixed, each with a Linux regression or proof that failed on the old behaviour; the next full run, 41163614397, showed engine windows 2, 3 and 5 of 8 green, and every job that ran its tests passed (engine windows 8/8 stopped before its tests on a Chocolatey 504; Remaining)

CI evidence (job logs read on 2026-10-31; logs kept outside the repository):
- Run 41156583300 on `ee698eac` (Phase 5 round 2), completed 02:09 UTC, is the primary evidence.
  - Green: the desktop engine bundle under Node (job 124404601186); unit (linux) (124404601299), its "Check generated client" and "Run HttpApi exerciser gates" steps included; unit (windows) (124404601342); engine linux 1–4 (124404601287, 124404601387, 124404601340, 124404601398); engine windows 1/8 (124404601477), 4/8 (124404601430), 6/8 (124404601333), 7/8 (124404601351) and 8/8 (124404601350); e2e (linux) (124404601381); e2e (windows) (124404601324).
  - Red: engine windows 2/8 (124404601339): 1 fail, 1254 pass, 29 skip, 1284 tests across 63 files. 3/8 (124404601251): 1 fail, 633 pass, 3 skip, 637 tests across 62 files. 5/8 (124404601394): 91 fail, 444 pass, 11 skip, 546 tests across 62 files.
  - Windows "Run Engine shard" step times: 1/8 8.4 min, 2/8 13.6, 3/8 22.9, 4/8 13.8, 5/8 6.2 (cut short by its failures), 6/8 19.8, 7/8 17.9, 8/8 4.0. All are under CI5's 25-minute threshold (CI5 cause 11); 3/8 is the closest.
- Run 41147540669 on `f6f04e34` (CI5) was red on engine windows 1/8 (124376067754), 2/8 (124376067679) and 5/8 (124376067599), with the same three failures. Round 2's new `test/task/isolation-merge-ask.test.ts` moved every later file one shard on, so task.test.ts went from 1/8 to 2/8 and isolation.test.ts from 2/8 to 3/8. Because the same failures followed their files into new shards, they are not order effects.
- Not readable: the log API returns at most the last 5000 lines, and the run's log zip is refused by the proxy (403). Shard 5's first three failures, the tui thread cases, are older than that window in both runs, so their own error output was not read; their cause below is inferred. In run 41147540669 the window also starts after a fourth, "attach preserves InstanceRef from the current fiber context", whose output run 41156583300 shows with the `chdir` error.
- Each shard-5 failure's own output in run 41156583300 (88 of its 91 failures are inside the window): 84 show a `chdir` error naming the deleted folder, and one, auth-guest's "guest principals carry only configured workspace and session claims", expects that folder as its workspace directory. Three show neither, so their attribution to cause 1 is inference too:
  - auth-guest's "guests cannot run host commands or answer permission requests": the owner's pending permission list was still empty after its 15 s wait (auth-guest.test.ts:730);
  - auth-guest's "guests cannot allow tools in their own sessions": one pending permission expected, none received (auth-guest.test.ts:856);
  - HttpApi SDK's "routes configured SDK directory and workspace for v2 location GETs": "SDK file search index was not ready". Two files earlier in the shard, httpapi-file's "serves search endpoints" got a 500 with the `chdir` error in its output, so a file search that failed the same way out of sight is the likely cause; that was not shown.
  - No mechanism was traced for the two permission waits. Run 41147540669's shard 5/8 shows the same three with no sign of the leak.

Root causes and fixes:
1. Shard 5/8, 91 failures: a test file leaked `process.cwd` and `os.homedir` into every later file of its Bun process.
   - `test/cli/tui/editor-context.test.tsx` spied on `process.cwd` in five tests and on `os.homedir` in four, and never restored them. Bun keeps a spy for the rest of the process. So after the file, `process.cwd()` returned its last test's temp folder, which that test's `await using` had already deleted.
   - On Windows, cross-spawn's command resolution (`spawn` → `parseNonShell` → `resolveCommand`) changes into the spawn's cwd and then back into `process.cwd()`, and that second `chdir` failed. All 141 `chdir` errors in the readable part of run 41156583300's shard-5 log name the same folder, for example "ENOENT: no such file or directory, chdir '…\vector-test-9p9mm4xfgbe\' -> 'C:\Users\runneradmin\AppData\Local\Temp\vector-test-a4bm4q4gya'". In run 41147540669 they all name `vector-test-rdzahh8xh3c`.
   - Code that reads `process.cwd()` directly got the deleted folder too. auth-guest's "guest principals carry only configured workspace and session claims" expected `C:\Users\runneradmin\AppData\Local\Temp\vector-test-a4bm4q4gya` as the workspace directory and received undefined.
   - Why it showed only now: editor-context is file 101 of the 498 in the sorted list, so it is in shard 5/8, and the next file in that shard is file 109, `test/cli/tui/thread.test.ts`, which holds the first three failures in both runs. Under six shards (run 41133403420, on this branch's 497 files) the next file in its shard was `plugin-loader.test.ts`. That file's first test spies on `process.cwd` again through its `mockTuiRuntime` fixture and calls `mockRestore()` on it, which evidently put the original back: that run's shard 5/6 failed none of these cases (CI5 entry).
   - Fix (test only): the file's `afterEach` calls `mock.restore()` and puts each of the four editor variables back, deleting one that was unset at the start. Assigning `undefined` leaves the variable present under Bun (`"K" in process.env` stays true; probed here). A new last test, "the editor tests leave process.cwd, os.homedir and the editor variables as they found them", checks all of them against the values captured when the file loaded.
   - Against the old `afterEach`, the new test failed on Linux: `process.cwd()` was `/var/tmp/vector-engine/ci6/vector-test-tiuk07r24k`, a folder that no longer existed.
   - A scan of the engine tests found no other file with a spy it never restores (editor-context-zed restores its one spy by hand). `mock.restore()` restores every spy in the process; no later file relies on a leaked one.
2. Shard 3/8 (2/8 in run 41147540669): "unchanged removes the checkout, reserved branch and inherited trust". At isolation.test.ts's last trust check, `WorkspaceTrust.status(…).trusted` was true, expected false.
   - Inherited trust is stored under `canonicalize(checkout)`, the real path while the checkout exists. `TaskIsolation.remove` calls `WorkspaceTrust.forget` after `Worktree.remove`, as F.4 settle step 2 orders. `canonicalize` of a path that no longer exists fell back to `path.resolve`, which keeps the caller's spelling.
   - That spelling comes from `Global.Path.data`, which on the hosted runner is under `C:\Users\RUNNER~1\AppData\Local\Temp`: the shard-2 log shows `RUNNER~1\AppData\Local\Temp\vector-test-data-5060\…` for the test XDG folders. The stored key came from `fs.realpath`, which gives the long name `runneradmin` there: the engine's `tmpdir` fixture takes `fs.realpath` of a folder under the same temp folder, and the logs show those folders as `C:\Users\runneradmin\AppData\Local\Temp\vector-test-…`. The keys differ, so `forget` found nothing and the checkout's approval stayed. The same happens on any platform when the data folder is reached through a link, such as macOS `/var`.
   - Product bug, branch-only: `forget` is WP25's, and main has none.
   - Fix (core `workspace-trust.ts`): `canonicalize` resolves a missing path through its deepest existing ancestor, with the same `fs.realpath`, and re-attaches the rest. A removed workspace therefore keys as it did while it existed. Existing paths resolve as before. It uses `fs/promises` only (Node-compatible), and the removal order is unchanged.
   - Regression (core `test/workspace-trust/inherit.test.ts`): "forget revokes a removed workspace through the link its path names, however much of it is gone". Two copies inherit the parent's approval under a linked data folder, one directly in it and one a level deeper. The first is removed and forgotten through the link; then the second's parent folder is removed and it is forgotten. Each is revoked in turn, and the parent stays trusted. The link is a junction on Windows and is unlinked before the folder is disposed (CI5 cause 3).
   - On HEAD's source the regression failed at the first revocation (still trusted). Under a temporary mutation that climbs one level only, the first part passed and the deeper revocation failed.
   - Side effect, recorded: a snapshot or unavailable snapshot of a missing folder now names it through its resolved ancestor, so its workspace and fingerprint change when an ancestor is a link. An approval stored under the old spelling of such a missing path can no longer be reached, which fails closed (untrusted).
3. Shard 2/8 (1/8 in run 41147540669): "task isolation saves > TaskManager.resume runs an isolated child in its worktree, saves before completion, and a Stop never waits on Git" failed at its home check: home's `notes.txt` was "child\n".
   - The first launch's ops were `stubOps({ onPrompt: edit })`. TaskNotify delivers the parent's completion note through the ops of the launch it reports on, after the quiet window (300 ms in this file). `TaskPlace.set` gives the parent the place `{ work: home }`, so `edit` wrote "child\n" into the parent's home.
   - On Linux the resume bumped the attempt before the note went out, and `claimNotification` refused the stale attempt-1 note. On Windows the save and the resume's start outlasted the quiet window, so the note came first.
   - Proof, before any fix: with only a wait for the parent's first prompt added before the resume (the old ops, wrapped to record the parent's prompts), the case failed on Linux exactly as on Windows. Every earlier assertion passed, and the home check got "child\n".
   - Fix (test only): each of the case's three ops answers the parent's turns with "noted" and leaves the child's behaviour as it was, and the case now waits for the first note ("the parent never heard back", 5 s, as the sibling note case does) before resuming. Every host therefore takes the order Windows took.
   - The shared `edit` stub is unchanged. A guard there that skipped writes at home would also hide a regression that ran the isolated child at home, because this case's home check is the only one among `edit`'s three users.

Contract corrections: none to the spec's wording.
- Product fix within WP25's contract: `forget` after a removal meets the key stored while the checkout existed. F.4's order (unlink the links, `Worktree.remove`, then `WorkspaceTrust.forget`) is kept.
- The wait made robust, with its reason: the TaskManager.resume case waits for the parent's first completion note (5 s) before it resumes, so it no longer depends on which comes first. It waits on the real note; no assertion was removed or loosened, and the `ISOLATED` waits are unchanged.
- Test only: editor-context restores its spies and editor variables and checks that it did.

Main (`78526052`): editor-context.test.tsx is identical there, so the leak is present but latent. Main's Windows engine job runs six shards, and main's sorted file list puts `plugin-loader.test.ts`, whose fixture restores `process.cwd`, right after editor-context in shard 3/6. It would surface under the eight-shard layout this branch brings; the CI6 fix travels with the 7.4 integration. `forget` and its fix are branch-only.

## 2026-10-31 P5F10 (refusals name only the caller's family) — status: Phase 5 round 2's finding 10 fixed in source, and the three findings of this change's review fixed (the launch and resume overlap refusal, the fork-to-source naming, the WP28 record), and the second review's one finding fixed (the resume's untested copy of the fail-closed catch, now one shared function with a regression of its own); each new regression failed on the old source or under its mutation and passes with the fix; every changed file run here except task-manage.test.ts (left to CI: it starts a Bun subprocess), and the core, engine and server typechecks green (Linux); committed; the full run on these changes, 41171765221, green on all 17 jobs

The leak, confirmed on HEAD's source (`06762768`) before any change: `FileState.verify` named the last writer from the process-wide writer map, and `OwnedPath.assertWritable` named a claim's title, task ID and owned path, a protected tree's task ID and every whole-tree writer's title, with no session relation checked. A guest's conversation in the same folder as an owner-only family was therefore told that family's subagent title and session ID (the regression below shows it). `PrivateAccess.output` matches only private root paths, so it never stepped in. Coordinator decision on the wording: the caller's own family keeps today's text; anyone else gets a generic phrase with no title and no session ID; when the relation cannot be determined, the generic phrase.

Contract corrections (intent-preserving):
- F.1, the `FileState.verify` matrix ("deleted" and "present, differs"). The other session is named as before ("the subagent "<title>" (<id>)", or "another session (<id>)" without a title) only when it is in the caller's conversation family. Otherwise the texts read "<file> was deleted by another conversation after you read it. Nothing was written." and "<file> changed since you last read it, written by another conversation. Nothing was written; read it again first." The "outside your edits" text, `unread` and `exists` are unchanged.
- F.2 refusal texts. Owned and changed keep today's texts when the claim's launcher is in the caller's family. Otherwise: "<file> belongs to another conversation's running subagent. It was not changed." and "<file> was changed by another conversation's running subagent and stays its file until it finishes. It was not changed.", with no title, task or session ID and no owned path. A helper is still told "Report the change you need to the parent."; a root is told "Wait for it to finish.", because task_manage reaches only its own conversation's subagents. That narrows R10's "wait, message or stop" to "wait" for this case.
- F.2 tree text. Today's text when every writer's launcher is in the caller's family. Otherwise the family's titles stay and the rest are counted: "This git command changes the whole working tree while N subagents are editing it ("A", K outside this conversation). It was not run. Wait for them to finish." The count was already shown; no other conversation's title or ID is.
- F.2 item 3, protected trees (no text was specified): "<file> is protected for subagent <taskID>. It was not changed." for the task's own family, otherwise "<file> is protected for another conversation's subagent. It was not changed."
- New core primitive `SessionAudience.inFamily(sessionID, member)`. It is one `EXISTS` over the existing indexed `family()` walk, in a third form of it, `lineage`, beside `fence` and `guest`: parent and child edges in both directions (siblings through a parent, IDs left after deletion included), and no fork edge in either direction. A source never shows its fork's later messages, and a fork shows its source only up to the fork point, which no lineage edge records, so neither is named the other's subagents. The first version used the guest-input form (a fork's source included); this change's review showed that it named to a fork, possibly a guest's, the subagents its source started after the fork, which the fork never shows (review finding 3 below). The plan's fence form (every edge both ways) would also name a fork's work to its source. The fence and the guest-input walk are unchanged: the fence still covers forks in both directions. An unknown member is not in any lineage. The relation never grants anything.
- New engine hook `Tool.Context.inFamily(sessionID)`. Only `SessionTools.resolve` supplies it, bound to the calling session, in the tool context and in both chokepoint contexts (the pre-ask check and `ctx.assertWritable`), so edit, write, apply_patch, the shell's detected writes and task_manage merge all carry it. It is `TaskClaims.familyOf(audience, sessionID)`, which the `TaskManager.resume` refusal also uses: a defect reading the relation is logged ("the conversation family could not be read") and answers false. Any context without the hook gets the generic texts. `FileState.verify` takes `inFamily` as a required key that may be undefined, so edit, write and apply_patch each pass `ctx.inFamily` explicitly.
- What each check is keyed on: a writer by its `sessionID`; a claim by its `parentSessionID`, the durable launcher, which is in the holder's family by construction and is known while the child is still "starting"; a protection by its `taskID`.
- Behaviour change by design: the owner's other conversations in the same folder (another family), and a fork and its source, now get the generic texts for each other's subagents. F.2's "two chats in one folder see each other's claims" still holds for the refusal itself; only the naming changed. Refusal texts already stored in history keep their names; there is no retroactive scrub, as with the transfer policy's "no retroactive erasure".
- WP22 test list ("another session writing between read and edit is refused, naming it"): naming now holds within the caller's family; edit.test.ts proves both forms.

Source: `packages/core/src/session/audience.ts` (`inFamily` and the walk's `lineage` form); engine `tool/tool.ts` (the hook), `session/tools.ts` (the hook, from `TaskClaims.familyOf`), `tool/file-state.ts` (`verify` names the writer only through the hook), `tool/edit.ts`, `tool/write.ts` and `tool/apply_patch.ts` (pass `ctx.inFamily`), `tool/owned-path.ts` (the protected, claim and whole-tree refusals), `task/claims.ts` (`overlaps` returns its pairs, `describe` words them, `familyOf` is the one fail-closed binding of the relation), `tool/task.ts` (the launch refusal, through `ctx.inFamily`) and `task/manager.ts` (the resume refusal, through `TaskClaims.familyOf` over `SessionAudience` itself, now one of its dependencies: `TaskIsolation` already depended on it, so the graph does not grow). The relation is read only on a refusal path (also on the recheck inside the file lock): one indexed read, no write transaction. Effect code and one SQL query, no Bun API, so the engine runtime stays Node-compatible. No public Protocol or HttpApi change (`Tool.Context` and `SessionAudience.Interface` are internal), so no generator.

Tests:
- `session-tools-writable.test.ts`: the old first case is unchanged and stays named (its claim's launcher is the caller). New: "a guest next to an owner-only family is refused without that family's subagent, session or paths, and the family still sees them". It uses real sessions: the owner's conversation, its child fenced with no directory (so no private root and PrivateAccess has nothing to refuse), and a guest's root in the same folder; the child holds `vault` and wrote `notes.md` after both conversations read it. Through the real SessionTools chokepoint, a probe's edit ask and the real edit, write and apply_patch tools (from `ToolRegistry.all()`) give the guest the exact generic texts, with no title, either session ID or "which owns". The owner's conversation gets today's exact texts with the neutralized title `"Rotate the <\system> keys"`. No permission is asked and the file is unchanged. New: "a family that cannot be read names no other conversation, even to the owner's own": an audience whose `inFamily` dies gives the owner the generic texts, without the defect's message.
- `file-state.test.ts`, `edit.test.ts`, `owned-path.test.ts` and `shell-ownership.test.ts` use a real `SessionAudience.make` over an in-memory database of their own; shell-ownership opens one for the file and registers nothing, so its main agent's family is its own conversation. Each still proves the refusal. file-state: the deleted and changed cases give the exact named text to the helper's parent, and the exact generic text to an unrelated conversation that read the file and to a caller without the hook; new case: an untitled writer in the family is "another session (<id>)". edit: a neighbour conversation is refused generically and the parent with the named text, and the file stays at the helper's write each time. owned-path: the owned and changed cases keep their named texts through the hook, and add the generic texts for a neighbour (owned, root ending) and for a caller without the hook (changed, helper ending); new protected-tree case, named to the task's parent and generic to a neighbour. shell-ownership: the named assertions run through the main agent's hook. The `git -C` case's claim, which belongs to `ses_other_root`, now expects the generic text without "Owner" or its IDs. New case "another conversation's shared-tree writers are counted but not named" covers ("A", 1 outside this conversation), and without the hook (2 outside this conversation).
- `core/test/session/audience.test.ts`: new "inFamily is a conversation's own lineage both ways, never across a fork in either direction nor to a guest next to it" (true for itself, ancestor, descendant, sibling, a child started after the fork from the source's side, and a fork with its own helper both ways; false for fork-to-source, including that later child, a fork's helper to the source's lineage, source-to-fork, a guest's root next to the fenced family and an unknown ID; the fork stays fenced). Before the review it expected fork-to-source true. The deletion case adds that the relation still walks the captured lineage through the deleted middle session.

Review of this change (2026-10-31; three findings, each checked against the code, all fixed):
1. The task-launch and resume overlap refusal still named another family's claims (major, privacy). Confirmed: `TaskClaims.overlaps` built `<title>: <owned> overlaps <existing>` for every active, writing, shared-tree claim outside the launcher's lineage, another family's included, so a guest launching with `owned_paths: ["."]` next to an owner-only family was told its subagent title, owned paths and the files it changed. The first version recorded this as observed and not changed; it is the same class of refusal as the four above, and the coordinator's rule (no path may leak) covers it, so it belongs to this package. Fixed: `overlaps` returns its pairs (the claim, the caller's own path, the claim's path or changed file), and `TaskClaims.describe` words them, keyed on each claim's `parentSessionID`, reading the relation once per launcher. A claim whose launcher is in the caller's family keeps today's line; any other gives only the caller's own path: "<owned> overlaps another conversation's running subagent", once however many of that conversation's paths or files overlap, with no title, path, file or ID. Without the relation nothing is named. The launch passes `ctx.inFamily`; `TaskManager.resume`, which the app and task_manage both reach, asks `SessionAudience.inFamily(root, launcher)` with the same fail-closed catch as SessionTools. Contract correction, D.3 and the launch rule: both refusals' headers and closing lines are unchanged; only another conversation's lines changed. The closing advice ("add it to depends_on", "stop it first") still fits a line of the caller's own family; for another conversation's line only waiting or other paths apply, and the line itself says it is another conversation's. The resume's within-batch lines name only the root's own children, so they are unchanged.
2. The WP28 status line and the WP28 Remaining CI item kept sentences that said the Windows passes were inferred only and that the six shard-5 cases were the next to check, beside the fifth run's green shards (minor, records). Confirmed by reading, and run 41163614397's job list read again here: engine windows 1 to 7 of 8 `success`, 8/8 `failure` at "Install ripgrep" with "Run Engine shard" `skipped`, 16 of 17 jobs `success`. Fixed: both now say that run shows the fixes on Windows, that only the six cases' original cause stays an inference, and that 8/8's files did not run in it.
3. `inFamily` named to a fork the subagents its source started after the fork (minor, privacy). Confirmed: the guest-input walk followed a fork's edge up to its source and then every child of the source, with no notion of the fork point, and the first core test expected `ses_fork` to `ses_child` true. A guest can hold such a fork, since `markGuest` refuses only an owner-only family. Fixed as the core primitive bullet above says: the walk's `lineage` form follows no fork edge either way, so a fork and its source each get the generic texts for the other's subagents. That also drops naming a source's pre-fork subagents to the fork, which the fork does show; failing closed there is the coordinator's rule when the relation cannot be determined, and the lineage does not record the fork point.

Owner question (raised, not decided here). Main's terminal tool (1.99.112) is allowed for every agent, subagents included. `agent.ts` defaults `terminal` and `terminal_input` to allow, `deriveSubagentSessionPermission` does not deny them, and `own()` treats the parent's terminals as the helper's own. So a helper can start a process that outlives the tool call and the helper itself. Spec 5.H refuses background commands to subagent sessions. Refusing the terminal to subagents would remove a capability main shipped; keeping it departs from 5.H. Until the owner answers, main's behaviour is kept. The Stop-including-helpers semantics for terminals stay with WP33. Round B adds one case the answer should cover (MAIN Round B entry): an isolated helper's terminals are in its checkout's location, which the app's terminal panel does not show, and they are closed when its checkout is saved, while main's terminal text (terminal.txt, `TERMINAL_POLICY`) tells every agent that its terminals appear in the user's panel and may stay open for a dev server the user is testing. Only the isolation brief now says otherwise, to isolated helpers; main's text for every other session is unchanged.

Found by reading code, for Round B (not fixed in the merge):
- Location services, and with them a checkout's PTYs, live in core's global `LayerMap` with `idleTimeToLive: "60 minutes"`. Nothing invalidates an entry when an isolated checkout is settled, merged or removed. `Pty`'s finalizer kills its processes only when that layer is released. So an isolated helper's terminal can outlive its checkout's retirement, against handoff 7.3 ("nothing may enter a checkout being retired"). On Windows its working directory can also block the removal. Not yet shown with a running case.
- In terminal `open` and `type`, the `unsandboxed_shell` ask comes before the guest guards.

## 2026-10-31 MAIN step 11 (terminal admission) — status: the terminal's command lines and typed lines now take the shell's file-lock admission and become the session's changes, once per call and only after every line's check passes; five SessionTools-driven regressions, each failing under its mutation (sha256-checked) and passing with the fix; the engine typecheck and every changed file's tests green here (Linux), terminal.test.ts's Bun cases left to CI; committed as `aa2cc01a`, after the merge commit

Change (engine `tool/shell.ts`, `tool/terminal.ts`):
- `ShellPermission.admit(ctx, scans)`, shared by the shell and the terminal. It takes one `FileLock.withFiles` over the union of every scan's `written` and `mutates`, runs `assertOwnership` on the union inside the locks (files, recursive targets and git mutations of every scan), and only after that passes calls `TaskClaims.touch` for each file. `assertOwnership` now takes a list of scans; `ask()` passes its one scan, so the pre-ask check (F.3) is unchanged.
- The shell tool calls `admit(ctx, [scan])` where it had its own copy of the same admission. Same locks, same check, same touches: no behaviour change.
- Terminal `open`: after the asks and the `shell.env` hook, immediately before `pty.create`. The files its command line writes become the session's changes before the program starts, as the shell's do before it spawns.
- Terminal `type` and `keys`: every line the strokes submit is asked first (each with its own pre-ask check), then one admission over all of them, before the pending line is updated and before the first `pty.write`. A line refused at its ask or at the admission refuses the whole call: nothing is typed and no file is recorded, so a refused second line leaves no claim on a file the first line names. Admission per line would have recorded the first line's file and then refused the second.
- No Bun API: Effect, `FileLock` and `TaskClaims` only, so the engine runtime stays Node-compatible. No public Protocol or HttpApi change, so no generator.

Contract corrections (intent kept):
- F.2/F.3 for the terminal: the MAIN entry's "What the terminal did not get in the merge commit" is done. A terminal call's lines are one admission, as a shell command is one.
- What admission records is what the scan resolves. A line Vector cannot read (recalled or completed text, keys, a foreign program's input) has no resolved targets, so it records nothing, and writes a terminal's process makes after the call returns are not seen (R10 / F.3 "Not seen", unchanged from the MAIN entry). A recorded file stays the helper's change until its claim settles, as a shell write does.

Tests (new `engine/test/session/terminal-admission.test.ts`; the real terminal tool through `SessionTools.resolve` with the real audience, claims, chokepoint and guest guards; every ask recorded and allowed; terminals run `/bin/sh`; gated off Windows, where terminals do not start in tests, as terminal.test.ts's `ptyTest` is; no Bun subprocess). "Nothing typed" is shown by typing `echo done` after the refusal, waiting for its output and reading the whole terminal: a line typed before it would have been echoed and run first.
1. "a guest next to an owner-only family is refused that family's files without its names, before any terminal or keystroke; the owner is told which subagent". The owner's conversation has two running helpers, one fenced owner-only: one changed `notes.md`, the other owns `vault`; the guest's root works in the same folder. For the guest and then the owner: `open` with `printf x > notes.md` and with `printf x > vault/new.ts` is refused with no ask, and the owner's list shows no terminal; in an open `/bin/sh`, typing each line is refused with only the `terminal_input` ask, and the shell never sees it. The guest's four refusals are the exact P5F10 generic texts, without either title, any session ID or "which owns"; the owner's are the exact named texts. `notes.md` is unchanged and `vault/new.ts` absent.
2. "a typed line that writes a file makes it the helper's change". An unscoped helper types `printf report > out.txt; echo written` into its terminal; the file is written and `TaskClaims.conflicts` from the parent names the helper; the parent's own terminal `open` writing `out.txt` is refused with "was changed by the running subagent "Write the report" (<id>)", and the file keeps the helper's bytes.
3. "a two-line type whose second line writes a sibling's file types nothing and claims nothing for the first". `printf a > first.txt` then `printf b > vault/x.ts` (a sibling owns `vault`): refused with the named text and "Report the change you need to the parent."; nothing typed, `first.txt` absent and claimed by no one.
4. "a claim made while a call's lines are asked for refuses the whole call at admission". The sibling claims `second.txt` inside the ask for the second line's edit, after that line's pre-ask check passed: the admission's check inside the locks refuses the call with the named text; nothing typed, neither file exists, and `first.txt` is claimed by no one.
5. "a guest family's terminal neither opens in a private task folder nor types git worktree list beside it; an owner's does both". A real linked worktree of the project repository, fenced (checkout and its Git metadata), reached through an `alias` link so the arguments never spell it: the guest's `open` there is refused ("This operation could expose private task data.") with no ask and no terminal; in the guest's own shell in the project, typing `git worktree list` is refused with only the `terminal_input` ask, without the private path, and the owner's full read of that terminal shows the command never ran. The owner opens a terminal through the link and its `git worktree list` prints the private checkout.

## 2026-10-31 MAIN Round B (main × multi-agent) — status: two findings fixed in source, each with a regression that fails on the old source or under its mutation (sha256-checked) and passes with the fix; three items reviewed with no change; the review of this entry found three more, all fixed (Review of this entry, below); the engine typecheck and every changed file's tests green here (Linux); committed: the round as `05c60fea`, after the merge commit, step 11 and Round A, and its review's three fixes in the commit after it (MAIN commit gate entry below)

Round B reads main's features where they meet multi-agent code. The MAIN entry's Round B list had five items.

1. Terminal lifetime (fixed). A terminal belongs to its location's services, which core's global `LayerMap` keeps for an hour after their last use, so an isolated helper's terminal outlived its checkout's save and retirement and could write into a checkout being saved or retired (handoff 7.3). Shown on the old source by the regression below: after the save, both of the helper's programs were still running and still writing.
   - `TaskIsolation.quiesce`, the cancel-and-join step before every snapshot, disposal and retirement (`settle` before its snapshot, `retire` before it disposes the instance), now also closes the checkout's terminals. After the runs and jobs it cancels, so no family run opens a new one, it lists the terminals of the checkout's location and removes each (`Pty.remove`, which publishes the deletion to the checkout's folder; the app's terminal panel follows the open project's folder and never listed these terminals), then waits for each program to end. A program that ignores the close's hangup or terminate is killed with its process group (`SIGKILL`) after a second on Linux and macOS; on Windows the close already ends the console's processes. After two more seconds a still-running program is logged and the save goes on. Waiting probes with signal 0, which sends nothing.
   - A location nothing has used is not started for this (`RcMap.has` first), so a checkout with no terminal gets no watchers or plugins. Location services that failed to start have no terminal service; that is logged and nothing is closed.
   - The map is the one the registry's terminal tool opens terminals in: `TaskIsolation.node` now depends on core's shared `locationServiceMapLayer`, which the shared memo map builds once, as the registry's own node does. A separately built map (mutation B3 below) finds no terminals.
   - No Bun API: `process.kill` and Effect only, so the engine runtime stays Node-compatible. No public Protocol or HttpApi change.
2. Ask order (fixed). In terminal `open` and `type`, the `unsandboxed_shell` ask (with `VECTOR_SHELL_SANDBOX` on) came before `ShellPermission`'s guest guards and the pre-ask ownership check, so a refused line still asked to run unsandboxed. `ShellPermission.ask` now takes an optional `first` ask, made after the guest guards and the ownership check and before the shell's own asks; the terminal passes its `unsandboxed_shell` ask there. An admitted line asks it first, as before, so terminal.test.ts's pinned order is unchanged. The shell tool passes none: its own `unsandboxed_shell` ask stays where it was, at spawn, when the sandbox cannot be applied.
3. `FreeModelGuests.marked` in the terminal's own-terminal filter (reviewed, no change). It reads `SessionAudience.hasGuestInput` first, the durable guest input WP28 persists before admission, then the in-memory and saved marks over the lineage, so a restart or an owner runner processing queued guest input keeps the filter on.
4. Run Guard loop rules for a helper's terminal polling (decided: kept as is). The doom-loop ask exempts terminal polling (`POLLING_ACTIONS`); SpendGuard's loop rule does not, and a helper's step is checked on its launch run (WP4: "Loop detection belongs to the launch run"), so three polls with the same input and the same output, with nothing new and no file edit between them, pause the whole run. Kept because:
   - only identical input and identical output count, and a polled program whose output moves on changes both (the cursor, and the text a `read` or `wait` returns), so polling a program that makes progress never trips it;
   - three identical answers from an idle program (each `wait` blocks up to 60 s) are paid steps with no progress, which the Run Guard exists to stop, and its stop is a pause the owner resumes, not a loss of work;
   - exempting terminal polling would let a helper spend the run's budget waiting on a hung program, and main applies the same rule to the root session's own polling.
5. Helpers' per-call models under free-model guest routes and Vector Local (reviewed, no change). In `SubagentModel.choose`:
   - On a free route (the parent's step on a free model), a brief that names another model is refused ("You run on a free model, and your subagents stay on it."). An agent's pinned model and the small-model routing are skipped, so every child stays on the parent's free model; research agents take the same route.
   - With a guest in the tree (`guests.marked(root.id)`, durable as in item 3), a named model must be strictly cheaper, and a model that may train on prompts is refused. An agent's own pinned model is the owner's configuration and is not narrowed (5.E.1, unchanged by the merge).
   - Under Vector Local, a named model must be on the parent's provider. The provider refuses any model whose requests would leave the listed model servers (off in Vector Local) before a stream starts, an agent's pinned model included. Web access None governs tools' network access, not inference, and the terminal and shell refusals under it apply to helpers' calls as to the root's.

Contract corrections (intent kept):
- WP27 / handoff 7.3 "stopping/joining descendants before snapshot/dispose/remove/dropWork" now also covers the checkout's terminals: closed and waited for before the snapshot, as runs and jobs are cancelled and joined. Not seen, as for the shell tool (not an OS sandbox): a process a terminal's program starts outside its process group, or detached from the terminal (`nohup`, `setsid`, a job-control shell's background jobs that ignore the hangup). A terminal another location opened with its starting folder inside the checkout is not closed; only the owner's conversation can open one there (the guest guards refuse a guest family), and it stays the owner's.
- F.3 and the MAIN entry's terminal ask order: the terminal's `unsandboxed_shell` ask now comes after every refusal (guest guards, ownership check) and before the shell's asks.
- Terminals for subagents (spec 5.H) stays an owner question (MAIN entry). The fix ends an isolated helper's terminals when its checkout is saved; a helper that works in the parent's folder uses the parent's location, whose terminals the parent's conversation keeps.
- Main's terminal text (terminal.txt lines 1 and 21, `TERMINAL_POLICY` in session/system.ts) tells every agent with the terminal tool that its terminals appear in the user's terminal panel and that it may leave one open for the user, such as a dev server they are testing. For an isolated helper neither holds: its terminals are in its checkout's location (the terminal tool keys `Pty` by the instance's folder, and TaskPlace runs its calls in the checkout), the app's panel listens to the open project's folder only (app `context/terminal.tsx`, `server-sdk.tsx`), so the terminals and a `reveal` never reach it, and `quiesce` closes them when the checkout is saved. The isolation brief (`TaskIsolation.brief`, and the same text in `start`'s result) now tells the helper so: its terminals are not in the user's panel, revealing one shows nothing, they end when its work is saved at the end of its run, and it must not leave a program running for anyone or report one as running or visible. It goes in the brief, not in `TERMINAL_POLICY`, because that policy is part of the prompt's `stable` part, the same for every session of an agent on a model; main's text for non-isolated sessions is unchanged. Next to the owner question in the MAIN entry.

Real Git, instances, sessions, isolation and terminals, the terminal tool through `SessionTools.resolve` from the registry, with the helper's calls in its checkout's instance (as TaskPlace routes them); no model. The helper opens two `/bin/sh` counters in its checkout, one deaf to hangup and terminate (`trap '' HUP TERM`); the parent opens one at home. With each counted at least three times and all three running, `settle`: the state is `changed`; both of the helper's programs have ended when it returns and the parent's still runs; neither file changes over the next 400 ms; the saved commit holds each file's last bytes; the checkout's location lists no terminal and the parent's still lists its own. Gated off Windows, as terminal.test.ts's `ptyTest` is. A finalizer added before the first open kills, with its process group, every counter whose open has returned and that still runs, skipping a result with no pid, so a run that fails after an open has returned leaves no counter behind. Not covered: an open that fails after its program has started returns no pid, so the finalizer cannot kill that counter.
- `engine/test/session/terminal-admission.test.ts`, a sixth case: "with the shell sandbox on, a line the guest guard or the ownership check refuses is never asked to run unsandboxed". With `VECTOR_SHELL_SANDBOX=1`: a guest's `open` in a link to a fenced folder is refused with no ask; a guest's typed `cat < alias/notes.txt` is refused with only the `terminal_input` ask, and the shell never sees it; a helper's `open` and typed line that write a sibling's `vault` file are refused with no ask and with only `terminal_input`; an admitted terminal's first ask is `unsandboxed_shell`, once; an admitted typed line asks `terminal_input`, then `unsandboxed_shell`, then the shell's asks. The link and the redirect keep the private path out of the arguments, so ShellPermission's read guard, not the argument check, is what refuses.
- `engine/test/task/isolation.test.ts`: its layer now provides the shared location services, which its direct `TaskIsolation.make` runs need. No case changed.

A3, `first` asked before the ownership check in `ask()`: fails at the helper's `open`, after the guest checks pass.

Otherwise both build the same `locationServiceMapLayer` through the shared memo map.

Review of this entry (three findings, each checked against the code; all fixed, none deferred):
- R1, an isolated helper is told the opposite of what happens to its terminals (contract). Confirmed by reading: the terminal tool keys `Pty` by `instance.directory`, which for an isolated helper is its checkout; the app's terminal panel subscribes to the open project's folder (`createDirSdkContext` filters the event stream by folder), so the checkout's `pty.created`, `pty.deleted` and `pty.revealed` never reach it; `quiesce` closes the terminals at the save; and terminal.txt and `TERMINAL_POLICY`, offered to isolated helpers too, promise the panel and a server left running. Fixed in the brief, recorded in the contract corrections above and next to the owner question in the MAIN entry; item 1's sentence on `Pty.remove` corrected. Regression: tool/task.test.ts's "an isolated child keeps its home row, gets the brief section and returns Vector's counts line" now also checks that the child's first prompt says its terminals are not in the user's panel and that it must not leave a program running or report one as running. With the sentence removed from `TaskIsolation.brief` (sha256-checked restore), that case fails at the new check.
- R2, no pid guard before the terminal-close escalation (safety). Confirmed: `ended` probed `process.kill(pid, 0)` and then sent `SIGKILL` to `-pid` with no check; core's `Pty.create` stores the library's `proc.pid` unchecked, the schema's `Pty.Info.pid` admits 0, and bun-pty's `_pid` is -1 until `bun_pty_get_pid` sets it, unchecked. Under Node here, `process.kill(0, 0)` and `process.kill(-1, 0)` both succeed, so either pid would read as alive for the whole second and then be killed: the engine's own process group for 0, every process the user may signal for -1. Fixed: `ended` returns with a logged warning unless the pid is a positive integer; `alive` and `kill` are reached only through it. No test: `ended` is module-private and only a fake terminal service could report such a pid; the guard's reason is in the source comment.
- R3, the regression's finalizer could miss the deaf counter (test hygiene). Confirmed by a run: with a failure injected right after the deaf counter's open, the old test (finalizer added after all three opens) failed and left `/bin/sh -c trap '' HUP TERM; ...deaf.txt...` running, found by its command line and killed by process group; the fixed test under the same injection failed and left none. Fixed: one finalizer, added before the first open, walks the opens recorded as each returns, skips a result with no pid, and tolerates a program that ends between its probe and the kill. The Tests bullet above now says what it covers and what it does not.

## 2026-10-31 CI7 — Windows whole-file shard overload after main integration

The full run on `b5af4269`, [41185841620](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/41185841620), completed with 16 of 17 jobs successful. Windows engine shard 7/8 (job `124492484269`) reached the **30-minute step deadline**, with no failed test assertion in its log. Its last passing case, "a background result waits out a pending revert instead of committing it", finished less than one second before the runner stopped the step. This supersedes the previous entry's statement that the push and full run were still pending; the integration was pushed and the run did occur, but it did not pass.

Diagnosis uses the logs of all eight Windows engine jobs. Summed reported test durations, in shard order, were 7.4, 8.9, 13.6, 9.5, 16.1, 11.4, at least 30.0, and 10.4 minutes (setup/file overhead is additional). `test/task/isolation.test.ts` alone took 16.04 minutes in shard 7; `test/tool/task.test.ts` started only about 2 minutes 25 seconds before the step deadline and continued passing until it was cut off. This is cumulative work imbalance, not evidence of a deadlock in that last passing task case.

Repair: distribute Windows engine files over twelve Bun shards instead of eight, with the existing 30-minute step deadline, 30-second per-test deadline, package cwd, preloads and assertions unchanged. No test is skipped or made less strict. A rough redistribution of the logged file durations puts the busiest twelve-way group near 24 minutes, versus nearly 29 minutes for ten groups. That estimate is incomplete because the timed-out job did not reach every file, and future file additions change Bun's assignment; only a fresh complete native run establishes the result. Linux sharding stays unchanged.

Validation: a static check confirms each Windows matrix shard 1–12 appears exactly once, the displayed and executed denominator is 12, and both deadlines remain unchanged; `git diff --check` passes. The combined branch's workflow compliance file passes all 22 tests; no test was skipped. The mandatory pre-push hook checks all packages before publication. ## 2026-11-01 WP29 — pure workflow core and schema

Implemented the v1 grammar, strict unknown-field validation with nearby-key hints, size and depth checks, supported output-schema checks, template scopes and data envelopes, fixed predicates, DAG/nested expansion, stable content keys, limit accounting, durable progress projection and bounded completion reports. Added browser-safe public Workflow contracts, approval policy compatibility, the schema generator and generated `workflow.json`, website retention and route smoke-check coverage. The guide's three complete examples are exercised directly by the parser tests. Runtime execution, owner-start admission and tool/routes remain WP30–WP32.

Contract clarifications from independent review (recorded in §5.G):
- `constructor` and `prototype` are reserved stage/input names because template traversal refuses them. Nested input references must name an actual outer input, even when the child input is optional; aggregate condition paths end exactly in `done`, `failed` or `items`.
- Graph dependencies and completion barriers are separate. A `from` edge participates in DAG validation but can stream; explicit `after`, aggregate conditions and stage-output templates add completion barriers. WP30 handles `wait: all` and sorting as source buffering.
- Approval metadata includes verifier asks and write placement. A writable verifier with no ownership or worktree marks the stage serial even when its producer is read-only; WP30 must use the same writer gate.
- A nested saved workflow retains its own resolved limits in `groupLimits`, and each expanded stage names its group. Parent and group limits apply together; unrelated outer stages do not inherit a child's budget. WP30 cannot launch nested work until group accounting is enforced, including resume and verifier calls. Nested result-stage references are validated at their child path, and `groupResults` retains namespaced stage selection, filter and max-items for WP30 group output. This resolves how nested limits combine; they are never silently discarded or used to raise a parent limit.
- Public node errors/previews and reports validate UTF-8 byte caps, not only character counts. The report caps the total at 64 KiB, omits excess stage summaries with a count, keeps the actual engine artifact path intact, and never cuts a data envelope midway. Node-only planning uses Node crypto/path; browser surfaces consume the public wire schemas. Text byte counts use TextEncoder.
- The public Plan shape remains §4.5. WP31's approval presentation still must show the shared delegated ceiling in addition to the run USD budget; WP29 does not claim to implement that engine-side presentation.

Evidence (2026-11-01, Linux; one file at a time through `/workspace/.tools/vector-serial`, package cwd, `--timeout 30000`):
- core: spec 8, template 4, predicate 2, plan 10, limits 3, progress 1, report 4, schema-file 1 — 33 passing cases;
- schema: workflow 5 and permission-policy 6 — 11 passing cases;
- web: deployed-schemas 2 passing cases; the first run could not bind loopback under the sandbox, and rerunning with local network permission passed;
- core and schema typechecks passed; the final core check ran after review fixes, including the targeted nested-result regression rerun; generated workflow.json equals the generator output; diff-check passed.

Permission compatibility uses the actual shipped 1.99.111 policy from `ee6b9a4100bf22113f7edc2a24cd7f3d16d0513b`, identified by successful release run 40236084221. The test fixture is the source file plus a provenance comment. All elevated workflow reasons refuse auto and Always approval under both policies.

Review: workflow_core checked graph/template/input limits; codium_review checked permissions, wire schemas, publication and reports. Findings above were fixed with regressions. This entry records local WP29 evidence, not a publication or full-suite result. Full general CI 41255692359 is running on the preceding integration/CI7 commit 6d34acd7; its result is recorded separately by the coordinating agent.

## 2026-11-01 WP30 — durable workflow runtime

Implemented local/Git input sources, trusted saved-workflow loading, task-part launch persistence, FIFO streaming scheduling, shared root writer gates, verify/repair loops, nested group budgets, durable resume and bounded completion artifacts. `WorkflowRunner.run` loads the engine adapter lazily and accepts the existing task definition and prompt operations; it never calls a provider itself. Workflow tool registration, hidden-agent admission, marker enforcement in TaskTool, owner start and HTTP/CLI routes remain WP31–WP32, so this checkpoint does not expose an executable workflow feature by itself.

The approved nested-limit contract required an extra engine-only ledger module: each nested group has a private scoped guard identity, with no Session row or model. Children inherit the real run and request's delegated scopes through it, and every scope receives a step once. The group's remaining spend, step and unknown-price allowance subtract prior usage on warm and cold resume. This keeps unrelated outer work independent of a stopped group. WP31 must validate the internal guard-parent marker's run/delegated lineage before TaskTool links a workflow child; ordinary callers cannot supply it as a tool argument.

Technical bounds and recovery semantics are now explicit in §5.G:
- Pull-based projections and a ready queue of at most 64, additionally bounded by remaining agents, avoid materializing a nested cross-product. A stress case with 1,024 expanded stages and 1,000 inputs per stage stores only four nodes before its three-agent limit. Sort/dedupe buffering and durable node state refuse above 32,000 entries; refusals produce partial results and an explicit error. File discovery and Git-output bounds are also visible refusals, not implicit truncation.
- Protected Session metadata stores a versioned checkpoint capped at 16 MiB. Result values above 2 KiB pin immutable child/assistant identities, output kind, digest and byte count; hydration bounds each value and the total separately. Session metadata has no smaller existing byte ceiling; BackgroundJobStore's separate 4 KiB metadata allowance is not used for this state. Expiring Truncate artifacts are regenerated when missing or changed, rather than used as durable answer storage.
- A FIFO checkpoint lock captures the state when its write actually starts. Pending launch keys persist before child creation, and child identities persist before provider execution. Cold recovery refuses ambiguous or foreign identities, consumes completed results without inference, and releases only reservations that created no child. Accounting merges cumulative event/row/result maxima before deriving resume offsets. Unknown settled usage still stops admission; partial event books cannot clear that refusal. Codium event accounting follows the A23 projector and preserves Codium versus unknown-price counters.
- Elapsed-time checkpoints identify active attempts and started groups. With no trusted earlier stop timestamp, cold recovery conservatively counts unsettled downtime; settled pauses do not count. Old interruption timestamps cannot cap a newer attempt, future checkpoints refuse, rollback cannot replenish counters, and repeated resume does not charge the same interval twice. This follows the approved deadline semantics and adds no downtime exemption.
- Edited specs may remove or rename a nested group: obsolete group associations are dropped while historical Sessions and root agent/spend/token counters remain charged. A same-ID resumed child notifies the runtime once after task metadata marks it running, so its permission/question wait still pauses admission after 60 seconds even when another child frees a slot.
- The verifier inherits the producer model unless overridden, and the pure planner checks that effective model against the verifier agent's model allowance. The fixed brief and escaped workflow title count toward the complete 64 KiB prompt limit.

Validation (Linux, 2026-11-01): **115 engine cases passed** across sources 11, library 6, launch 10, ledger 12, runner 6, resume 9, state 14, bounds 5, scheduling 8 and runtime 34. The core plan file passed all 11 cases, including inherited verifier-model permissions. Core and final engine typechecks passed. The final runtime pass includes same-ID waiting with an available slot, crash/clock recovery, removed and renamed groups, real file/Git fanout and full-prefix prompt bounds; launch was rerun after its final notification change. A test-only optional-prompt narrowing fixed the final engine type errors without changing its assertion. Formatting and diff checks passed.

Independent review by `integration_review` closed the removed-group and resumed-waiting findings after reading their cold/causal regressions and the final source. `workflow_core` contributed the source/library/ledger/state modules and real persistence fixtures; the root agent separately checked the final waiting regression and approved the conservative clock semantics. Local checks used package cwd, one test file per command, `--timeout 30000`, and the global `/workspace/.tools/vector-serial` wrapper. Fixtures used real session, event, file, Git and guard services with injected local execution and no provider request. This records focused local evidence, not a new full-suite CI result or release. General CI and release-evidence integration remain coordinated separately by the root agent, followed by the required complete pre-push hook.

## 2026-11-01 WP31 — workflow tool and engine integration

Registered the shallow `workflow` tool with its existing guide and a late task getter, added `WorkflowRuns` validation/approval/start/resume, and connected trusted coordinators and agents to TaskTool, task management, notifications and session protections. The hidden native coordinator has no model prompt and retains a final deny-all policy. Model-facing arguments cannot supply workflow authority or owner quiet options. Public workflow HTTP/CLI entry points and owner synthetic-message admission remain WP32; this checkpoint does not claim those routes exist.

Contract clarifications and review fixes:

- The root-approved coordinator admission mode consumes a request launch and workflow-run allowance and retains delegated spend checks, while creating zero execution-slot demand, reservation, prepayment or queue entries. Actual agent children retain workflow slot priority and root reserve behavior. No exempt-marker bypass is used. Foreground admission scopes can remain open without deadlocking workers at one, two or three configured slots; coordinator resume still refuses an exhausted delegated ceiling.
- Approval includes effective producer/verifier models and effort, possible permission asks and both run and shared delegated ceilings. Real web/MCP history and child holds preserve elevated asks even when a workflow/task part is also present. A true engine agent-process marker and an oversized plan also prevent reusable approval. Guest joins and changed plan/root permissions after the ask refuse before child creation. The approved root permission fingerprint persists in protected run progress and is checked before coordinator or worker execution, including automatic retries. Only a separately validated workflow resume replaces it. Current inherited denies are retained when an existing worker resumes.
- Workflow group guard parents are private engine identities. TaskTool verifies their run/delegated scope inheritance before linking and again before execution. Agents cannot start another coordinator, forge their parent lineage, promote themselves, or gain the ordinary exemption marker.
- Workflow lifecycle totals combine durable own/delegated usage and the validated runtime checkpoint's step/tool counts. Before any checkpoint exists, a fallback examines at most 64 verified children; beyond that, or when accounting cannot be decoded, usage remains unknown. It never exposes a partial subtotal or fabricated zero. Runtime ledgers and spend admission do not use this presentation fallback. Stopping a run still traverses its actual child ancestry, including the unchanged 300-agent fixture, and preserves unrelated siblings. Ordinary task inspection keeps its existing output tail; workflow reports preserve their artifact header.
- Owner-started runs default to quiet parent completion. Worker completion and permission/question attention become durable synthetic quiet messages inside the native coordinator without calling prompt operations. Result claims remain deduplicated. Run notes retain aggregate usage and workflow resume/inspect/stop guidance.
- Saved workflow JSON now fingerprints the whole workflow file in PromptTrust. The old config-prompt projection could miss edits to workflow stages. Workflow task use stays excluded from learning, and genuine MCP taint takes precedence over generic delegation taint. Prompt/command/shell/revert guards and reserved native-agent names cover the coordinator's other entry points.
- Structured-output restatement changes only the existing `restate: true` path. Accepted bounded original schemas retain their exact contract in the description when SDK transforms cannot carry boolean, nullable, `not` or `$ref` sibling semantics. The engine continues to validate the original schema. Four real SDK serializers are exercised with an intercepted offline fetch; their requests retain that contract, and 22 invalid examples per provider fail engine validation. This is offline wire evidence, not a claim of live provider acceptance. Ordinary tool transforms retain their previous behavior and schema depth/cycle/byte limits remain enforced.

The next Codium provenance integration must combine both own and included-descendant proofs before asserting workflow `tokensOwnOnly`. Historical/imported unknown provenance must stay unknown; copying only the coordinator's own proof would mislabel its aggregate. This is a recorded integration obligation, not a field added ahead of the Codium schema change.

The unchanged 300-agent stop fixture passed in 16.98 seconds under its 30-second timeout. The final causal queued-worker case was rerun after adding actual job-registration and non-promotion assertions; default automatic retry remained enabled and still produced zero provider prompts until explicit resume. The final quiet-note pass includes exact aggregate token/tool attributes. An initial provider-fixture typecheck failure was corrected by supplying the offline fetch's required `preconnect` method and handling the SDK's `PromiseLike` result without `.catch`; its five tests and the final engine typecheck passed afterward. No Core, Schema or Protocol source changed in WP31. Formatting and `git diff --check` passed.

All commands used the owning package, one test file per invocation with the unchanged 30,000 ms timeout, and `/workspace/.tools/vector-serial`; fixtures used local execution and no provider request. Independent `integration_review_v14` reread and cleared the source fixes, including the approved-policy retry case, durable quiet-note claims and documented bounded unknown accounting. `workflow_core` supplied the tool/session/config/Manager protections and quiet-note regressions. ## 2026-11-01 WP32 — owner workflow routes and command line

Implemented the six experimental workflow routes with explicit `WorkflowApiError`, default-agent capability checks, owner-only global/start/resume authorization, and pre-forward workspace guards. Existing guests may read runs they can access but never receive the saved spec; the run's actual parent must match the requested conversation. Native workflow listing filters coordinators before the existing bounded task scan and refuses visibly if more than 200 runs would make the approved non-paginated response incomplete. Detail returns at most 500 nodes with UTF-8 bounds and keeps the spec optional during queued startup.

Owner admission now uses the actual shared SessionRunState lock and default caller, records the owner's command-style user action before its synthetic assistant/tool, and starts no coordinator model request. Preview is read-only and generic because its approved input has no session ID; actual start revalidates current conversation permissions and saved model. It preserves a free selection over a paid default and retains Codium Auto as an explicitly warned dynamic selection. Child inference resolves Auto through the existing per-request Codium path. Owner start does not overwrite the selected main agent or a concurrent human model switch. Preview and execution share saved-input merging and unchanged-saved-source bounds on resume.

Independent review found and closed four owner boundaries: an incoming human prompt could be swallowed by the handoff result, a rejected owner start could propagate its own failure to that joined prompt, cancellation just outside launch could leave a running transcript part, and replacement-spec resume could preview different inputs than it executed. Causal tests cover success and both failure handoffs, a distinct concurrent model selection, and cancellation before/after launch. Internal handoff metadata lives in tool state metadata: the real SDK history test caught that a top-level boolean would be forwarded as invalid provider options. A second real SDK regression proved that its empty-object omission needs the existing NoContent payload pattern for default resume.

The CLI provides offline file validation plus attached saved/validate/list/show/resume/stop. A missing capabilities flag produces explicit pre-1.99.113 upgrade guidance; false still permits management reads/stops. `run --workflow` validates and prints warnings/plan before start, defaults quiet, and supports an explicit summary. Unsupported prompt-only arguments are rejected instead of silently dropping model or budget choices. The new command gate runs agent-marker refusal before local policy/account work, while offline validation bypasses the account transport. Its actual yargs tests exercise parsed positional actions, duplicate-option refusal, source and published entrypoints, and ordering. The Local backstop remains the first entrypoint import. The root-owned run helper has separate real SDK/SSE tests and independent lifecycle/security review.

The final CLI lifecycle review also closed stale-attempt/status ordering, bounded network operations, sibling permission scope, and summary correlation: only the assistant answering this run's completion note may satisfy the requested summary. Actual wire task states are `completed`/`failed`/`stopped`; the run helper now terminates on those values, and SDK fixtures type their status against the generated detail contract instead of accepting internal `error`/`cancelled` literals. The shared tracker still accepts internal task-event states as well. A real pending-note service regression then found the Stop gap between durable note publication and model-runner admission. TaskNotify now registers that in-flight message before publication, permanently revokes it on Stop, and checks it inside the actual runner; a later human unfence cannot revive the old note. Suppressed deliveries persist both quiet markers, and normal mixed waking batches retain their own per-note flags. The trusted note attempt is exposed in notification metadata for stale-note rejection. The registry is ephemeral and cleaned when delivery settles; it grants no public wire authority.

Integration obligations: V15's GitHub credential loading and narrow Actions account exemption must be preserved when merging the new command-gate caller. Codium A25 must preserve the workflow `tokensOwnOnly` proof across the durable usage schema and combine own plus delegated proof in aggregate display; old/unproven records remain unknown. No account setting, live provider, main merge or release was performed by this package.

Validation (Linux, 2026-11-01): **105 distinct focused cases passed**: owner admission/handoff 6, owner model selection 3, actual yargs command gate 5, shared server authorization 5, existing Runs 14, real HTTP/SDK workflow routes 9, workflow CLI 16, run/workflow SDK/SSE lifecycle 25, pending notification admission 4, existing task notifications 16 and workflow notifications 2. The final HTTP and owner-model reruns use typed public terminal states; the run suite includes actual `failed` and `stopped` responses, stale attempts, unrelated sibling permissions/answers, bounded disconnect cleanup and suppressed summaries. The notification cases exercise the real Prompt/TaskManager/LLM boundary with causal barriers, including Stop followed by a new human unfence and the positive waking control. Repeated checks after fixes are not counted as additional cases.

All six workflow HTTP exerciser scenarios passed, with 302 registered routes and no missing or extra scenarios. SDK/OpenAPI regeneration, its normal TypeScript build, client regeneration, server package types and the final shared engine typecheck passed. Targeted serial `run --help` and `workflow --help` captures updated their snapshots and added workflow help coverage; the full multi-process help harness was left to CI. Formatting of changed source and `git diff --check` passed. Tests ran one named file per invocation under the unchanged 30,000 ms case timeout, using the global workload wrapper and local fixtures without live provider traffic.

Independent owner/routes/notification and CLI lifecycle reviews are clear after the reported fixes. The 39-file WP32 checkpoint includes the root-owned CLI slice and the independently authored HTTP fixture. The full phase-6 Linux/Windows gate remains a hosted CI run after the combined accounting integration, not a claim inferred from these local tests; the required 23-package pre-push hook remains in force.


WP33 contract correction approved during read-only preparation: `Task.StopResult` and `Task.WaitResult` gain optional `shells: Task.Shell[]` only for explicit shell-ID requests. Ordinary task results remain unchanged; shell jobs are never fabricated as `Task.Info`. Every returned shell output path remains owner-only. This resolves the existing 5.H stop/wait requirement's omitted response shape; the schema/runtime implementation remains the next package after the accounting integration.


## 2026-11-01 — A25 accounting integration after WP32

Integrated the immutable Codium checkpoint `295ab95e007f46726272730a3e5840d4c20d16ca` into WP32 `4e2ba3c59e8f9bae1ad3db0f91c83c8605a174a3`.
