Those results describe that standalone source, not this combined integration.

The merge retains WP28's full-batch identity preflight, durable audience restrictions, home routing and owner-local export behavior. Accepted replay projectors receive the private accounting provenance context only after duplicate/sequence checks. UI snapshots project canonical accounting while raw synchronization/history preserve stored event bytes. The native and legacy event handlers apply both privacy filtering and canonical accounting projection.

Workflow display now requires both own and delegated token proof and pairs the proof with the observed quantity. Editable progress cannot borrow a trusted flag to certify its own number. Unknown checkpoints preserve known pricing evidence and show uncertainty; mixed work retains its known own dollar subtotal alongside Codium and unpriced labels. Pure Codium work does not show a zero-token gauge. Completion notes gate token attributes on the same proof. Workflow CLI JSON shares the task projection, and run JSON omits workflow checkpoint/token metadata without mutating stored parts.

Runtime accounting retains conservative maxima for limit enforcement. Hydrated proof is untrusted; raw step updates cannot grant it. A fresh canonical observation may certify only quantities it covers. Workflow launch independently compares lifecycle usage with authoritative Session totals and all token buckets. The regression saves a forged larger subtotal with a forged true flag, resumes it, and proves that the larger limit counter survives while display proof remains false.

The schema generator produced the same two ALTER statements already present in incoming migration `20261101020803_session_token_provenance.ts`. The newly generated duplicate was byte-compared after normalizing only its migration ID and removed. A second generator run found no schema changes; the combined schema/registry and both SDK/client outputs were regenerated. The database migration test also verifies there are no ungenerated changes.

Focused validation covers **221 distinct passing cases** across 19 files: Core report 5, workflow runtime 3, workflow state 15, workflow manager 9, workflow notifications 2, workflow CLI 17, run usage output 3, tasks CLI 3, run/workflow lifecycle 25, database migration 21, core events 46, audience 22, token provenance 19, event accounting 2, home routing 1, event HTTP 3, session audience 12, sanitized export 7 and background-note billing 6. The session-audience file initially passed 11 cases and failed its direct event-response fixture because the newly required Database service was not exposed by that fixture. Adding the real Database node fixed the affected case; there was no subsequent full-file rerun. Engine typechecking also caught a readonly schema type in the new CLI fixture; it now uses the actual generated SDK TaskInfo contract. Neither correction changes production behavior.

Independent source review is clear for privacy/replay, canonical value/proof pairing, runtime/launch recovery, mixed billing, notes and CLI projection. Core, Engine and Server package types passed after the fixture corrections. The mandatory clean-source push hook and hosted full phase-6 run remain next. No live provider, account setting, main merge or release action is included.

## 2026-11-02 Phase 6 review (WP29–WP32)

Seventeen review findings were checked against the code and all were confirmed. Fifteen are fixed in full and two in part (scheduler cost and the retry prompt), with the reasons below. Most fixes have a new regression. The scheduler, approval-read and Stop-cap changes rely on the existing runner, scheduling, bounds, Runs and stop tests: a dedicated Stop case would need more than 2,000 child sessions.

**The contract text is restored.** Commits `9ae43953`, `e2b36f39`, `35a66514` and `4e2ba3c5` had edited `multiagent-parity-spec.md` to match the code. It is back to its `b5af4269` text. Each clarification it had gained is recorded here instead:
- §4.1 module map: the orchestration directory also holds `WorkflowRuntime`, `WorkflowLedger`, `WorkflowState` and, after this review, `WorkflowCheckpoint`. The runtime adapter handles session persistence, live events and scoped ledgers; the state codec validates durable references and bounds. Core gains `WorkflowCheckpointStore` (below).
- §4.5 `WaitResult`/`StopResult` `shells`: the WP33 contract correction at the end of the WP32 entry.
- §4.6 `taskNotification` gains `attempt?`: the trusted attempt of the note, so a client can reject a stale note after a resume (the run helper's tracker uses it). Notes also carry the effective `quiet` flag.
- §4.7 `TaskNotify` gains `canReply(parentID, messageID)`: engine-only admission for a note already persisted but not yet admitted to the model runner (C.2). It has no wire form.
- C.2 delivery registration and Stop revocation: the WP32 entry.
- G.1 source and buffer bounds: the WP30 entry. Reserved `constructor`/`prototype` identifiers: the WP29 entry.
- G.2 verifier model inheritance: the WP30 entry. Writable verifiers and nested limits: the WP29 entry.
- G.3 revalidation after approval: the WP31 entry.
- G.4 coordinator admission: the WP31 entry. The 64-node ready queue, ledgers and progress/checkpoint split: the WP30 entry, with the checkpoint change below.
- G.5 report bounds: the WP29 entry. Observation fallback and quiet coordinator notes: the WP31 entry.
- G.6 persistence, recovery and clock rules: the WP30 entry.
- G.8 owner-start rules: the WP32 entry.
- WP30 file and test lists: `runtime.ts`, `ledger.ts` and `state.ts` are new files, with `launch.test.ts`, `ledger.test.ts`, `state.test.ts`, `bounds.test.ts`, `scheduling.test.ts` and `runtime.test.ts` beside the listed tests.

**A second table (§4.10 says one).** The runtime checkpoint (up to 16 MiB) had lived in the run Session's lifecycle metadata. Every save rewrote the Session row and published a durable `session.updated` carrying the whole Session, so each save also added an `event` row that stays until the Session is deleted, and every client received it. The checkpoint now lives in its own table, `workflow_checkpoint` (`packages/core/src/workflow-checkpoint.sql.ts`; migration `20261103025953_workflow_checkpoint` from `bun run script/migration.ts --name workflow_checkpoint`): one row per run, keyed by the run Session with `on delete cascade`, holding the writing attempt, a SHA-256 digest and the checkpoint text. `WorkflowCheckpointStore` writes it in one upsert that refuses an older attempt and skips an unchanged digest. G.6 calls this only "protected versioned runtime state", so the move keeps the contract's meaning.
- The run record keeps only its small progress, the report and `progress.accounting` `{steps, toolUses}`, rewritten only when those totals change. `SubagentLifecycle.observe` reads that summary, so task lists and settlement never load the checkpoint row.
- A checkpoint an earlier build kept in metadata is read once, then dropped from the record after the first row is saved.
- Progress publication saves the checkpoint only on the 30 s heartbeat. Checkpoints before each admission and after each outcome are unchanged (G.4).

**Other review changes.**
- Writer status (G.2, G.4). An agent writes when it may edit, or when it may run a bash rule outside the read-only verification commands (`git diff*`, `bun test*` and the rest). Before, explore, review and security were planned as writers. Each node then took all 16 permits of the root gate, so read-only fan-outs ran one at a time.
- `workflow` is withheld whenever `task` is, including a task disabled by permission or a message's `tools`. `TASK_OFFERED` follows the same rules (G.3, G.9).
- `WorkflowApiError` gains `disabled` and `issues` as §4.9 defines. Owner starts refused by settings (background subagents off, task or workflow denied for the default agent) return `disabled`. Invalid starts and resumes return their structured issues. Model-tool refusals inside `WorkflowRuns` keep `forbidden`, since no route returns them.
- Workflow agents resume by stable node identity: §4.11's agent marker gains `node?`. Before, a repair or a second verifier round carried a new execution key, and TaskTool refused to resume its own session. The execution `key` stays in the record for crash recovery.
- Launches pass no caller history to TaskTool, which reads none. A run's part metadata in the parent shows progress only: no runtime, report or summary.
- Approval reads the parent's history, which already carries settled children's holds. Only children whose results have not reached the parent (still open, or a note not yet delivered) are read, at most 64 of them and 50 recent messages each. Records store no hold, so the review's "stored hold" alternative does not exist.
- Stop counts only open run descendants toward its 2,000 cap. Past the cap it still stops the coordinator, whose interruption cancels its running nodes and their children, and logs that the rest was left to that cascade.
- `vector run --workflow` inspects the run at most every 5 s while the run's record says it is queued or running, and at once otherwise. The event stream retries dropped connections inside the SDK. A stream the server ends is resubscribed up to three times. After any reconnect the run, its note, the parent's answers and its status are read back. The run is stopped only after reconnection fails, on the inactivity timeout or on an error.

**Fixed in part.**
- Repeated briefs. A repair sends the verifier feedback with a short fixed line, not the whole brief again, and the execution key still covers the full logical prompt. A retry after a failure keeps the whole brief, because a failed attempt (for example a queued worker refused before any provider call) may never have received it.
- Scheduler cost. Each source item's node key is memoized, so an admitted item is never rendered or hashed again. Template stage views and output order are rebuilt only when outputs or artifacts change. Stage aggregates are computed once per tick, and only when a stage condition needs them. The settled-stage check walks the nodes once per tick instead of once per stage, and the progress projection runs at most once a second instead of every tick. The 50 ms tick itself remains: rescanning only on change would replace the scheduling loop, and with the memo each tick costs a pass of cheap lookups.
- Raw input values (WP29 template list). An input renders as the author's own text, unenveloped, so `@scope/pkg` stays literal and attaches only a path that actually exists. That is the same as any brief. The core test pins that framing, beside the enveloped agent-data case where `@` cannot form a mention.

**Evidence** (macOS, 2026-11-02; package cwd, one file per command, `--timeout 30000`). Engine: runtime 36 of 37, runs 17, resume 10, runner 6, scheduling 8, bounds 5, launch 10, state 15, owner 6, owner models 3, manager workflow 9, task workflow 6, workflow tool 11, task 157, lifecycle read 12, HTTP workflow 10, session stop 4, run workflow CLI 27, workflow CLI 17, usage output 3, LLM request 19, workflow guards 5, note taint 6, agent 63 and recovery 7. Core: template 5, spec 9 and database migration 21. The one runtime failure is the real file-source fan-out: this machine has no ripgrep binary ("ripgrep execution failed"), so it is left to CI, which installs ripgrep. The core schema check (`script/migration.ts --check`) reports no ungenerated changes. The SDK and OpenAPI were regenerated; the client generator produced no change. Core and engine typechecks pass. Before each fix was restored, a temporary revert made its new regression fail: the verify loop, writer status and part projection. Full suites run in CI.

## 2026-11-02 WP33 (background shell commands)

`shell` takes `background: true`; `ShellJobs` (`packages/engine/src/task/shells.ts`) runs the command as a durable `shell` job, writes its output to `<tool-output>/shell-<id>.log`, and sends a `shell` note through `TaskNotify` when it exits. `task_manage list` and `GET …/tasks?shells=true` list commands, stop and wait take `shl_` IDs, "Stop including helpers" stops them, recovery reports them, and `capabilities.backgroundShells` is true. Where the code departs from or adds to the contract:

- **Stop and wait results.** `Task.StopResult` and `Task.WaitResult` gain optional `shells: Task.Shell[]`, the correction recorded at the end of the WP32 entry. They appear only when the request named a `shl_` ID; ordinary results are unchanged. `stop {all: true}` still reaches subagents only: commands stop by their ID or with "Stop including helpers". An ID the session did not start is refused (`invalid`) before anything stops.
- **The row names the pid.** 5.H lists the row metadata as `{parentSessionId, background, command, outputPath}`. It also carries `pid`, so recovery can say "the process may still be running as pid N". To know the pid when the row is written, `ShellJobs.start` spawns the process first and then starts the job; a failure before the job starts kills the process and releases its slot.
- **Refusals.** `ShellJobs.start` is `Effect<Task.Shell>` as 4.7 says, so a refusal is a defect carrying its reason, as tool refusals are. The shell tool refuses a subagent, background subagents turned off, and a timeout above 24 hours before it asks anything; a timeout above 24 hours is refused, not clamped. The limit of 8 is checked when the command starts, after the asks. It is counted across every instance in this process, keyed by `TaskLimits.conversationOf`; another engine process on the same database is not counted.
- **Optional wiring.** `ShellTool` and `TaskManager` read `ShellJobs` with `Effect.serviceOption`, and `ShellJobs.node` is in both nodes' deps, so every real build has it. A test that builds either alone (the shell tests, `task-batch-promotion.test.ts`) keeps working: there, background commands are refused and `shl_` IDs are `invalid`.
- **Note states.** A command that exits on its own sends `completed` (exit 0) or `failed` (another exit code, a timeout, a signal, or output withheld) and wakes the session. A stopped command sends a quiet `stopped` note. A command stopped because its instance was going away sends none; recovery reports it as `interrupted` after the restart. `shellNotification.exitCode` is present only when the command exited with a code. The job itself ends `completed`, `error` (failed), `cancelled` (stopped) or `interrupted`, and `Task.Shell.status` maps those to completed, failed, stopped and interrupted.
- **Framing.** Each note neutralizes the core control tags and its own `shell-notification`, `shell` and `command_output` tags in the command line and its output (I13). That is done in `shells.ts`, not by widening core's `TaskSize.neutralizeTags`.
- **Same checks.** Besides the scan, asks, admission and sandbox, the output goes through the session's `assertOutput` check chunk by chunk, as a foreground command's does. Output it refuses is never written or shown, and the command is stopped.
- **Listings.** `ShellJobs.list` also returns rows earlier engines wrote for the session: a running row whose owner is dead reads as interrupted, and such rows carry no exit code. `outputPath` is removed from list, stop and wait results for anyone but the owner.
- **Recovery note.** Every recovered command's note says the process may still be running, with its pid, and that Vector never stops a process it can no longer see. The wording is the same whatever the cause. Commands do not count in `reconcile`'s `reported`, which counts helpers.
- **Tool text.** `shell.txt` gains one static sentence: a long command that ends on its own can run with `background: true` instead of a long timeout. `task_manage`'s `task_ids` description adds that stop and wait also take background command IDs (`shl_…`). Both are fixed text (I22).
- **Tests outside the list.** `test/tool/parameters.test.ts` gains a case for the new parameter, and its bash wire-shape snapshot gains `background`, because 4.4 adds the parameter (I22 names this file). The exerciser's capabilities scenario also checks `backgroundShells`.
- **Not done.** `Truncate.cleanup` removes only `tool_…` files, so command logs (up to 64 MiB each) are never cleaned up. `vector run` does not wait for background commands: they are killed when the run ends and its instance is disposed (CLI surfaces are WP39). No guest test checks that `outputPath` is removed from route results. The Windows process-tree case in `shells.test.ts` runs only on Windows CI.

Evidence (macOS, 2026-11-02; package cwd, one file per command, `--timeout 30000`). Engine: `test/task/shells.test.ts` 8 pass and 1 skip (the Windows-only case), `test/tool/task-manage.test.ts` 48, `test/tool/parameters.test.ts` 62, `test/tool/task-manage-schema.test.ts` 3, `test/task/notify.test.ts` 16, `test/task/recovery.test.ts` 7, `test/tool/shell.test.ts` 63, `test/server/task-batch-promotion.test.ts` 5, `test/server/session-stop.test.ts` 4, `test/server/auth-guest.test.ts` 27. Schema: `test/compatibility.test.ts` 7. Exerciser: coverage 335 pass with none missing or extra; the effect scenarios `experimental.task.list` (with `.active` and `.shells`) and `experimental.capabilities.get` pass. The SDK and OpenAPI were regenerated; the client generator produced no change. Schema and engine typechecks pass. Full suites run in CI.

## 2026-11-02 Phase 7 review (WP33)

Thirteen review findings were checked against the code. Several repeat each other: two cover the recovery wording, two the `vector run` wait, two the log cleanup and two the timeout check before the asks. Every one was confirmed except one part. A background `timeout: 0` never reaches the asks: the shell tool's parameters take `timeout` as a positive integer, so decoding refuses 0 before `execute` runs. Only a direct `ShellJobs.start` call got the wrong 24-hour reason. That call now has its own reason. These corrections replace the matching bullets of the WP33 entry above.

- **Recovery note by cause.** `ShellJobs.interrupted` takes the row's `cause`. A `crash` row (a dead owner's running command) keeps "The process may still be running as pid N; Vector never stops a process it can no longer see." A `disposed` or `shutdown` row was settled only after the run's cleanup killed the command's process tree. Its note says Vector stopped the command when it reloaded the folder or shut down, says it is no longer running, and names no pid. By the next pass that number may belong to an unrelated process. 5.H gives the pid wording only to a dead owner's command, so this meets it.
- **`vector run` waits for background commands.** The run loop's tracker also counts a command from the finished shell part that names it (`metadata.background` and a `shl_` `shellID`) until its `shellNotification` part arrives. That note is answered like a subagent's: a stopped command's quiet note settles it, and any other note waits for the turn that reads it. The inactivity bound (`VECTOR_RUN_BACKGROUND_WAIT_MS`, 10 minutes by default) applies as it does to subagents. A command quiet for longer is stopped through `task.stop`, which takes `shl_` IDs, and the run fails. The timeout message counts subagents and commands apart. Pure helpers in `run/background-wait.ts` do the tracking. WP34 and WP39 do not cover this loop, so the WP33 entry's hand-off to WP39 is withdrawn.
- **Row metadata and revert.** Besides `pid`, a command's row and job metadata carry `parentMessageId` and `callID` from the call that started it, the keys background subagents use. Revert cleanup now cancels running `shell` jobs as well as `task` jobs launched by the removed messages or calls. The exit note carries `launchID`, so TaskNotify drops it once the message is gone.
- **Checks before the asks.** Besides the subagent, disabled and over-24-hours refusals, the tool asks `ShellJobs.full(sessionID)` before any ask and refuses a conversation that already runs 8 commands. `start` still reserves the place and stays the check that counts.
- **Bounded stop.** Cleanup closes the command's scope, which disposes its output pipes, alongside the kill, and bounds the kill to 10 seconds. Before, a command that ignored SIGTERM could leave a descendant outside its process group holding its output. The kill then waited for that output to close with no limit, and Stop, "Stop including helpers" and instance disposal hung with it.
- **Agent listing.** `task_manage list` shows the agent every running command and the 5 that ended most recently, without `title`, which repeats the command. `TaskManager.list({shells: true})` and the route still list them all, up to 100. `outputPath` stays in the agent's rows: after compaction the agent may no longer have the start result that named the path.
- **Log cleanup.** `Truncate.cleanup` also removes `shell-<shl_ id>.log` files. A log goes once its ID's timestamp and its last write are both older than the 7-day retention. A log this process still writes to is kept through `Truncate.hold`, however quiet the command has been.
- **Tests.** `auth-guest.test.ts` checks that guests get rows without `outputPath` from the list, stop and wait routes, and that the owner's rows keep it (I19). It uses the handler-level layer with a mocked manager. `task-manage.test.ts` runs `TaskManageTool` `list` itself. `shells.test.ts` adds the disposed and shutdown recovery wording, the limit check before the asks, the row's message and call, and the bounded stop. `revert-compact.test.ts` covers the revert of a command, `truncation.test.ts` the log cleanup, and `background-wait.test.ts` and `run-process.test.ts` the run's wait. Only the Windows process-tree case of the WP33 "Not done" list remains, and it runs on Windows CI.

Evidence (macOS, 2026-11-02; package cwd, one file per command, `--timeout 30000`). Engine: `test/task/shells.test.ts` 10 pass and 1 skip (the Windows-only case), `test/tool/task-manage.test.ts` 49, `test/server/auth-guest.test.ts` 28, `test/tool/truncation.test.ts` 23, `test/session/revert-compact.test.ts` 8, `test/task/recovery.test.ts` 7, `test/tool/shell.test.ts` 63, `test/cli/run/background-wait.test.ts` 7, `test/cli/run-workflow.test.ts` 27. In `test/cli/run/run-process.test.ts` only the new background-command case was run, with `-t` and `--timeout 60000`; the whole file runs in CI. Each new regression was checked by reverting its fix for one run: the bounded stop (the old cleanup hung past the 30 s test timeout), the guest `outputPath` strip, revert cancelling commands, and the run's wait. Engine typecheck passes. No schema, route or tool-parameter shape changed, so no generator was run.

## 2026-11-03 WP34 (CLI: `vector tasks` and `vector run` interrupts)

`vector tasks` gains `interrupt [--helpers]`, `resume <id…> | --interrupted [--text]`, `promote`, `changes <id> [--diff]`, `merge <id> [--file …] [--yes]`, `discard <id> [--yes]`, `workflow [runID]`, `list --active --shells` and `stop --background`. `vector run` gains `--stop-helpers` and a Ctrl+C handler in its non-interactive branch, and the split footer (`--mini`) gains "Stop including helpers", "Resume interrupted helpers" and the helpers notice. Where the code departs from or adds to the contract:

- **`Task.Info.background` is now filled.** 4.5 adds the field and I.6 refuses `stop --background` when it is missing, but no earlier WP wrote it, so every engine would have been refused. `TaskManager.observe` (`task/manager.ts`, outside the file list) now always sends it: true when the record or its job is background. `test/tool/task-manage.test.ts` (outside the list) gains one case for it, which fails without the change.
- **The first Ctrl+C fences.** It stops the main turn through route 1 with `helpers: false` when the engine has `sessionStop`, otherwise through the unchanged abort. Route 1 fences the parent (WP12), so a helper's later report starts no new turn and the run ends once its helpers finish, instead of the main agent starting again after the user pressed Ctrl+C. Any press makes the run exit 130 when it ends. "Stop everything" is route 1 with `helpers: true`, or abort plus `task.stop {all}` on an older engine. The stop requests are bounded to 5 s before the second press exits.
- **Which runs handle Ctrl+C.** Only the non-interactive prompt and `--command` runs, when stdin or stderr is a terminal. `--workflow` runs keep the default, and `--stop-helpers` is refused with `--workflow` like the other prompt-only options. SIGTERM is untouched.
- **Helpers left.** The count is the session's own queued, running or waiting subagents that are not foreground (foreground work ends with the turn), plus its running background commands, from `task.list {active, shells}`. Nested children and desktop workspaces are not counted. With none left, nothing extra is printed.
- **Where the code sits.** `run/interrupt.ts` holds the pure `interruptPlan` and, beside it, the SDK helpers the run and the footer share (`stopSession`, `countHelpers`, `resumeInterrupted`, `watchInterrupts`). `runtime.lifecycle.ts` only passes the two new footer callbacks through; its interactive SIGINT is unchanged. `footer.view.tsx` (outside the list) registers the `session.interrupt.all` keymap command and hands the callbacks to the palette; the command has no default key until WP39 adds `session_interrupt_all`. The palette rows moved into pure `runCommandEntries` and `runCommandPick` in `footer.command.tsx`, with the existing rows unchanged.
- **Footer behaviour.** Both helper commands are always in the palette, idle or not, as on the desktop. The notice after an interrupt is a scrollback line, "N helpers still running · "Stop including helpers" in the command palette stops them", rather than a status that the next turn would overwrite. "Resume interrupted helpers" resumes every interrupted helper that is not resumed, not dismissed and has `controls.resume`, and says to update an engine without `taskResume`.
- **`vector tasks` details.**
  - A capability flag that is present but `false` prints "This server's engine has turned off what it needs to …" instead of the update message. A 404 or a non-object answer counts as missing, as an HTML answer does. `workflow` needs only that `workflows` is present: `false` still lists and shows runs, as `vector workflow` does.
  - `list --active` sends `active` and filters locally, with no capability check. `list --shells` needs `backgroundShells`. Options used with the wrong action are refused before any request.
  - `stop --background` checks `background` only on the session's queued or running native subagents, stops the ones with `background: true` and `controls.stop`, and sends at most 200 IDs per call. `resume` sends at most 32 per call; if a later call fails, the error says how many were already resumed.
  - The `[text]` positional and `--text` are the same yargs key, so `resume <id> "message"` also works. More IDs go through `--task`.
  - `merge` and `discard` refuse under the agent marker inside `executeTaskCommand`, before any request (I12). `command-gate.ts` is unchanged, so an account check may run first. At a terminal they ask "(y/N)"; without a terminal they do nothing unless `--yes` is given, and exit 1.
  - `interrupt` without `--helpers` prints that helpers keep running and how to stop them; it makes no extra list request.
  - `formatTasks(tasks, {limits?, now?})` prints the limits line first ("5 of 8 running · 2 waiting · 12 of 32 started this request · $3.10 of $25.00 subagent spend"; "waiting" only when some wait; unknown spend as "unknown" or "$x + unknown"; "(ceiling reached)" when stopped), then per task "provider/model · high effort · fork", the queued reason, "Worktree: branch · state · N files changed (+a -d)" with conflicts, "Interrupted when Vector stopped (cause); nothing resumes on its own" until resumed or dismissed, "Needs your permission · 3m" or "Needs your answer · 3m", and "Result: JSON, valid". Every string, the inspect output, messages, structured JSON and patches included, passes `sanitizeTerminalText`; tabs in a patch become two spaces first.
- **Help.** `--size` reads "standard and thorough offer subagents; light only manages ones already running". `--stop-helpers` says that without `--attach`, exiting ends the helpers anyway. The `--mini` description in `tui.ts` (outside the list) says that without `--attach`, exiting ends the subagents and background commands it started. The `vector run --help` snapshot in `test/cli/help/__snapshots__/help-snapshots.test.ts.snap` (outside the list) was rewritten from one serial `run --help` capture at COLUMNS=120; the new option widens the option column, so every line moved.

Evidence (macOS, 2026-11-03; package cwd, one file per command, `--timeout 30000`). Engine: `test/cli/tasks.test.ts` 13, `test/cli/run/interrupt.test.ts` 10 (new), `test/cli/run/footer.menu.test.ts` 4, `test/cli/run/runtime.test.ts` 9 (including a real `RunFooter` on a split-footer test renderer: one Esc while running only arms the interrupt, the second fires it, and Esc while idle does nothing), `test/tool/task-manage.test.ts` 50, `test/task/manager-workflow.test.ts` 9, `test/cli/workflow.test.ts` 17, `test/cli/run-workflow.test.ts` 27, `test/cli/run/footer.view.test.tsx` 22 pass. The `background` regression was checked by removing the manager line for one run (it failed). Engine typecheck passes. No HTTP schema, route or config changed, so no generator was run. Left to CI: the help-snapshot harness (it spawns 8 processes), `test/server/httpapi-sdk.test.ts` (it calls `executeTaskCommand` against a real server), and an end-to-end Ctrl+C on a running `vector run`, which is covered only by `watchInterrupts` with a fake signal source. Website copy for these commands is WP40a's.

## 2026-11-03 WP35 (app foundations: capabilities, stop, settings)

The desktop reads the engine's capability flags once per server, follow-ups steer by default with Queue in Settings → General → "While Vector works", Alt+Enter flips the mode for one message, slash commands and shell input always queue while Vector works, a failed steer leaves the session busy, the composer's Stop gains a "Stop including helpers (N)" menu, Stop, Esc and Ctrl+G use the "Stop button" setting, and AltGr never runs a shortcut. Where the code departs from or adds to the contract:

- **`pages/session.tsx` (outside the list).** Its `queueEnabled` becomes `queueEnabled(alternate)`, built by the pure `queueEnabled` in `prompt-input/submit.ts` from the session's busy, blocked and child state, `settings.general.followup` and `steerSupported` of the server's `/global/health` version. Without it the two new settings would change nothing until WP36 moves the queue. The same file shows the older-engine notice ("Messages wait in the queue on this engine") the first time a follow-up queues on an engine before 1.99.100, once per server per window. Nothing else in the page changed.
- **Commands queue through the same callback.** `shouldQueue(alternate)` stays the only queue signal. A slash command or shell input queues whenever the queue is open, which is `shouldQueue(false) || shouldQueue(true)` because `alternate` only flips the mode (`queues` in submit.ts). A slash command is `/name` for a command the engine lists, as `sendFollowupDraft` detects it; text that only starts with "/" is a message and steers. In plan mode and for a new session nothing queues, as before.
- **Queued shell input.** I.1 queues shell input, but a queued draft had no shell form. `FollowupDraft` gains `shell?: true`, and `sendFollowupDraft` runs such a draft with `session.shell` when it is sent (the engine refuses a shell command during a run).
- **Failed steer.** A message sent while the session works (and not queued) is a steer: it goes out without the optimistic busy and idle writes, and a failure no longer sets `session_status` to idle. A failed send from an idle session is unchanged.
- **Plain Stop is the unchanged abort.** A main-only Stop (the default, or "Stop the main turn" in the menu) is `POST /session/:id/abort`, not route 1 with `helpers: false`, so it does not fence the parent. WP34's CLI fences on its first Ctrl+C; the desktop keeps today's Stop, as section 0 says. After a main-only Stop with helpers left, the toast "Main turn stopped. N helpers are still working." offers "Stop them too" (the wide stop) and "Show tasks" (opens the Tasks pane).
- **Who counts as a helper.** `helperTargets` counts the live background subagents on the open root session's board: not foreground ones (they end with the turn), not desktop `workspace:`/`swarm:` tasks or `team`/`swarm`/`external` sources, and not tasks whose `controls.stop` is false. A subagent's own page counts none, so its Stop has no menu. Background commands are not counted in N; route 1 still stops them.
- **`stopSession` details.** Route 1 is used when `sessionStop` is set, and its failure throws (nothing else is tried behind it). Step 2 needs `backgroundSubagents`, which only an engine with the task routes sends. Helpers the engine lists no task row for (sessions from before the board) are aborted by session in step 2 as well. A `task.stop` chunk that fails for another reason is counted and the other chunks still go. The main turn is aborted once on every path. A failed request shows "Could not stop every helper"; route 1's `excluded` shows "Agent workspaces keep running; stop them from the Agent Dashboard."
- **Capabilities query.** It uses each server's global client, so the engine answers from the same default instance as the existing global provider query; the per-directory `workflows` flag is read from that instance. Any answer (404, an error status, an HTML page, text or JSON that is not an object) reads as every flag off. Only a failure with no answer throws, so the query retries rather than hiding features until the next reconnect. It is refetched on every reconnect of the event stream and is exposed as `serverSync().capabilities()`.
- **AltGraph guard.** `altGraphHeld(event)` skips the event only off macOS. A Mac has no AltGr, and Firefox on macOS reports Option as AltGraph, so a literal check would turn off every Alt shortcut there. The guard sits in `matchKeybind` (which the terminal also uses) and in the command keydown path; that path's keymap build and keydown decision moved into pure `commandKeymap` and `keydownCommand` with unchanged behaviour, so the test drives the provider's own decision.
- **Test fixtures.** happy-dom's `getModifierState("AltGraph")` returns `altKey`, which no browser does for a plain Ctrl+Alt press. The two existing `command-keybind.test.ts` cases built with `altKey: true` now build their events through a helper that reports AltGraph as a browser does; their expectations are unchanged. `submit.test.ts`'s harness gained a mutable prompt and command list, recorded `session_status` writes and stop requests, and a switch to make `promptAsync` fail; no existing expectation changed.
- **Copy.** The judge-scope description says Thorough is when you ask for it or when the agent raises an exceptionally hard request (A.4's escalate), and that ordinary work, delegated or not, is judged only with "Every task that changes files". The follow-up row's description is rewritten for Steer, Queue, Alt+Enter and the always-queued commands; other locales keep their own wording until translated.
- **Not done here.** The palette command "Stop including helpers" (`session.stopAll`, working while idle) is WP38's `use-session-commands.tsx`; `stopSession` already works on an idle session. The I.1 toast for a steer while a foreground subagent holds the turn, with "Move to background", needs the promote surfaces of WP37 and is not in any WP35 file.

Evidence (macOS, 2026-11-03; `packages/app` cwd, one file per command, `--timeout 30000`). `src/features/background-tasks/engine-capabilities.test.ts` 10 (new), `src/features/background-tasks/stop-helpers.test.ts` 9 (new), `src/context/settings.test.ts` 6, `src/components/settings-v2/settings-registry.test.ts` 15, `src/components/prompt-input/submit.test.ts` 71, `src/context/command-keybind.test.ts` 8, `src/context/command.test.ts` 2 pass. Each new regression was checked by breaking its fix for one run: the failed steer, commands always queuing, the abort scope (four cases) and the AltGraph guard (both cases) failed and pass with the fix. The app typecheck passes. No HTTP schema, route or engine config changed, so no generator was run.

## 2026-11-03 WP36 (app follow-up queue)

The desktop queue moved out of `pages/session.tsx` into `packages/app/src/features/followup/`: `followup-model.ts` (pure limits, gate, claims, adopt), `followup-store.ts` (the shared `vector.followup.dat` storage, the write lock and the broadcast) and `followup-queue.tsx` (the provider, one drainer per server and the background drains, mounted in the router root by `app.tsx`). A queued message is sent when its turn ends with the session's page closed, while one window is open; the queue pauses after a spend stop, a Stop or an error until Resume (I7); a failed send shows "Not sent" with Retry in the dock. Where the code departs from or adds to the contract:

- **Files outside the list.**
  - `prompt-input/submit.ts`: `FollowupDraft` gains `replied?: true`, and `sendFollowupDraft` treats it like a loaded assistant reply, so it skips `prepareAgentTask`. I.1 says `sendFollowupDraft` skips it; the function lives there.
  - `session-composer-region.tsx` and `session-composer-region-controller.ts`: the dock type gains `paused` and `onResume`, and the region passes them through.
  - `settings-v2/general.tsx`: without `navigator.locks` the follow-up row adds "This browser cannot share the queue between pages, so a queued message is sent only while its session is open." I.1 says Settings says so.
  - `i18n/en.ts`: the queue's toasts, the dock's failed, Retry, paused and Resume, and the Settings sentence. Other locales fall back to English.
  - `utils/persist.ts`: `evict()` skips keys under `vector.followup.dat:`, so another store's quota eviction never deletes a queue. The queue's own writes never call it (a full storage refuses the write). `utils/persist.test.ts` gains one case for this.
- **The dock also shows a pause.** Besides the failed state and Retry, the dock shows "Queue paused: <reason>" with Resume. The pause toast appears only in the window holding the drain lock and fades; without the line a paused queue would look stuck in every other window.
- **Pause and resume.** A queue stores `paused: {reason, at}` (`budget`, `aborted` or `error`) and `resumed`, the assistant message whose ending the user accepted. The gate's `lastRunEnded` is read from the last assistant message the session has loaded. A bad ending that nobody resumed makes `nextToSend` answer `{hold: "ended"}`; the drainer then writes the pause and shows the toast once. Resume clears the pause and records `resumed`, so that run's error no longer holds the queue but a later run's does. Queueing another message never clears a pause (the old page cleared it, which I7 forbids). Stop pauses the queue as `aborted`, as before. Send now and Retry count as the user resuming: they clear the pause and the failure first.
- **Claims and the send lock.** Every send, from the drainer or from Send now in any window, runs under the Web Lock `vector.followup.send:<server>:<session>` taken with `ifAvailable`, so two windows never send one session's head at once. A claim found while holding that lock is from a send that never finished. Whether the engine has its message is checked first in the loaded messages, then with `GET /session/:id/message/:messageID`. 404 means not delivered, and the item is resent with the claimed ID. Any other answer marks the item failed. A failed item keeps its claim, and Retry checks it the same way.
- **What the drainer waits for.** `ready` is the server's health answer plus the directory sync's `status === "complete"`. That bootstrap reads agents, commands, config and the directory's session statuses. Session statuses are loaded per directory in this app, so the directory bootstrap is the session-status bootstrap. A directory whose bootstrap stays partial (one of its reads failed) holds its queue until a later bootstrap completes, for example after a reconnect. Then `serverSync.session.sync(sessionID)`, whose not-found error drops the queue. The drainer holds each queued directory's sync and pins each queued session's messages while it has items.
- **Background drains.** `GlobalProvider` already keeps a server context and event stream for every listed server. `FollowupBackgroundDrains` mounts, for each non-selected server with items, that server's SDK and sync providers, its drainer and the directory syncs it holds. They unmount when its queues empty. The selected server's drainer is always mounted. Both sit in the router root (`FollowupQueueProvider`), not in `SelectedServerProviders`, so legacy-layout routes outside that shell also drain.
- **Storage.**
  - The desktop uses the renderer store `vector.followup.dat` over IPC; the web uses localStorage under `vector.followup.dat:`. A browser that refuses storage keeps the queue in memory.
  - The index key holds item counts per server and session, so the server limit needs no reads of other queues. It is written before a queue that has items and after one is removed.
  - Without Web Locks, writes are ordered only within one window, and only the page showing a session drains it.
- **Enqueue.** The composer's `onQueue` stays synchronous. The page first checks the limits against this window's copy and refuses there, so the composer keeps the text. If the stored write then refuses (another window filled the queue, or the storage is full), a toast says so and the message goes back into the composer when it is still empty on that session. Item IDs stay `Identifier.ascending("message")`; claims always use a fresh ID. Attachments count pasted images' and data-URL files' stored bytes.
- **Legacy adopt.** The `followup.v1` store is still per server and directory. Its queues move into the shared store when a session page of that directory opens, after the shared store has loaded. Items are marked replied, a legacy pause becomes `aborted`, a legacy failure carries over, and the limits apply. Then the legacy store is cleared. Adopting is idempotent by item ID.
- **Tests.**
  - `pages/session/followup-queue.test.ts` was moved with `git mv` to `features/followup/followup-queue.test.ts` and rewritten for the drainer. Its two limit cases (accepts up to 20, refuses the 21st) now live in `followup-model.test.ts`.
  - The drainer and store tests import `followup-queue.tsx`, which pulls in `@solidjs/router`; its server build cannot load under bun test, so they mock the router as `submit.test.ts` does.
  - The drainer core (`createFollowupDrain`) has no Solid in it, so the tests drive it directly with fake Web Locks, a shared slow storage and a fake BroadcastChannel.

Evidence (macOS, 2026-11-03; `packages/app` cwd, one file per command, `--timeout 30000`). `src/features/followup/followup-model.test.ts` 28 (new; I7 included), `src/features/followup/followup-store.test.ts` 6 (new), `src/features/followup/followup-queue.test.ts` 7 (moved and rewritten), `src/utils/persist.test.ts` 15, `src/components/prompt-input/submit.test.ts` 71, `src/components/settings-v2/settings-registry.test.ts` 15 pass. Three regressions were checked by breaking their fix for one run, and each failed: the `replied` check in `sendFollowupDraft`, resending a claim with its own ID, and the eviction skip. The concurrent-windows case also shows that the same two writes without the lock lose one. The app typecheck passes. No HTTP schema, route or engine config changed, so no generator was run. Not run here: the timeline benchmark (`packages/app/AGENTS.md`), which is the owner's to run; no Playwright spec covers the queue yet.

## 2026-11-03 WP37 (app Tasks pane and chips)

The Tasks pane shows the limits line (`limitsLine` in the new `task-limits.ts`, nothing without `limits`), model · effort, a Fork badge, a JSON chip, the queued reason, "Needs your permission · 3m" with Answer, a worktree row with Review, Merge and Discard, an "Interrupted when Vector restarted" section with Resume and Resume all, "Move to background" on cards and beside chat chips, structured JSON and messages in Details, "Continue with this message" or "Ask Vector to continue it" for a finished subagent, a Background commands section, and workflow runs as one card with stage bars. Where the code departs from or adds to the contract:

- **Where the requests live.** The request helpers (`promoteRequest`, `resumeRequest`, `continueRequest`, `changesRequest`, `mergeRequest`, `discardRequest`, `resumeRunRequest`, `ownerOnlyRefusal`) are pure exports of `background-tasks.ts`, so the pane, the chip and the tests share them. `background-tasks-state.test.ts` tests them (its layout cases are unchanged); `background-tasks-state.ts` itself did not change.
- **Files outside the list.** `stop-helpers.ts` (WP35) skips workflow agents in `helperTargets`, with one new `stop-helpers.test.ts` case: a run's agents join the board while its stages are open, and without the skip "Stop including helpers (N)" would count them. The run row stands for them, and route 1 or `task.stop` on the run stops them.
- **Workflow runs.** A run and its agents share the key `subagent:workflow:<runID>`; the run row (`workflow.role === "run"`, which has no `groupID`) is keyed by its own ID. The run's title names the card. Agents load with `task.list({sessionID: runID})` only while a stage is open, refresh with the board, and leave the board when every stage closes. `stoppableAgents` and `taskSpend` drop agents whose run row is listed, and so do the live, running and waiting counts (the header badge), so opening a run never changes them.
- **Workflow Resume.** The contract shows it "when `controls.resume`", but `TaskManager.offers` returns no controls for workflow records, so it would never show. It shows when the engine has `workflows` and the run is stopped or failed, or when `controls.resume` is set. Route 12 is sent with `retryFailed: true`; a 403 says "Only the server owner can resume a workflow run." "Open results" shows the run's report from inspect (its `output`) in the card.
- **Resume.** Resume and Resume all send route 3 in chunks of 32 with every interrupted ID that has `controls.resume`, and only with `taskResume`; without it the section says "Update this engine to resume them from here." The engine resumes a request all or none (WP14), so a failed chunk leaves its IDs unresumed while earlier chunks stand, and no later chunk is sent. A partial result toasts "Resumed X of N helpers". "· up to N at once" names `limits.maxRunning` and is left out when the board has no `limits` (a guest or non-owner). The section lists interrupted helpers from every card, whatever the trash hid; their cards stay under Finished.
- **Move to background.** The card's button moves every promotable agent of the card, the chip's the phase's; there is no per-row button. Promotable means live, `controls.background`, and `parentSessionID` equal to the root. With `taskPromote` it posts route 2 with those IDs; without it, the old route for a single subagent, and a batch (several agents, or one with a `batchID`) shows "This engine can move a single subagent, not a batch" in place of the button. A request that moves nothing toasts "Nothing was moved to the background". The I.1 toast for a steer while a foreground subagent holds the turn is not built (its file, `prompt-input/submit.ts`, is not in WP37's list); `BackgroundTasks.promote` is ready for it.
- **Continue.** "Ask Vector to continue it" is also what an engine without `controls.resume` (or without `taskResume`) gets, and the only path for sessions from before the board. It appends `Continue "<title>" (task <id>) with its task_id, using @<subagent type>: <draft>` to the root composer, the title JSON-quoted and the agent part covering `@<type>`. Workflow agents, runs and desktop workspaces are not offered Continue.
- **Worktree row.** Review calls route 4 with `diff=true` and shows the files and the patch as text. Merge and Discard re-arm after 4 s (`createConfirm` in the pane). A 403 shows the owner-only sentence. A merge result with conflicts lists them with "Ask Vector to resolve", which appends the paths (as JSON) and asks for `task_manage merge`. The display copy never holds `directory`: `readIsolation` drops it, also from an owner's board row.
- **Rows.** The Fork badge, the JSON chip, the queued line and the waiting line sit in the row's details line, because the docked name column is about 77 px wide. "default" effort is left out of "short · effort". A board row's queued reason and waiting replace what the part said, so a row that started running clears them.
- **Background commands.** Listed only when the engine has `backgroundShells` (the board then asks for `shells=true`). Stop sends `task.stop` with the `shl_` ID. "Open output" uses the desktop's `openPath` and shows only when the owner's row carries `outputPath`; the web app has no Open output.
- **Strings.** Every new string is in `en.ts` under `backgroundTasks.*`; the pure labels take the language's translator and default to English. The pane's existing inline English is unchanged, and other locales fall back to English.
- **`board.background`.** `task.background ?? true`, as I.3 says: foreground agents no longer count as background for Stop all or the helpers count.

After it the helpers left are counted from `task.list {active, shells}` with the CLI's rule (session's own active subagents that are not foreground, plus running commands; desktop workspaces and workflow agents left out), and the toast names the configured key of `session.interrupt.all`, or `/stop-all` when it is unbound. With `interrupt_scope: "all"`, Esc Esc runs "Stop including helpers" through a new optional `Prompt` prop, `onStopAll`, which the session passes for main sessions only; without it Esc Esc stays main-only. Route 1 failing shows its error and tries nothing else. The palette command is enabled only in main sessions, idle or not.
- **"Stop background subagents".** It lists the session's active tasks and sends `backgroundStopRequests`: native subagents that are queued, running or waiting, with `background: true`, and `controls.stop` not false, in chunks of 200. Desktop workspaces (`workspace:`/`swarm:` IDs, other sources) and a workflow run's agents (they stop with their run) are left out. A task without `Task.Info.background` (an engine before WP34) counts as background only when the transcript launched its session in the background. The fallback is the one above: aborting each known background session.

Tasks with a pending interruption come first under "Interrupted by a restart". The footer shows the limits line when the list carries `limits`. "Resume all interrupted (N)" needs `taskResume` and counts resumable rows that are neither resumed nor dismissed; "Stop including helpers" is a leading action in main sessions only. "Move to background" is route 2 with the task ID (`taskPromote`, `controls.background`). ## 2026-11-04 Phase 8 review

Ten findings were raised; all ten were confirmed against the code. Three pairs named the same defect twice (the shell and command claim IDs, the "Stop them too" target and the drainer's busy marking), so seven defects were fixed. Where the code departs from or adds to the contract:

- **Ctrl+C in `vector run` without `--attach`.** The first press stops the main turn and every helper, then exits 130, with or without `--stop-helpers`. Route 1 is used with `helpers: true`, or the abort plus `task.stop {all}` on an older engine. With `--attach` the two-press flow is still as I.6 describes it. The reason: a terminal in cooked mode sends Ctrl+C to the whole foreground process group. Without `--attach` the engine runs in the CLI's process, so its stdio MCP servers (the MCP SDK's stdio transport spawns them in the same group) and its LSP servers get the signal, and usually exit. On Windows the background commands get it too, because they are spawned with `detached: false`. Helpers kept running would then work without their MCP tools and LSP, and "N helpers are still running" could count commands that had just died. To keep the first-press flow there, those children would need their own process group, which means an MCP transport of Vector's own and group kills on dispose for LSP. That is more than a review fix. The `--stop-helpers` help text did not change: "Without --attach, exiting ends them anyway" still holds, so the help snapshot is unchanged. For WP40a: the terminal docs should say that without `--attach`, Ctrl+C stops the run and its helpers at once.
- **The exit path disposes the in-process engine.** Before `process.exit(130)`, a run without `--attach` disposes its instance, waiting at most 5 s like the stop before it. The background commands' process trees therefore end with it (5.H), as the help says. A press while that runs exits at once.
- **Steer toast (I.1).** This toast is now built, which replaces the "not built" notes under WP35, WP37 and WP38. After a steer the engine accepted, "Vector reads your message after the running subagents finish this step" is shown when the root's board holds live foreground subagents. A foreground subagent here is one not in the background, live, and either a native subagent whose parent is the root or a transcript agent with no task row. "Move to background" appears only for the ones `promotable` returns (`controls.background`), and not when an older engine cannot move a batch. It goes through `BackgroundTasks.promote`. Clicked after another conversation was opened, it moves nothing. When none can be moved, the toast shows without the button. `prompt-input.tsx` passes the hold to `createPromptSubmit` as `foreground`.
- **"Stop them too".** The session, its client, the capability flags and the helper targets are read when Stop runs, and the toast's action stops exactly those. Stop had already paused that session's queue and cleared its todos, so the action does neither again, and never touches the conversation that is open when it is clicked.
- **Claims for shell input and slash commands.** `sendFollowupDraft` now passes the claim's `messageID` to `session.shell` and `session.command`, as it already did for prompts. A reload while a queued `/review` or shell command runs then drops it instead of running it twice.
- **The drainer's busy marking.** The drainer sends through the new `sendQueued`, which reads `session_working` first. A send from an idle session still marks it busy. Send now and Retry while the session works steer the turn instead, and they neither mark it busy nor, when they fail, idle.
- **"Open output".** The desktop's `open-path` opens only existing folders, so the button opens the folder that holds the log, and the full path stays in the button's tooltip. It shows only for a local server (`server.isLocal()`, as the session header does). A failure shows the toast "Could not open the output folder" instead of failing silently. No reveal-file IPC was added.
- **`vector tasks` confirmation.** Ctrl+C or a closed input at "(y/N)" counts as no, so `merge` and `discard` exit 1 with "Nothing was merged/discarded…". Before, the question never settled and the process exited 0. `confirmAtTerminal` is exported, with injectable streams, for its test.
- No HTTP schema or route changed (the SDK already sends `messageID` for shell and command), so no generator was run. Codium, Hands and @vector files were not touched.

Evidence (macOS, 2026-11-04; package cwd, one file per command, `--timeout 30000`). `packages/app`: `src/features/followup/followup-queue.test.ts` 9, `src/components/prompt-input/submit.test.ts` 73, `src/features/background-tasks/background-tasks-state.test.ts` 18. `packages/engine`: `test/cli/run/interrupt.test.ts` 12, `test/cli/tasks.test.ts` 14. All pass. Two of the new cases were also run against the old code, and both failed there: the claim-ID case (with the two `messageID` lines removed) and the stop-target case (with the action back on `abort("all")`). The app and engine typechecks pass. Left to CI: an end-to-end Ctrl+C on a real `vector run`, which is covered only through `watchInterrupts` with a fake signal source.

## 2026-11-04 WP40a (website docs, release notes and CI wiring)

The website documents the build on the pages 5.J lists, a new Workflows page sits under Agents after Subagents, `docs-content.ts` carries labelled rows, the FAQ answer and a subagent spend row, `release-199113.md` has a "Multi-agent parity" section, and CI's website step runs the five files. Where the work departs from or adds to the contract:

- **Shipped paragraphs that match the test's list.** Six paragraphs that shipped before 1.99.113 match the contract's regex for older features: the worktrees of agent workspaces (`subagents`' workspace note, two `parallel-agents` paragraphs, `permissions`' repository-trust paragraph) and the session `--fork` flag (two `terminal-agent` paragraphs). Labelling them would call shipped behaviour new. `release-113-multiagent-docs.test.ts` lists them in `SHIPPED` with the reason, skips them, and fails if any of them is gone or appears twice. The workspace note keeps its text and a labelled note follows it, as 5.J asks for a changed statement.
- **"Vector sets no cap".** The test forbids the phrase in `docs-content.ts`, while 5.J keeps shipped statements. The "How many" row and the FAQ answer keep the old fact in the past tense for 1.99.112 and earlier ("Up to desktop 1.99.112, Vector set no cap…"), followed by the labelled limits.
- **Workflow examples.** `docs-config-reference.test.ts` decodes every `<pre><code>` sample whose text starts with `{` as a `vector.json`, so the three workflow examples use `<pre data-sample="workflow">`. The new test parses them, checks they equal the examples in `orchestration/guide.txt` and that `WorkflowSpec.parse` accepts them. The page's `vector.json` samples keep the plain markup and are decoded strictly.
- **Which blocks are checked.** As in `release-113-docs.test.ts`, the label check reads `<p>` and `<dd>` blocks; a command sample (`<p class="doc-cmd">`) is not a paragraph and is skipped. Every paragraph of the new Workflows page carries the label. Rows rendered from `docs-content.ts` are checked by their own tests (the four `subagentKinds` rows, the FAQ answer, the explore role and the spend row).
- **The engine's numbers.** `packages/web` depends on core but not on the engine, so the test reads `TaskLimits.GUEST`, `MAX_RUNNING`, `MAX_PER_REQUEST` and `BUSY` (`task/limits.ts`), `QUIET` and `HOLD` (`task/note-window.ts`), `NOTE_EVERY` (`task/attention.ts`), the background-command `LIMIT`, `MAX_TIMEOUT` and `LOG_LIMIT` (`task/shells.ts`) and the `max_kept` default (`task/isolation.ts`) from their source text, and checks that `TaskLimits.DEFAULTS` is built from `ConfigV1.SubagentDefaults`, which it imports. `WorkflowLimits` and `WorkflowSpec.BYTES` are imported from core.
- **More than the list.** Labelled sentences also went into the `subagentKinds` "While they run" row (background by default), the desktop composer section (Steer, the queue dock, the Stop chevron, `/workflow`), the Workflows page's CLI section, and the release notes intro line.
- **In-app copy.** Settings → Agents (`settings-v2/subagents.tsx`) no longer says subagents are for "big tasks" in the page subtitle, the Subagents card and the General subagents switch: they now say "independent, substantial parts of a task", which is true on engines before and after 1.99.113. The facts shown for older engines are unchanged. No app test pins these strings; the app typecheck and `settings-registry.test.ts` pass.
- **"What blocks the publish".** Besides every test path in section 6 (the test checks each one is named), the list names the files earlier entries moved or added: WP27's `lsp/isolation.test.ts` and `mcp/isolation.test.ts`, WP30's runtime files, WP32's `cli/run-workflow.test.ts`, the Phase 7 review's `cli/run/background-wait.test.ts`, WP38's `session-interrupted-dock.test.ts` and `workflow-part.test.ts`, and WP39's `test/cli/cmd/tui/sync.test.tsx`. WP40b's specs are named with the directory they will live in.
- **CI.** "Run website release copy tests" runs the five files in one `bun test` call, on Linux only, as before.
- Codium, Hands and @vector files were not touched. No route, schema, config or keybind changed, so no generator was run.

Evidence (macOS, 2026-11-04, added by the Phase 9 review, which found this entry had none; the runs are on the tree with that review's fixes, `packages/web` cwd, one file per command, `--timeout 30000`). `test/release-113-multiagent-docs.test.ts` 10 (nine from WP40a and the review's new copy check), `test/docs-settings.test.ts` 5 and `test/release-113-docs.test.ts` 8 pass; so do `docs-config-paths` 2, `docs-config-reference` 4 and `external-agents-rules` 3, which read the same pages. `bun typecheck` (`astro check`) reports 0 errors and 0 warnings over 202 files. `test/deployed-schemas.test.ts` was not run here; CI runs it with the other four.

## 2026-11-04 WP40b (Playwright e2e specs)

The nine specs are in `packages/app/e2e/regression/`: `followup-steer-queue`, `stop-helpers`, `background-tasks-controls`, `background-tasks-details`, `subagent-limits`, `isolated-subagent`, `workflow-progress`, `workflow-permission` and `background-commands`. `MockServerConfig` gains `capabilities`, `taskList` and `workflow`, and the mock records config PATCHes and task route posts. None of the specs has run on this Mac (section 6: CI runs them with `bun --cwd packages/app test:e2e:local`). Where the work departs from or adds to the contract:

- **Files outside the list.**
  - `e2e/utils/multiagent.ts` (new) holds the fixture the nine specs share: one root session, child sessions, `task()` for Background tasks rows, the composer's model, the page's storage, and `setup()`, which returns the capture, `open()` and `send()`.
  - `e2e/tsconfig.json` lists the nine specs, so `bun run typecheck:e2e` checks them.
- **The mock, beyond the three named keys.**
  - `taskRoute` answers the task routes other than the board: route 1, the old background route, stop, wait, resume, route 2, inspect, changes, message, merge and discard.
  - `globalConfig`: when it is set, `GET /global/config` returns it and a PATCH deep-merges into it, so a saved setting reads back.
  - `sessionStatus` may be a function, and `reply(status, body, contentType)` builds a 403, a 404 or an HTML answer.
  - `mockVectorServer` now returns the capture: config PATCH bodies, task route posts, workflow posts, and posts to `/session/:id/…` (abort, prompt_async, permission replies).
  - The same-origin matcher also takes `/experimental/…` and those `/session/:id/…` paths. Every route a spec leaves unconfigured answers as before (`{}` from a separate backend, the dev server's fallback otherwise). A probe with fake routes showed both modes unchanged.
- **Events.** The specs use the existing live stream (`installSseTransport`) rather than the mock's polled `events`. A status change, a spend stop or a child's streamed part therefore arrives on an open stream, with no reconnect in between.
- **"A 404 falls back" (stop-helpers).** Here the engine answers 404 for `/experimental/capabilities`, which I30 reads as every flag off. "Stop including helpers" then aborts the main turn and each helper's session (I.2 step 3), and posts nothing to the task routes. The helper is known from the transcript. A route 1 failure itself still throws with no fallback, as WP35 recorded.
- **"Open output" (background-commands).** The button needs the desktop's `openPath` and a local server (Phase 8 review). The web build that Playwright drives has neither, so the spec checks that the web build shows no Open output and never shows the log's path. The desktop button itself is not covered by any Playwright spec, only `outputFolder` by its unit test.
- **The board and late flags.** As written, the board read `backgroundShells` only when it refreshed, and a flag that arrived late did not trigger a refresh, so the background-commands spec sent one `session.updated` after the page opened. The Phase 9 review fixed the board (a change of the flag refreshes it) and removed that event from the spec, which now checks that the section appears with no conversation event. `setup().open()` still waits for the capabilities answer, so every control the specs use reads the flags.
- **"The chip in the timeline" (workflow-progress).** This is the `workflow` tool part (session-ui `WorkflowPart`: "Workflow", the run's title, "started"). The app's task chips are drawn only for `task` parts.
- **"Streams its parts live" (isolated-subagent).** The parent's dock shows the isolated child's permission after `session.created` and `permission.asked` arrive at its home location. The child is not in the `/session` list until it is announced. Its parts stream on its own page through `message.part.delta` and `message.part.updated`, also sent at the home location.
- **"Navigating Home" (followup-steer-queue).** The spec clicks the Home nav button, so the session's page unmounts while the window stays open, which is the drain I.1 describes. A page reload is not covered. The spend-stop case also checks I7's Resume: the queue sends only after the user resumes it. The failed-drain case opens the session from the toast and finds the item marked "Not sent" with Retry.
- **"Non-owners see no card" (subagent-limits).** The guest is the reader whom the owner-only `GET /experimental/workflow/saved` refuses with 403 (WP38's probe). The spec also checks that the Workflows card is absent.
- Codium, Hands and @vector files were not touched. No app source, route, schema or config changed, so no generator was run.

Evidence (macOS, 2026-11-04). `packages/app`: `bun run typecheck:e2e` passes and checks the nine specs and both utils files; a deliberate type error in `multiagent.ts` failed it, then was removed. `bun run typecheck` passes. A Bun script (in the scratchpad, not committed) drove `mockVectorServer` with fake routes. It showed every new route answered by its handler, the capture recording each post, and unconfigured routes answering as before in both modes. Not run here: the specs themselves (Playwright, CI only).

## 2026-11-04 Phase 9 review (WP40a, WP40b)

Nine findings, all confirmed against the code and fixed. Where the fixes choose between options or go beyond the finding:

- **Docs that said more than the engine does.** `subagents.astro` and the `subagentKinds` "When Vector uses them" row now say Light requests, and requests where the user said "no subagents", start only the subagents the user names with `@` (`TaskSize.withheld` keeps `task` when `agents > 0`). The restart section says a helper that never started cannot be resumed, Resume all included, and the agent launches it again with `task` (D.3 step 6). The worktree section says nothing outside linked folders reaches the user's files before a merge, and a labelled caution says a linked folder is the checkout's own folder, outside Review, Merge, Discard and checkpoints. Tokenomics' learned-defaults subagent split moved under "Learned defaults". The two shipped FAQ answers about big tasks and the subagents card keep their text and gain a labelled sentence (5.J).
- **The docs test.** "While they run" joined the labelled `subagentKinds` rows, the two FAQ answers are checked for the label, and a new test pins the corrected sentences and the moved paragraph's section. No expectation was loosened.
- **Background commands and a settings reload (option chosen: the reload goes ahead).** `disposeIdleInstances` no longer counts a running `shell` job as work, so a global reload stops background commands as 5.H and the shells.ts `disposed` note describe, instead of holding a folder's old settings, a lowered `budget.subagents.usd` among them, for as long as a dev server runs. A command that exited within the report window still holds the reload until its note is out, as a finished background subagent does. Keeping the deferral would have needed the docs to say that settings do not apply while a command runs, which leaves the spend ceiling unenforceable for that time. `global-lifecycle.test.ts` gains two cases (a running command does not hold the reload and is gone after it; a background subagent beside one still does); both fail on the old line.
- **Settings → Agents asks first about background commands.** `runningWork` reads session statuses, and a conversation whose only work is a background command is idle. With `backgroundShells`, the page now asks `task.list` (`shells=true`, `active=true`, `limit=1`) of the root sessions the app holds a status for, the 20 most recently updated (`commandRoots`, `COMMAND_ROOTS`), with a 3 s timeout that counts as none, and adds the running ones to the dialog ("1 background command is running."). The engine has no route that lists commands across conversations, so a conversation the app never saw work, or one past the 20, is not counted; adding such a route is more than a review fix. Flipping a switch or saving now waits for that answer before it applies or asks. Other Settings pages, such as provider keys, still save without asking; the release notes say so under "Decide what the build leaves open".
- **The board and late flags (WP40b).** `createBackgroundTasks` refreshes the board when `capabilities().backgroundShells` changes. `test-browser/background-tasks.test.ts` gains a case where the flags answer after the first load and no event follows; it fails without the new effect. `background-commands.spec.ts` no longer sends `session.updated`.
- **Release notes.** "Decide what the build leaves open" gains two items: a settings save stops background commands, and linked folders sit outside review and checkpoints.
- Codium, Hands and @vector files were not touched. No route, schema or config changed, so no generator was run.

Evidence (macOS, 2026-11-04; package cwd, one file per command, `--timeout 30000`). Engine: `test/server/global-lifecycle.test.ts` 7 pass; with the old `working` line both new cases failed. App: `test-browser/background-tasks.test.ts` 9 (`--conditions=browser --preload ./happydom.ts`) and `src/components/settings-v2/subagent-switches.test.ts` 18 (`--preload ./happydom.ts`) pass; with the new effect disabled the late-flags case failed. Web: as in WP40a's evidence line above. Typechecks: engine `bun typecheck`, app `bun typecheck` and `bun run typecheck:e2e`, and web `astro check` pass. Not run here: the Playwright spec (CI only).

   - run 41959395503 on `f551fd76`, job `127021881188`, step 03:11:19-03:41:26.

   Diagnosis:
   - Baseline: in run 41925753700, attempt 1's job `126909620451` ran the step in 19:17 (22:53:15-23:12:32). Its attempt-2 copy, `126931388197`, has the same times and is the log read. Bun reported 629 pass, 28 skip and 0 fail of 657 tests across 45 files (1,155.87 s).

   - Summed test durations from the logs: 1,150.0 s for the baseline's 45 files, against 1,794.0 s and 1,799.6 s for the 38 files that each cut run reached.
   - Run 41959395503 against the baseline:
     - isolation 684.6 s for 79 cases, against 434.2 s for 83 (1.58x);
     - session/task-size 385.5 s against 262.8 s (1.47x);
     - session/prompt 279.0 s against 135.0 s (2.07x);

## 2026-11-04 Final end-to-end review

Fifteen findings, each confirmed again against the code before it was fixed; none was rejected. The parts the review's skeptic had already rejected (the default-instance capability read, R10's shell caveat, the tokenomics claim and the FAQ answer about limits) stay as they were. Where the fixes choose between options or go beyond the finding:

- **Notes that start a turn after "thanks" (A.2).** The Chat rule now counts every note that starts a turn: a non-cancelled, non-quiet `taskNotification`, a non-quiet `taskMessage` (a `task_manage report` delivered with a turn: a batch is quiet unless one of its notes wakes, and every note that wakes is counted), and a non-quiet `shellNotification` whose state is not `stopped` or `interrupted`. A.2 names only `taskNotification`. A background command's launching request is found from the shell tool's part (`metadata.background` and its `shl_` `shellID`), as a child's is from the task call, and one out of view counts as Standard. `notified` still counts completion notes alone, so the WP8 entry's "one predicate" no longer holds: a report or an exit note never lets a Light request resume a child through `namedOnly`.
- **Light and background commands (A.1).** The offer's `children` is also true while the conversation's main agent has a running background command, read from `BackgroundJob.list()` only when the request is offered Light and has no snapshot. The command's result and the over-limit refusal both say "stop it with task_manage stop"; A.1's table has no term for commands. `notified` is not used for this, so Light still launches and resumes nothing.
- **Settings save while work runs (B.11), option (b).** The engine waits for a folder's turns and subagents before it reloads that folder (global-lifecycle.ts, kept by the Phase 9 review), so `stopWorkDescription` no longer says the save stops them or that the setting applies from the next message. It says that work keeps the current setting until it ends, and that background commands stop. The General subagents row and the subagents docs page say the same. Stopping helpers before the save (option a) was not chosen: it would stop conversations in folders the app does not show, and Vector's own helpers, which have no stop route. `subagent-switches.test.ts`'s expected texts changed with the wording.
- **Stop including helpers and terminals (D.1, the MAIN entry's hand-off to WP33).** `TaskManager.interrupt` with helpers now closes the terminals that subagent sessions under the conversation opened, finished helpers' included, at the conversation's home and in each helper's work folder, and waits for their programs (`TaskIsolation.closeTerminals`, the step a checkout's save already used). The main agent's own terminals stay open, as the user's terminal panel shows them, and so do other conversations'. The owner question about terminals for subagents (MAIN entry) stays open; this only makes the stop stop them.
- **Isolation disk check (F.4).** `TaskIsolation.start` checks the free space again under the folder's creation lock, against the same estimate, and fails with the `disk` error. Launches that passed `check` together are no longer all admitted against one reading.
- **Background command notes across a restart (5.H).** When a command ends on its own or is stopped, its row records how (`metadata.ended`: state, bytes, exit code and flags, `failed` cut to 500 characters). `TaskNotify` marks the row reported as it writes the note's message, or when it drops the note with its launch. Restart recovery takes the ended rows of an engine that is gone whose note was never marked (`BackgroundJobStore.undelivered`, claimed with `delivered`), rebuilds the note from the row and the end of the log, and posts it quietly; a note whose launching message is gone is dropped, as the window drops it. A note whose prompt fails to write after it was marked is still lost, as before. The core store gains `ended`, `delivered` and `undelivered`.
- **Capabilities after a settings save (I30).** Server sync reads the flags again after `global.disposed`, after `server.instance.disposed` (a folder that waited for its work reloads later) and when a config update succeeds, besides a reconnect.
- **Settings → Agents for a guest (5.B.7).** The How many fact shows the lower of the configured limits and 4, 8 and $5 when the owner-only probe says the reader is a guest. The guest Playwright case expects those numbers.
- **How many wording (5.B.9).** The fact and the docs' How many row say only the at-once limit makes the main agent's launches wait, and launches past the per-request count are refused.
- **The Tasks pane.** A workflow run's list asks for 200 agents (the engine's most), keeps `truncated`, and a stage with started agents and none in a cut list says the list shows only the active and most recent ones. The JSON chip's text names its state ("JSON valid", "JSON invalid", "JSON unchecked"), and a stage's count adds "N failed" and "N skipped" in words; `subagent-model.test.ts` and the details Playwright case expect the new chip text. A cut review patch names `git diff ...<branch>` for a worktree, or says Merge applies every change for a copy, instead of "Open the worktree".
- **Copy.** The desktop page names the dock's own controls (Steer, Delete, Not sent with Retry, Resume) and the docs test pins them to the dock's strings. Settings → General adds "Queued messages send while a Vector window is open, even with their session closed." when the browser can share the queue (R6), and the release note's summary says so too. The release notes' fork step checks Cache Tokens (read/write) in the fork's Context tab.
- Codium, Hands and @vector files were not touched. No route, schema or config changed, so no generator was run.

Evidence (macOS, 2026-11-04; package cwd, one file per command, `--timeout 30000`). Engine: `test/session/task-size-history.test.ts` 34, `test/session/task-size.test.ts` 53, `test/task/shells.test.ts` 11, `test/task/isolation.test.ts` 90, `test/task/notify.test.ts` 16, `test/task/notify-admission.test.ts` 4, `test/task/recovery.test.ts` 7, `test/task/workflow-notify.test.ts` 2, `test/tool/task-manage.test.ts` 50 and `test/server/session-stop.test.ts` 4 pass. `test/task/isolation-terminal.test.ts`: the new stop case passes; the existing save case fails here on macOS before any terminal code runs (`/var` against `/private/var` in the checkout path), unchanged by this work and covered by Linux CI. With the old code the new sizing, Light, terminal and disk cases each failed. Core: `test/background-job-store.test.ts` 13 pass. App: `subagent-switches.test.ts` 18, `subagent-limits.test.ts` 11, `subagent-model.test.ts` 78, `engine-capabilities.test.ts` 12 and `followup-model.test.ts` 29 (`--preload ./happydom.ts`), and `test-browser/background-tasks.test.ts` 10 (`--conditions=browser --preload ./happydom.ts`) pass. Web: `release-113-multiagent-docs.test.ts` 11, `docs-settings` 5, `release-copy` 11, `release-111-docs` 11, `docs-config-paths` 2, `brain-docs` 12 and `repository-traces` 5 pass. Typechecks: core, engine and app `bun typecheck`, app `bun run typecheck:e2e`, and web `astro check` pass. Not run here: the Playwright specs (CI only).

- upstream-free reads tracked files in batches of 256.
- No deadline changed, and no assertion was removed.
